Multiparameter Quantum Supergroups, Deformations and Specializations Thanks: 2020 MSC: 17B37, 17B62 — Keywords: Quantum Groups, Quantum Enveloping Algebras.
Gastón Andrés García ♭ {}^{\flat}\, , Fabio Gavarini ♯ \,{}^{\sharp}\, , Margherita Paolini ♮

Abstract
. In this paper we introduce a multiparameter version of the quantum universal enveloping superalgebras introduced by Yamane in [ Ya1 ] . For these objects we consider: — (1) their deformations by twist and by 2–cocycle (both of “toral type”); in particular, we prove that this family is stable under both types of deformations; — (2) their semiclassical limits, which are multiparameter Lie superbialgebras; — (3) the deformations by twist and by 2–cocycle (of “toral type”) of these multiparameter Lie superbialgebras: in particular, we prove that this family is stable under these deformations, and that “quantization commutes with deformation”. To the memory of Pierre Cartier, with our deepest admiration.



Communications in Contemporary Mathematics
(to appear)

1.
Introduction

In the last forty years, quantum groups
— mainly in the form of quantized universal enveloping algebras, hereafter shortened into QUEA’s —
have been thoroughly studied, both in their
formal
version (à la Drinfeld, say),
and in their
polynomial
formulation (following Jimbo and Lusztig).
The semiclassical limit underlying them is given by Lie bialgebras (infinitesimally)
and Poisson-Lie groups (globally): conversely, the “quantization problem” for
a Lie bialgebra consists in the quest for some QUEA whose semiclassical
limit yields back the given Lie bialgebra.

Quite soon,
multiparameter
quantum groups have been introduced too, including those coming from the FRT-construction, as well as multiparameter QUEA’s — or MpQUEA’s, in short.
A standard method for that uses the technique of
deformation
of Hopf algebras,
which comes in two versions: deformation by twist — where only the comultiplication
and the antipode are modified — and deformation by 2–cocycle
— where one changes only the multiplication and the antipode.
The twist deformation was applied by Reshetikhin to Drinfeld’s formal QUEA’s

U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
, thus finding some multiparameter QUEA’s in which the new,
“additional” parameters show up to describe the new coalgebra structure.
The second method instead was applied by several people, all working with Jimbo & Lusztig’s
polynomial QUEA
U_{q}(\hskip 0.8pt\mathfrak{g})
— in a suitable “quantum double version” —
thus providing some other multiparameter QUEA’s whose parameters enter
in the description of the (new) algebra structure. A common,
unified

point of view on this matter was presented in
[
GG3
]
(see also
[
GG1
]
):
the key message there is that, although the two families of MpQUEA’s arising
from deformations by twist or by 2–cocycle seem to be entirely disconnected,
they form instead one and the same family. In addition, a second key result from
[
GG3
]
is that
deformations of either kind of any MpQUEA still provide some new MpQUEA’s,

in this same family.

Looking at MpQUEA’s, it was quickly realized that their many parameters could always be
re-arranged so that only one of them kept the role of “quantization parameter”,
while all the other took instead a “geometrical” meaning.
Namely, when one specializes the single “quantum parameter”
and thus gets the semiclassical limit, the remaining parameters enter in the description
of the Poisson structure (in the form of a Lie cobracket) that is induced
onto the underlying Lie algebra. In this way, specialization
of MpQUEA’s yields
multiparameter

structures onto the semiclassical limit, so one ends up with multiparameter
Lie bialgebras (or MpLbA’s, in short). On the other hand, one can also introduce
this family of MpLbA’s via an independent construction, and prove that this family
is stable under deformations (in the sense of Lie bialgebra theory) by twist or by
2–cocycle; see
[
GG3
]
for details.

The present paper extends the previous world-building process to the setup
of quantum
super
groups: namely, we introduce
formal multiparameter quantized enveloping
super
algebras
(in short
formal MpQUESA
’s, or just
FoMpQUESA
’s) and we study their deformations, by twist and by 2–cocycle.

Both Lie superalgebras and their quantizations, the quantized universal enveloping superalgebras (QUESA),
can be regarded as Lie algebras and quantum universal enveloping algebras in the category of super vector spaces.
As noted by Majid
[
Mj
]
, both classical and super objects are related via the
process of the so-called
bosonization
which yields a functor from one category to the
other. Despite this strong connection, Lie superalgebras and QUESA’s are interesting on their own,
mainly because of their wide range of applications.
Simple Lie superalgebras over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero
were classified by Kac in
[
Ka2
]
: like for simple Lie algebras, this classification is given in terms of Cartan matrices and (generalized) Dynkin diagrams. Nevertheless, a single Lie superbialgebra may have non-isomorphic Dynkin diagrams.
As the study of MpQUEA’s (and their semiclassical limit) was mostly bounded
to those whose semiclassical limits were Lie algebras of contragredient
type with symmetrizable Cartan matrices, we focus onto MpQUESA’s whose semiclassical limit are simple contragredient Lie superalgebras of type
A
–
G

(w.r.t. the classification in
[
Ka2
]
). These QUESA’s were first introduced by
Yamane, in
[
Ya1
]
, who defined formal QUESA’s of type
A
–
G
: for them, relations are not only commutation relations and super quantum Serre relations, as one also needs to add higher order relations.
Likewise, the MpLSbA’s that we construct are, as Lie superalgebras, the contragredient ones of type
A
–
G
, again.

Specifically, we introduce multiparameter versions of Yamane’s formal QUESA’s (in short, FoMpQUESA’s), see Definition
4.2.2
, by mimicking the strategy we developed in
[
GG3
]
for the non-super case. The very definition is based upon the connection between
the multiparameters and the action of a fixed commutative subsuperalgebra:
this is encoded in the notion of
realization
of a multiparameter matrix
P
,
much like for Kac-Moody algebras. This allows us to relate the quantum objects
with their semiclassical limit, and also multiparameter objects with Yamane’s standard ones:
the latter step goes through deformation(s).
To do this, we consider deformations by toral twists of Yamane’s uniparameter QUESA’s,
just like Reshetikhin did with Drinfeld’s uniparameter QUEA’s, thus finding suitable new, deformed QUESA’s that are now “multiparameter” ones, as they depend on the many parameters coming from the twist:
these parameters, though, only show up in the description of the coalgebra structure,
while the algebra structure is independent of them.
As a consequence, one may endow these deformed QUESA’s with a Hopf superalgebra
structure.
The second, key idea is to perform on these MpQUESA’s a careful “change of presentation”,
modifying the original generators (from Yamane’s presentation) into new ones: the outcome is a
new presentation where the parameters appear in the defining relations, whereas the description
of the coalgebra structure now is entirely parameter-free. Finally, we take this last presentation as
“the” pattern for our definition of MpQUESA, given again by generators and relations,
mimicking the last presentation of the deformed MpQUEA’s that we found after the “change-the-generators” step.
The arguments used in this analysis apply again to study, more in general, the deformations by twist of
any

one of our MpQUESA’s: thus we find that any such deformation is yet another MpQUESA — this is proved in Theorem

5.1.3
. Moreover, we prove in Proposition
4.2.8
that any FoMpQUESA
can be obtained from a Yamane’s uniparameter QUESA by a proper choice of the twist. Then we draw our attention to an extension of the dual notion of twist,
polar–
2
–cocycles
, and show in Theorem
5.2.15
that actually the family of FoMpQUESA is again stable by deformations of the superalgebra structure by a polar–
2
–cocycle. In conclusion, any FoMpQUESA can be obtained from Yamane’s (uniparameter) QUESA either by a twist deformation
or by a polar–
2
–cocycle deformation — the former fact is fairly natural, but the latter instead is quite surprising indeed. So, for every FoMpQUESA one can decide
to focus the dependence on the discrete multiparameters either on the supercoalgebra structure or on the superalgebra structure: in fact, we chose the second option. As in the non-super setting
[
GG3
]
, everything is encoded in the notion of “realization” of multiparameters.

Quite naturally, the same strategy used in the quantum setup can be adopted as well in the semiclassical framework, even with some simplifications. Therefore, we develop our construction of MpLSbA’s, and the study of their deformations,
independently
of the quantum setup — though everything about that might be deduced by specialization from the quantum level (cf. Theorem
6.1.2
). We obtain then a self-contained “chapter” of Lie superbialgebra theory, that introduces a new family of multiparameter Lie superbialgebras
and their deformations, the key fact being that this family is stable under deformations (by twist and by 2–cocycle), see Theorem
3.3.3
and Theorem
3.4.3
. These MpLSbA’s come equipped with a presentation “à la Serre”, in which the parameters rule the Lie superalgebra structure (cf. §
3.2
). In addition, every such MpLSbA admits an alternative presentation, in which the Lie algebra structure stands fixed, while the Lie coalgebra structure varies according to the multiparameter matrix
P
. Like in the quantum setup, the isomorphism between the two presentations is quite meaningful, as it boils down to a well-chosen change of generators. In short, we might say that our multiparameters are encoded in realizations, FoMpQUESA’s are quantizations of MpLSbA’s, and multiparameter objects are given by deformations of either the (super)algebra or the (super)coalgebra structure of uniparameter objects — see a diagrammatic summary in §
6.3
.
In all this,
the underlying Lie superalgebras are the (well-known) simple, contragredient ones of type
A
–
G\,
(in Kac’ classification)
: we are thus providing a new contribution to a classical topic of utmost importance. Even more, indeed,
all of our construction also extends,
verbatim
, to the setup of
affine
Lie superalgebras and their quantum counterparts
, as introduced in
[
Ya2
]
and
[
Ya3
]
.

Finally, we briefly introduce
polynomial
MpQUESA’s: mimicking the recipe of Lusztig & Jimbo for polynomial QUEA’s, these are certain subalgebras (up to modifying the ground ring) of our FoMpQUESA’s. We also study both types of deformations for these objects. Now the polar–
2
–cocycles become actually Hopf
2
–cocycles, and the deformation of the algebraic structure is well-understood. On the other hand, the twist deformations need to satisfy an arithmetic condition in order to be meaningful; nevertheless, these types of deformations cannot be seen,
strictu sensu
, as genuine “twist deformations” of our polynomial MpQUESA’s.
In fact, “formal” quantum groups are better suited to deal with deformations (up to resorting to the broader notion of “polar–
2
–cocycles”), so we focused mainly onto them.

One can also consider multiparameter versions of quantum formal series superalgebras on supergroups: in
[
GP
]
this is done by constructing such an object as dual to the FoMpQUESA’s of the present paper. The same goal was pursued in
[
Ma
]
via an FRT-construction: the link between
[
Ma
]
and
[
GP
]
is discussed in
[
GP
]
as well.

A last word about the organization of the paper, and its detailed content.

In Section
2
we introduce the “combinatorial data” underlying our constructions of MpLSbA’s and FoMpQUESA’s alike: the notion of multiparameters, their realizations and deformations. Then in Section
3
we present our MpLSbA’s, given by an explicit presentation (involving parameters) and bearing a Lie bialgebra structure which arise as deformation of one arising from Yamane’s QUESA’s. We also study their deformations, both by (toral) twists and by (toral) 2–cocycles:
in particular, this proves that the family of these Lie superbialgebras is
stable w.r.t. deformations
. In addition, before presenting our new results about multiparameter objects and their deformations, we quickly resume the general theory of Lie superbialgebras and their deformations, as the literature on the subject seems to be quite poor.

Section
4
is dedicated to our newly minted notion of FoMpQUESA’s. We begin by introducing them as superalgebras with a given presentation (by generators and relations), which closely mimics that of Yamane’s QUESA’s in
[
Ya1
]
but for involving more general parameters. Then we prove that they do bear a structure of Hopf superalgebra, which is obtained from that of a usual Yamane’s QUESA through deformation by a suitable (toral) twist followed by a suitable “change of variables”. Finally, we prove the functoriality (w.r.t. the underlying, Cartan-like combinatorial data) of our construction of FoMpQUEA’s, and their triangular decomposition.

We spend Section
5
to discuss deformations of FoMpQUEA’s by (toral) twists and by (toral) polar–2–cocycles. In short, we prove that these deformations turn FoMpQUEA’s into new FoMpQUEA’s again, hence the family of all FoMpQUESA’s is
stable by such deformations
. Moreover, under mild assumptions things go the other way around too, so that any FoMpQUESA’s can be realized as a suitable deformation — both by (toral) twist and by (toral) polar–2–cocycle — of one of Yamane’s uniparameter QUESA’s.

In Section
6
we compare the classical and quantum setups. Namely, we perform specializations of FoMpQUESA’s, showing that their semiclassical limit is always a MpLSbA, with the same underlying “Cartan datum”. Conversely, any possible MpLSbA arises as such a limit: in other words, for any MpLSbA there is a suitable FoMpQUESA which is quantization of it. As a second step, we compare deformations before and after specialization/quantization: the outcome is, in a nutshell, that “specialization/quantization and deformation commute with each other”.

Finally, in Section
7
we introduce the notion of
polynomial MpQUESA’s
, and we study their behavior under the processes of both types of deformations.

2.
Multiparameters and their realizations

In this section we fix the basic combinatorial data that we need. The definition of our multiparameter Lie superbialgebras and formal multiparameter quantum super groups requires a full lot of related material that we now present. In particular,
\,\mathbb{N}=\{0,1,\ldots\}\,
and
\,\mathbb{N}_{+}:=\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\}\,
,
while
\Bbbk
will be a field of characteristic zero. This material is actually taken from
[
GG3
]
, §2, up to minimal changes: we shall then skip many details (and proofs), as the reader can find them in
[
GG3
]
.

2.1.
Cartan data, multiparameter matrices, and realizations

We introduce hereafter the “multiparameters”, which we will use to construct (semi)classical and quantum objects as well. The theory can be developed in wide generality, yet we restrict ourselves to the setup more closely related with (contragredient) Lie superalgebras of simple type — the so-called
basic
ones.

2.1.1
.

Cartan data.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a complex, finite-dimensional simple Lie superalgebra.
By the classification results due to Kac — cf.
[
Ka2
]
—
we know that the isomorphism class of
\mathfrak{g}
falls within a family in a finite list,
namely
A_{N}
,
B_{N}
,
C_{N}
,
D_{N}
,
D(2\,,1;\alpha)
,
F_{4}
and

G_{3}\,
: we discard hereafter the case
D(2,1;\alpha)
, as we build upon

[
Ya1
]
, which also drops it. In short, we shall refer to these as
“
basic
Lie superalgebras” — of type
A\,
,
B\,
, etc. up to

G\,
, in short
A-G\,
.

Every basic Lie superalgebra
\mathfrak{g}
can be realized as a

contragredient
Lie superalgebra
, or a

suitable quotient
of it
, following Kac’ recipe in
[
Ka2
, §2.5.1]
:
this involves fixing a set of (positive) simple roots, say

\,\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n}\,
, with their parities
— given by a map
\,p:I\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{2}\,
— and an associated
“Cartan matrix”
\,A={\big(\,a_{ij}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\in\textsl{M}_{n}(\mathbb{Z})\,
— where
\,n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\,

is the
rank
of
\mathfrak{g}
and
\,I:=\{1,\dots,n\}\,
—
as the starting point of the construction. Note that the matrix
A\,
,
besides having integral coefficients, is also symmetrizable: indeed, we can (and will) also fix “minimal” elements
\,d_{i}\in\frac{\,1\,}{\,2\,}\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}\,(\,i=1,\dots,n\,)\,
such that
\,DA\,
is symmetric with integral entries, where
D
is the diagonal matrix
\,D:={\big(\,d_{i}\,\delta_{ij}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\,
.

We stress the fact that for the same basic Lie superalgebra

\mathfrak{g}
one has different pairs
(A,p)
that yield a realization of
\mathfrak{g}

as the contragredient Lie superalgebra associated with that pair.
In turn, every such pair corresponds (bijectively) to a suitable

Dynkin diagram
, after the recipe explained in
[
Ka2
, §2.5 (Tables IV–V)]
:
in particular, by default we “incorporate” the parity in the Dynkin diagram itself, by
labelling each vertex as explained in
[
Ka2
]
,
so that the label itself yields the parity of the corresponding (vertex, hence) simple root.
Namely, the label for a vertex in a Dynkin diagram is a “colour”, which is “white”,
“black” or “grey” respectively for the vertex
“
\,\circ\,
”, “
\,\bullet\,
” or “
\,\otimes\,
”:
then the parity is
{\bar{0}}
for white vertices and it is
{\bar{1}}

for black and for grey vertices. Therefore, we can construct our basic Lie superalgebras
directly from these (labelled) Dynkin diagrams. Still following
[
Ya1
]
,
we assume that in cases
F(4)
and
G(3)
the Dynkin is

distinguished
, i.e. it has exactly one vertex which is odd.

In the end, the list of all Dynkin diagrams we will deal with (arranged by type

A
,
B
, etc.) is the following (with the symbol “
\,\times\,
” standing
for a white or grey vertex):

A_{n}
1
2
3
n

B_{n}\phantom{i}(I)
1
2
3
n

B_{n}\phantom{i}(I\!I)
1
2
3
n

C_{n}
1
2
3
n

D_{n}\phantom{i}(I)
1
2
3
n
n-1

D_{n}\phantom{i}(I\!I)
1
2
3
n
n-1

F_{4}
1
4
3
2

G_{3}
1
3
2

Note
that every Dynkin diagram as above defines a parity function
\,p:I\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{2}\,
, given by
\,p(i):={\bar{0}}\,
if the vertex
i
(
\in I\,
) is white and
\,p(i):={\bar{1}}\,
if
i
is grey or black.

Definition 2.1.2
.

We will call
Cartan super-datum
any pair
(A,p)
where
A
is a Cartan matrix associated with one of the Dynkin diagrams listed in §
2.1.1
above, and
\,p:I\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{2}\,
is the parity function defined by that Dynkin diagram.
\diamondsuit

A basic tool in the subsequent construction is the following, taken from
[
GG3
]
, §2 (see also
[
Ka1
]
, Ch. 1):

Definition 2.1.3
.

Let
\hbar
be a formal variable, and
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
the ring of formal power series in
\hbar

with coefficients in
\Bbbk\,
. Let
\mathfrak{h}
be a free
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–module of finite rank, and pick subsets

\;\Pi^{\vee}:={\big\{T^{+}_{i},T^{-}_{i}\big\}}_{i\in I}\!\subseteq\mathfrak{h}\;
,
and

\;\Pi:={\big\{\alpha_{i}\big\}}_{i\in I}\subseteq\mathfrak{h}^{*}:=\operatorname{Hom}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\!\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\;
.
For later use, we also introduce the elements
\,S_{i}:=2^{-1}\big(\,T^{+}_{i}+T^{-}_{i}\big)\,
and

\,\varLambda_{i}:=2^{-1}\big(\,T^{+}_{i}-T^{-}_{i}\big)\,
(for
\,i\in I\,
) and the sets

\;\Sigma:={\big\{S_{i}\big\}}_{i\in I}\!\subseteq\mathfrak{h}\;
and

\;\Lambda:={\big\{\varLambda_{i}\big\}}_{i\in I}\!\subseteq\mathfrak{h}\;
.

Let
\,P\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
be any
(n\times n)
–matrix with entries in
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
.

(a)
We call the triple
\;{\mathcal{R}}\,:=\,\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\;

a
realization
of
P\,
over
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
,
with
rank
defined as
\,\operatorname{rk}({\mathcal{R}}):=\operatorname{rk}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}(\mathfrak{h})\,
,  if:

(a.1)

\;\alpha_{j}\big(\,T^{+}_{i}\big)\,=\,p_{\,ij}\;
,

\;\;\;\alpha_{j}\big(\,T^{-}_{i}\big)\,=\,p_{j\,i}\;
,  for all
\,i,j\in I\,
;

(a.2)
the set
\,\overline{\Sigma}:={\big\{\,\overline{S}_{i}:=S_{i}\;(\text{\,mod\ }\hbar\,\mathfrak{h}\,)\big\}}_{i\in I}\,

is
\Bbbk
–linearly independent in
\,\overline{\mathfrak{h}}:=\mathfrak{h}\Big/\hbar\,\mathfrak{h}\,
—

N.B.:
this is equivalent to saying that
\Sigma
itself can be completed to a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–basis of
\mathfrak{h}\,
,
hence in particular
\Sigma
is
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–linearly independent in
\,\mathfrak{h}\,
.

(b)
We call a realization
\;{\mathcal{R}}\,:=\,\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\;

of the matrix
P
, respectively,

(b.1)

straight
if the set
\,\overline{\Pi}:={\big\{\,\overline{\alpha}_{i}:=\alpha_{i}\;(\text{\,mod\ }\hbar\,\mathfrak{h}^{*})\big\}}_{i\in I}\,

is
\Bbbk
–linearly independent in
\,\overline{\mathfrak{h}^{*}}:=\mathfrak{h}^{*}\Big/\hbar\,\mathfrak{h}^{*}\,

—
N.B.:
this is equivalent to saying that
\Pi
can be completed to a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–basis of
\mathfrak{h}^{*}\,
,
thus in particular
\Pi
is
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–linearly independent in
\,\mathfrak{h}^{*}\,
;

(b.2)

small
if

\,\;\textsl{Span}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big({\{S_{i}\}}_{i\in I}\big)\;=\;\textsl{Span}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big({\big\{T_{i}^{+},T_{i}^{-}\big\}}_{i\in I}\big)\;\,
;

(b.3)

split
if the set

\,\overline{\Pi^{\vee}}:={\big\{\,\overline{T^{\pm}}_{i}:=T^{\pm}_{i}\;(\text{\,mod\ }\hbar\,\mathfrak{h}\,)\big\}}_{i\in I}\,

is
\Bbbk
–linearly independent in
\,\overline{\mathfrak{h}}:=\mathfrak{h}\Big/\hbar\,\mathfrak{h}\,
—
N.B.:

this is equivalent to saying that
\Pi^{\vee}
can be completed to a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–basis of
\mathfrak{h}\,
,
hence in particular it is
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–linearly independent in
\,\mathfrak{h}\,
;

(b.4)

minimal
if

\,\;\textsl{Span}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big({\big\{T_{i}^{+},T_{i}^{-}\big\}}_{i\in I}\big)\,=\;\mathfrak{h}\;\,

—
N.B.:
in particular,
{\mathcal{R}}
is
split
and
minimal
if and only if

\,{\big\{\,T_{i}^{+},T_{i}^{-}\big\}}_{i\in I}\,
is a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–basis of
\mathfrak{h}\,
.

(c)
For any pair of realizations
\;{\mathcal{R}}\,:=\,\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\;
and
\;\dot{{\mathcal{R}}}\,:=\,\big(\,\dot{\mathfrak{h}}\,,\dot{\Pi}\,,{\dot{\Pi}}^{\vee}\,\big)\;
of the same matrix
P
,
a
(homo)morphism

\,\underline{\phi}:{\mathcal{R}}\longrightarrow\dot{{\mathcal{R}}}\,
is the datum of any
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–module morphism
\,\phi:\mathfrak{h}\longrightarrow\dot{\mathfrak{h}}\,
such that
\,\phi\big(T_{i}^{\pm}\big)={\dot{T}}_{\sigma(i)}^{\pm}\,
and
\,\phi^{*}\big(\dot{\alpha}_{\sigma(i)}\big)=\alpha_{i}\,
(for all
\,i\in I\,
) for some permutation
\,\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{I}\,
— the symmetric group over
I
— hence, in particular,
\,\phi\big(\Pi^{\vee}\big)={\dot{\Pi}}^{\vee}\,
, and also that
\,\phi^{*}\big(\dot{\Pi}\big)=\Pi\,

—
N.B.:
realizations along with their morphisms form a category,
in which the iso–/epi–/mono–morphisms are those morphisms
\phi
as above that actually are
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–module iso–/epi–/mono–morphisms.

(d)
Let
A
be any Cartan matrix associated with a Dynkin diagram from the list given in §
2.1.1
, and let
\,D:={\big(\hskip 0.7ptd_{i}\,\delta_{ij}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\,
be the diagonal matrix associated with
A\,
, as in §
2.1.1
. We say that a square matrix
\,P\in M_{n}({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]})\,
is
of Cartan type
, with associated Cartan matrix
A\,
, if
\;P_{s}:=2^{-1}\big(P+P^{\,\scriptscriptstyle T}\big)=DA\;
.

N.B.:
condition
(b.3)
is equivalent to requiring that

\,\overline{\Sigma}\cup\overline{\Lambda}\,
be
\Bbbk
–linearly independent in

\,\overline{\mathfrak{h}}:=\mathfrak{h}\Big/\hbar\,\mathfrak{h}\,
;  in turn, this is equivalent to saying that

\,\Sigma\cup\Lambda\,
itself can be completed to a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–basis of

\mathfrak{h}\,
, hence in particular it is
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–linearly independent. Similarly, the condition

\,\phi\big(T_{i}^{\pm}\big)={\dot{T}}_{\sigma(i)}^{\pm}\,
—
\,i\in I\,
,
for some permutation
\,\sigma\in\mathbb{S}(I)\,
— in
(c)
can be replaced by

\,\phi(S_{i})={\dot{S}}_{\sigma(i)}\,
and
\,\phi(\varLambda_{i})={\dot{\varLambda}}_{\sigma(i)}\,
.

(e)
In an entirely similar way, one may define realizations of a matrix

\,P:={\big(\,p_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\in M_{n}(\Bbbk)\,
over a ground field
\Bbbk\,
.
Such a realization
\,{\mathcal{R}}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,
consists of a
\Bbbk
–vector space

\mathfrak{h}
and distinguished subsets
\;\Pi^{\vee}:={\big\{T^{+}_{i},T^{-}_{i}\big\}}_{i\in I}\!\subseteq\mathfrak{h}\;

and
\;\Pi:={\big\{\alpha_{i}\big\}}_{i\in I}\subseteq\mathfrak{h}^{*}:=\operatorname{Hom}_{\Bbbk}\!\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Bbbk\big)\;
:
then condition
(a.1)
reads the same, while
(a.2)
instead says that the set

\,{\big\{\,S_{i}=2^{-1}\big(T^{+}_{i}+T^{-}_{i}\big)\,\big\}}_{i\in I}\,
is linearly independent, and the

rank
of the realization is
\,\operatorname{rk}({\mathcal{R}}):=\dim_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{h})\,
. Also,
{\mathcal{R}}
is
straight
,
resp.
split
, if
\Pi\,
, resp.
\Pi^{\vee}
, is linearly independent.
\diamondsuit

The following consequence of the definitions yields a close link with Kac’ notion:

Lemma 2.1.4
.

(cf.
[
GG3
]
, Lemma 2.1.4))

Let
\,P\in M_{n}({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]})\,
be a matrix as above. If
\;{\mathcal{R}}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,

is a straight realization of
\,P
, then the triple
\,\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}_{S}\big)\,

— with
\,\Pi^{\vee}_{S}\!:={\{S_{i}\}}_{i\in I}\,
— is a realization of
\,P_{s}:=2^{-1}\big(P+P^{\,\scriptscriptstyle T}\big)\,

— over the ring
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
— in the sense of
[
Ka1
]
, Ch. 1, §1.1, but for condition (1.1.3).
\square

Note that condition (1.1.3) in
[
Ka1
, Ch. 1, §1.1]
, is fulfilled whenever
\,\operatorname{rk}(\mathfrak{h})=2\,n-\operatorname{rk}(P_{s})\,
;
in particular, we can always achieve that condition up to suitably enlarging or restricting
\mathfrak{h}\,
.
In any case, from now on with any straight realization of a matrix
P
of Cartan type, for some Cartan matrix
A\,
,
we shall always associate the realization of
\,P_{s}=DA\,
given by Lemma
2.1.4
,
hence also the corresponding realization of
A
and then all the related data and machinery mentioned in §
2.1
.

We collect now a few technical results:

Proposition 2.1.5
.

(cf.
[
GG3
]
, Proposition 2.1.5)

(a)  For every
\,P\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
and every
\,\ell\geq 3\,n-\operatorname{rk}\big(P+P^{\,\scriptscriptstyle T}\big)\,
,
there exists a straight split realization of
P
with
\,\operatorname{rk}(\mathfrak{h})=\ell\,
,
which
is unique up to isomorphisms.

(b)  Claim (a) still holds true if we drop the word “straight” and pick
\,\ell\geq 2\,n\,
.
\square

Remark 2.1.6
.

It follows from definitions that a
necessary
condition for a small realization of any
\,P\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,

to exist is
\,\operatorname{rk}\!\big(P_{s}\,\big|P_{a}\big)=\operatorname{rk}(P_{s})\,
.
Conversely, with much the same arguments used in the proof of
Proposition
2.1.5
, we can prove that such a condition is also
sufficient
, as the following holds true, indeed:

Proposition 2.1.7
.

(cf.
[
GG3
]
, Proposition 2.1.7)

If
\,P\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
is such that
\,\operatorname{rk}\!\big(P_{s}\,\big|P_{a}\big)=\operatorname{rk}(P_{s})\,
,
then, for all
\,\ell\geq 2\,n-\operatorname{rk}(P_{s})\,
,
there exists a straight small realization of
\,P
with
\,\operatorname{rk}(\mathfrak{h})=\ell\,
,
and such a realization is unique up to isomorphisms.
\square

After this existence results concerning realizations of special type, we can achieve a more general result with two additional steps. The first one tells in short that every realization can be “lifted” to a
split
one:

Lemma 2.1.8
.

(cf.
[
GG3
]
, Lemma 2.1.8)

Let
\;{\mathcal{R}}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\;
be a realization of
\,P\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
. Then:

(a)  There exists a
split
realization
\;\dot{{\mathcal{R}}}:=\big(\,\dot{\mathfrak{h}}\,,\dot{\Pi}\,,{\dot{\Pi}}^{\vee}\,\big)\,
of the same matrix
P
and an epimorphism of realizations
\;\underline{\pi}:\dot{{\mathcal{R}}}\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{R}}\;
such that, if
\,\mathfrak{h}_{{}_{T}}:=\textsl{Span}\Big({\big\{T_{i}^{\pm}\big\}}_{i\in I}\Big)\,
and
\,\dot{\mathfrak{h}}_{{}_{T}}:=\textsl{Span}\Big({\big\{\dot{T}_{i}^{\pm}\big\}}_{i\in I}\Big)\,
, then
\,\underline{\pi}\,
induces an isomorphism
\;\dot{\pi}:\dot{\mathfrak{h}}\Big/\dot{\mathfrak{h}}_{{}_{T}}\cong\mathfrak{h}\Big/\mathfrak{h}_{{}_{T}}\;
.

If in addition
\,{\mathcal{R}}
is
straight
, resp.
minimal
, then a
split
realization
\,\dot{{\mathcal{R}}}

as above can be found that is
straight
, resp.
minimal
, as well.

(b)  There exists a
straight
realization
\;\hat{{\mathcal{R}}}:=\big(\,\hat{\mathfrak{h}}\,,\hat{\Pi}\,,{\hat{\Pi}}^{\vee}\,\big)\,
of the same matrix
P
and an epimorphism of realizations
\;\underline{\pi}:\hat{{\mathcal{R}}}\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{R}}\;
such that, if
\,\mathfrak{h}_{{}_{T}}:=\textsl{Span}\Big({\big\{T_{i}^{\pm}\big\}}_{i\in I}\Big)\,
and
\,\hat{\mathfrak{h}}_{{}_{T}}:=\textsl{Span}\Big({\big\{\hat{T}_{i}^{\pm}\big\}}_{i\in I}\Big)\,
, then
\,\underline{\pi}\,
induces an isomorphism
\;\hat{\pi}:\hat{\mathfrak{h}}_{{}_{T}}\cong\mathfrak{h}_{{}_{T}}\;
.

If in addition
\,{\mathcal{R}}
is
split
, resp.
minimal
, then a
straight
realization
\,\hat{{\mathcal{R}}}

as above can be found that is
split
, resp.
minimal
, as well.

Proof.

(a)
This is nothing but Lemma 2.1.8 in
[
GG3
]
.

(b)
Take
\,\hat{\mathfrak{h}}:=\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}\,
for some
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–module
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}
such that the
\alpha_{i}
’s extend from
\mathfrak{h}
to all of
\hat{\mathfrak{h}}
so to give linearly independent elements
\hat{\alpha}_{i}
’s of
\hat{\mathfrak{h}}\,
: e.g., choose
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}
to be a free
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–module of rank
|I|
with
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–basis
\,{\{e_{j}\}}_{j\in I}\,
and set
\,\hat{\alpha}_{i}{\big|}_{\mathfrak{h}}:=\alpha_{i}\,
and
\,\hat{\alpha}_{i}(e_{j}):=\delta_{i,j}\,
for all
\,i,j\in I\,
. Then
\;\hat{{\mathcal{R}}}:=\big(\,\hat{\mathfrak{h}}\,,\hat{\Pi}:={\big\{\,\hat{\alpha}_{i}\,\big\}}_{i\in I}\,,{\hat{\Pi}}^{\vee}:=\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,
is a
straight
realization of
P
as required, which is even split, respectively minimal, if so is
{\mathcal{R}}\,
; moreover, an epimorphism
\;\underline{\pi}:\hat{{\mathcal{R}}}\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{R}}\;
is given by the natural projection
\,\pi:\hat{\mathfrak{h}}:=\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{h}\,
, which induces an isomorphism
\;\hat{\pi}:\hat{\mathfrak{h}}_{{}_{T}}\cong\mathfrak{h}_{{}_{T}}\;
as required.
∎

A last, significant result concerns morphisms between realizations.

Lemma 2.1.9
.

(cf.
[
GG3
]
, Lemma 2.1.8)

Let
\;\hat{{\mathcal{R}}}\,:=\,\big(\,\hat{\mathfrak{h}}\,,\hat{\Pi}\,,\hat{\Pi}^{\vee}\,\big)\;
and

\;\check{{\mathcal{R}}}\,:=\,\big(\,\check{\mathfrak{h}}\,,\check{\Pi}\,,\check{\Pi}^{\vee}\,\big)\;

be two realizations of a same
\,P\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
, and let

\;\underline{\phi}:\hat{{\mathcal{R}}}\longrightarrow\check{{\mathcal{R}}}\;

be a morphism between them. Then

\;\operatorname{Ker}\big(\,\phi:\hat{\mathfrak{h}}\longrightarrow\check{\mathfrak{h}}\,\big)\,\subseteq\,\bigcap\limits_{j\in I}\operatorname{Ker}(\hat{\alpha}_{j})\;\,
.
\square

Remark 2.1.10
.

Working with matrices in
\,M_{n}(\Bbbk)\,
and realizations of them over
\Bbbk
(for some field
\Bbbk\,
), all the previous constructions still make sense; some results (e.g., Proposition
2.1.5
and Lemma
2.1.8
) even get stronger and/or have simpler proofs.

2.2.
Twist deformations of multiparameters and realizations

In this subsection we introduce the notion of
deformation by twist
of realizations, which will be needed later when dealing with (quantum) super-Lie theoretical objects. Like before, we are essentially recollecting results from
[
GG3
]
, §2.2, so we skip those details that can be found there.

2.2.1
.

Deforming realizations (and matrices) by twist.
Fix
\,P:={\big(\,p_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,

and a realization
\;{\mathcal{R}}\,:=\,\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,
,
possibly — up to changing minimal details in what follows — over
\Bbbk
if
\,P\in M_{n}(\Bbbk)\,
.

Recall that
\mathfrak{h}
is, by assumption, a free
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–module of finite rank
\,t:=\operatorname{rk}(\mathfrak{h})\,
.
We fix in
\mathfrak{h}
any
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–basis
\,{\big\{H_{g}\big\}}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\,
,
where
\mathcal{G}
is an index set with
\,|\mathcal{G}|=\operatorname{rk}(\mathfrak{h})=t\,
.

Fix an antisymmetric square matrix
\;\Phi=\big(\phi_{gk}\big)_{g,k\in\mathcal{G}}\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\;

— in fact, we might work with any
\,\Phi\in M_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
,
but at some point we should single out its antisymmetric part

\,\Phi_{a}:=2^{-1}\big(\Phi-\Phi^{\scriptscriptstyle T}\,\big)\,

which would be all that matters. We define the
twisted “distinguished toral elements” (or “coroots”)

T^{\pm}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\ell}\;:=\;T^{\pm}_{\ell}\,\pm{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})\,\phi_{kg}\,H_{k}

(2.1)

As a matter of notation, let

\,\mathsf{T}:=\bigg(\,{\displaystyle{\underline{T}^{+}\atop\underline{T}^{-}}}\bigg)\,

be the
(2n\times 1)
–matrix given by the column vectors

\,\underline{T}^{\pm}={\big(\,T_{i}^{\pm}\big)}_{i\in I}\,
. Similarly, let

\;\mathsf{T}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}}:=\bigg(\,{\displaystyle{\underline{T}^{+}_{\,\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\atop\underline{T}^{-}_{\,\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}}}\bigg)\;

be the
(2n\times 1)
–matrix given by the (superposed) two column vectors

\,\underline{T}^{\pm}_{\,\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}={\big(\,T_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{\pm}\big)}_{i\in I}\,
,
and
\mathsf{H}
the column vector
\,\mathsf{H}:={\big(\,H_{g}\big)}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\,
.
Moreover, denote by
\mathfrak{A}
the
(n\times t)
–matrix with entries in
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
given by

\;\mathfrak{A}:={\big(\,\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})\big)}_{\ell\in I}^{g\in\mathcal{G}}\;
,  and set

\;\mathfrak{A}_{\bullet}:=\begin{pmatrix}+\mathfrak{A}\,\\
-\mathfrak{A}\,\end{pmatrix}\;

— a matrix of size
(2n\times t)\,
.

Now, using matrix notation we have

\;\mathsf{T}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,=\,\mathsf{T}-\,\mathfrak{A}_{\bullet}\,\Phi\,\mathsf{H}\;
.
Eventually, define also

P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,=\,{\big(\,p^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\,:=\,{\Big(\,p_{i,j}+{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\alpha_{j}(H_{g})\,\phi_{g,k}\,\alpha_{i}(H_{k})\Big)}_{i,j\in I}\,=\,P-\mathfrak{A}\,\Phi\,\mathfrak{A}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle T}

(2.2)

Now, using the above notation, a direct computation yields

\hfil\displaystyle S_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}:=\,2^{-1}\big(\,T^{+}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}+T^{-}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}\big)\,=\,2^{-1}\big(\,T^{+}_{i}+T^{-}_{i}\big)=S_{i}\qquad\qquad\forall\;\;i\in I

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad\alpha_{j}\big(\,T^{+}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}\,\big)\,=\,p^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i,j}\quad,\qquad\alpha_{j}\big(\,T^{-}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}\,\big)\,=\,p^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j,i}\qquad\qquad\qquad\forall\;\;i,j\in I

so that the triple
\;{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}_{\Phi}\,,\Pi_{\Phi}\,,\Pi^{\vee}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,\big)\;

with
\,\mathfrak{h}_{\Phi}:=\mathfrak{h}\,
,
\,\Pi_{\Phi}:=\Pi\,
and

\,\Pi^{\vee}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}:=\big\{\,T^{+}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}\,,T^{-}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}\,\big|\,i\in I\,\big\}\,
,  is a realization of the matrix

\,P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}={\big(\,p^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\,

— as in Definition
2.1.3
; also, by construction

{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}
is also
straight
, resp.
small
,
if and only if such is
{\mathcal{R}}\,
. Moreover,
P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}

is the sum of
P
plus an antisymmetric matrix, so

the symmetric part of
P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}
is the same as
P
,
i.e.
\,{\big(P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}_{s}=P_{s}\,
.
In particular, if
P
is of Cartan type, then so is
P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,
,
and they are associated with the same Cartan matrix. In short, we get:

Proposition 2.2.2
.

(cf.
[
GG3
]
, Proposition 2.2.2)

With notation as above, the following holds true:

(a)  the matrix
\;P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,:=\,P-\,\mathfrak{A}\,\Phi\,\mathfrak{A}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle T}\;

obeys
\,{\big(P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}_{s}=P_{s}\,
;  in particular, if

P
is of Cartan type, then so is
P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,
,
and they are associated with the same Cartan matrix.

(b)  the triple
\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}_{\Phi}\!:=\mathfrak{h}\,,\,\Pi_{\Phi}\!:=\Pi\,,\,\Pi^{\vee}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\!:={\big\{T^{+}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}\,,T^{-}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}\big\}}_{i\in I}\,\big)\,

is a realization of
\,P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,
;  moreover,
{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}
is
straight
,
resp.
small
, if and only if such is
{\mathcal{R}}\,
.
\square

Definition 2.2.3
.

The realization
\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,\big)\,

of the matrix
\,P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}={\big(\,p^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}

is called a
twist deformation
(via
\Phi\,
) of the realization

\;{\mathcal{R}}=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,
of
P\,
.

Similarly, the matrix
P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}
is called a
twist deformation

of the matrix
P
.
\diamondsuit

Remarks 2.2.4
.

(a)
Observe that, by the very definition of twisting one has that

\big(P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi^{\prime}}=P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi+\Phi^{\prime}}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\big({\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi^{\prime}}={\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi+\Phi^{\prime}}\qquad\text{ for all }\quad\Phi\,,\,\Phi^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)

Therefore, the additive group
\,\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
acts on the set of
(multiparameter) matrices of size
\,n:=|I|\,
with fixed symmetric part,
as well as on the set of their realizations of (any) fixed rank.
When two matrices

\,P,P^{\prime}\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
belong to the same orbit of this
\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)
–action we say that

P
and
P^{\prime}
are twist equivalent
.

(b)
It follows from Proposition
2.2.2
(a)

that if two multiparameter matrices
P
and
P^{\prime}
are twist equivalent,
then their symmetric part is the same, that is
\,P_{s}=P^{\prime}_{s}\,
.
Next result shows the converse holds true as well,
up to taking the group

\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)
big enough, namely with
\,t\geq 3\,n-\operatorname{rk}(P_{s})\,
.

Lemma 2.2.5
.

(cf.
[
GG3
]
, Lemma 2.2.5)

With notation as above, let
\,P,P^{\prime}\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
,
and consider the aforementioned action by twist on
M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)

by any additive group
\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)
with
\,t\geq 3\,n-\operatorname{rk}(P_{s})\,
.
Then
P
and
P^{\prime}\,
are twist equivalent if and only if
\,P_{s}=P^{\prime}_{s}\,
.
\square

Next proposition “upgrades” the previous result to the level of realizations.

Proposition 2.2.6
.

(cf.
[
GG3
]
, Proposition 2.2.7)

Let
P
and
P^{\prime}
be two matrices in
M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)
with the same symmetric part, i.e. such that
\,P_{s}=P^{\prime}_{s}\;
.

(a)  Let
\,{\mathcal{R}}
be a
straight
realization of
P
of rank
\,t\geq 3\,n-\operatorname{rk}(P_{s})\,
. Then there is
\,\Phi\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
such that
\,P^{\prime}=P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,
and the corresponding realization
\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,
is straight.

In short, if
\,P^{\prime}_{s}=P_{s}\,
then from any
straight
realization of
P
we can obtain by twist deformation a straight realization (of the same rank) for
P^{\prime}
, and viceversa.

(b)  Let
\,{\mathcal{R}}
and
{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}
be
straight small
realizations of
\,P
and
P^{\prime}\,
, such that
\,\operatorname{rk}({\mathcal{R}})=\operatorname{rk}({\mathcal{R}}^{\prime})=:t\geq 3\,n-\operatorname{rk}(P_{s})\,
.
Then there exists

\,\Phi\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
such that
\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\cong{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,
.

In short, if
\,P^{\prime}_{s}=P_{s}\,
then any straight small realization of
P^{\prime}
is isomorphic to a twist deformation of a
straight small realization of
P
of same rank, and viceversa.

(c)  Every
straight small
realization
\,{\mathcal{R}}
of
P
is isomorphic to some
twist deformation of the standard realization of
P_{s}
of the same rank as
\,{\mathcal{R}}\,
,

as in Lemma
2.1.4
.
\square

2.2.7
.

Stability issues for twisted realizations.
Applying deformation by twist upon a given realization provides a new realization (for a different matrix, in general). If the initial realization is
split
, however, the new realization achieved through deformation is
not
necessarily split, in general. This issue is discussed at length in
[
GG3
]
, §§2.2.8–9, where examples and counterexamples are given as well.

2.3.
2-cocycle deformations of multiparameters and realizations

In this subsection we introduce the notion of
deformation by 2–cocycles
of realizations (and of multiparameters), which is dual to deformation by twist. Here again, we directly report material from
[
GG3
]
, §2.3, so we skip all secondary details that can be found there.

2.3.1
.

Deforming realizations (and matrices) by
2
–cocycles.
Fix again

\,P:={\big(\,p_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
and a realization

\,{\mathcal{R}}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,
of it.
We consider special deformations of realizations, called “2-cocycle deformations”. To this end, like in §
2.2
, we fix in
\mathfrak{h}
a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–basis

\,{\big\{H_{g}\big\}}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\,
, with
\mathcal{G}
an index set,
\,|\mathcal{G}|=\operatorname{rk}(\mathfrak{h})=t\,
.

Let
\,\chi:\mathfrak{h}\times\mathfrak{h}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
be any
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–bilinear map:
note that it bijectively corresponds to some

\,X={\big(\,\chi_{g{}\gamma}\big)}_{g,\gamma\in\mathcal{G}}\in M_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
via

\,\chi_{g{}\gamma}=\chi(H_{g}\,,H_{\gamma})\,
. We assume that
\chi
is antisymmetric, which means

\,\chi^{\scriptscriptstyle T}(x,y)=-\chi(x,y)\,
where

\,\chi^{\scriptscriptstyle T}(x,y):=\chi(y,x)\,
,  for all
\,x,y\in\mathfrak{h}\,
;
this is equivalent to saying that
X
is antisymmetric, i.e.
\,X\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
.
We denote by
\,\text{\sl Alt}_{\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}}\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\times\mathfrak{h}\,,\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,\big)\,
the set of all antisymmetric,

{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–bilinear maps from
\,\mathfrak{h}\times\mathfrak{h}\,
to
\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
.
We assume also that
\chi
obeys

\chi(S_{i}\,,\,-\,)\,=\,0\,=\,\chi(\,-\,,S_{i})\qquad\qquad\forall\;\;i\in I

(2.3)

where
\,S_{i}:=2^{-1}\big(\,T^{+}_{i}+T^{-}_{i}\big)\,
for all
\,i\in I\,
. In particular,
this implies (for
\,i\in I\,
,
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
) that

\;\chi\big(+T_{i}^{+},\,T\,\big)\,=\,\chi\big(-T_{i}^{-},\,T\,\big)\;
,
\;\chi\big(\,T\,,+T_{i}^{+}\big)\,=\,\chi\big(\,T\,,-T_{i}^{-}\big)\;
,
hence

+\chi\big(\,\text{--}\,,T_{i}^{+}\big)=-\chi\big(\,\text{--}\,,T_{i}^{-}\big)\qquad\qquad\forall\;\;i\in I

(2.4)

For later use,
we introduce also the notation

\text{\sl Alt}_{\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}}^{\,S}(\mathfrak{h})\,:=\,\Big\{\,\chi\in\text{\sl Alt}_{\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}}\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\times\mathfrak{h}\,,\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,\big)\,\Big|\;\chi\text{\ obeys\ }(\ref{eq: condition-chi})\,\Big\}

(2.5)

and to each
\,\chi\in\text{\sl Alt}_{\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}}^{\,S}(\mathfrak{h})\,
we associate

\,\mathring{X}:={\Big(\mathring{\chi}_{i{}j}=\chi\big(\,T_{i}^{+},T_{j}^{+}\big)\!\Big)}_{i,j\in I}\!\in\mathfrak{so}_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
.

Basing on the above, we define

P_{(\chi)}:=\,P\,+\,\mathring{X}\,=\,{\Big(\,p^{(\chi)}_{i{}j}:=\,p_{ij}+\mathring{\chi}_{i{}j}\Big)}_{\!i,j\in I}\;\,,\!\quad\Pi_{(\chi)}:=\,{\Big\{\,\alpha_{i}^{(\chi)}:=\,\alpha_{i}\pm\chi\big(\,\text{--}\,,T_{i}^{\pm}\big)\Big\}}_{i\in I}

We are now ready for our key result on
2
–cocycle deformations.

Proposition 2.3.2
.

(cf.
[
GG3
]
, Proposition 2.3.2)

Keep notation as above. Then:

(a)
\,P_{(\chi)}\,:=\,P\,+\,\mathring{X}\;
obeys
\,{\big(P_{(\chi)}\big)}_{s}=P_{s}\,
;
in particular, if
P
is of Cartan type, then so is
P_{(\chi)}\,
,
and they are associated with the same Cartan matrix.

(b)  the triple
\,{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)}\,=\,\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi_{(\chi)}\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,

is a realization of the matrix
\,P_{(\chi)}\,
,  which is minimal, resp. split, if so is
\,{\mathcal{R}}\,
.
\square

Definition 2.3.3
.

The realization
\,{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)}=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi_{(\chi)}\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,
of

\,P_{(\chi)}={\big(\,p^{(\chi)}_{i{}j}\big)}_{\!i,j\in I}\,
is called a

2–cocycle deformation
of the realization

\;{\mathcal{R}}=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,
of
P\,
.

Similarly, the matrix
P_{(\chi)}
is called a
2–cocycle deformation
of the matrix
P
.
\diamondsuit

Remarks 2.3.4
.

(a)
The very definitions give

\big(P_{(\chi)}\big)_{(\chi)^{\prime}}=P_{(\chi+\chi^{\prime})}\quad\text{ and }\quad\big({\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)}\big)_{(\chi^{\prime})}={\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi+\chi^{\prime})}\qquad\text{ for all }\;\;\chi\,,\,\chi^{\prime}\in\text{\sl Alt}_{\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}}^{\,S}(\mathfrak{h})

Thus, the additive group
\,\text{\sl Alt}_{\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}}^{\,S}(\mathfrak{h})\,
acts on the set of
(multiparameter) matrices of size
\,n:=|I|\,

with fixed symmetric part, as well as on the set of their realizations of (any) fixed rank.

When two matrices
P
and
P^{\prime}
in
\,M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,

belong to the same orbit of this
\text{\sl Alt}_{\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}}^{\,S}(\mathfrak{h})
–action,
we say that
P
and
P^{\prime}
are 2–cocycle equivalent
.

(b)
It follows from Proposition
2.3.2
(a)

that if two multiparameter matrices
P
and
P^{\prime}
are
2
–cocycle equivalent,
then their symmetric part is the same, i.e.
\,P_{s}=P^{\prime}_{s}\,
.
As a consequence of the next result, the converse holds true as well
(cf. Lemma
2.3.6
below),
under mild, additional assumptions
.

Next result concerns the aforementioned
\text{\sl Alt}_{\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}}^{\,S}(\mathfrak{h})
–action
on realizations; indeed, up to minor details it can be seen as the
“
2
–cocycle analogue” of Proposition
2.2.6
:

Proposition 2.3.5
.

(cf.
[
GG3
]
, Proposition 2.3.2)

Let
\,P,P^{\prime}\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
be two matrices with the same symmetric part,
i.e. such that
\,P_{s}=P^{\prime}_{s}\,
. Moreover, let
\,{\mathcal{R}}
be a
split
realization of
P
.

(a)  There exists a map
\,\chi\in\text{\sl Alt}_{\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}}^{\,S}(\mathfrak{h})\,

such that
\,P^{\prime}=P_{(\chi)}\,
and the realization

\,{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)}\,=\,\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi_{(\chi)}\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,
of
\,P^{\prime}=P_{(\chi)}\,

is
split
. In a nutshell, if
\,P^{\prime}_{s}=P_{s}\,
then from any
split
realization of
P

we can obtain a split realization (of the same rank) of
P^{\prime}
by 2–cocycle deformation, and viceversa.

(b)  Assume in addition that
\,{\mathcal{R}}
be
minimal
.
Then
\,{\mathcal{R}}
is isomorphic to a 2–cocycle deformation of the split minimal realization of
\,P_{s}\,
.
\square

As a byproduct, we find the following “2-cocycle counterpart” of Lemma
2.2.5
:

Lemma 2.3.6
.

(cf.
[
GG3
, Lemma 2.3.6]
)

With notation as above, let
\,P,P^{\prime}\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
. Then
P
and
P^{\prime}
are 2-cocycle equivalent (for the above 2-cocycle action on
M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)
of an additive group
\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
) if and only if
\,P_{s}=P^{\prime}_{s}\,
.
\square

Observation 2.3.7
.

To sum up, we wish to stress the following, remarkable fact. Consider two matrices

\,P,P^{\prime}\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,

with the same symmetric part
\,P_{s}=P^{\prime}_{s}\,
, and a realization

\,{\mathcal{R}}=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,
of
P

that is
split
and
straight
. Then, by
Proposition
2.2.6
and Proposition
2.3.5
,
one can construct
two
realizations
{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}
and

{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)}
of
P^{\prime}
by a twist deformation,
respectively a 2-cocycle deformation, of
{\mathcal{R}}
that affects only the coroot set
\Pi^{\vee}

or the root set
\Pi\,
, respectively; in particular,

{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}
is still straight (yet possibly not split) and
{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)}

is still split (yet possibly not straight),
while both have the same rank as
{\mathcal{R}}\,
.

3.
Multiparameter Lie superbialgebras and their deformations

In this section we first recall the notion of Lie superbialgebra, and we explain their theory of deformations, both by twist and by 2–cocycles. Then we introduce our
multiparameter Lie superbialgebras
(MpLSbAs in short). Finally, we study deformations of MpLSbAs, both by toral twists and by toral 2–cocycles, proving in particular that our family of MpLSbAs is
stable
for deformations of either type.

3.1.
Lie superbialgebras and their deformations

We recall hereafter a few notions — including some partially new, to the best of our knowledge — concerning Lie superbialgebras and their deformations. For basic definitions and results see
[
An
,
Ka2
]
; as to notation, we adopt the same used for Lie bialgebras in
[
GG3
]
. We work over any field of characteristic different from 2.

3.1.1
.

Generalities on Lie superbialgebras.

We begin by giving some basic and (more or less) well-known definitions.

A
super vector space
(or “vector superspace”) is a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
–graded vector space
\,V=V_{\bar{0}}\oplus V_{\bar{1}}\,
; the homogeneous component
V_{\bar{0}}
is called the even part and
V_{\bar{1}}
the odd part. For any homogeneous element
\,v\in V\,
,  we denote by
\,|v|\in\{{\bar{0}},{\bar{1}}\}=:\mathbb{Z}_{2}\,
the degree of
v\,
, which is defined by
\,|v|=\overline{a}\iff v\in V_{\overline{a}}\;
. By a common abuse of notation, in the sequel whenever a formula involves parity of some elements, one assumes that the concerned elements be homogeneous, and then extends the validity of that formula to all elements by linearity.

Any super vector space has a
braiding
map
\,\sigma:V\otimes V\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow V\otimes V\,
defined by
\,\sigma(v\otimes w):={(-1)}^{|v||w|}w\otimes v\,
for all
\,v,w\in V\,
,  which satisfies the braid equation
\,(\sigma\otimes\operatorname{id})(\operatorname{id}\otimes\sigma)(\sigma\otimes\operatorname{id})=(\operatorname{id}\otimes\sigma)(\sigma\otimes\operatorname{id})(\operatorname{id}\otimes\sigma)\,
. As
\,\sigma^{2}=\operatorname{id}\,
, the map
\sigma
induces a representation of the symmetric group
\mathbb{S}_{n}
on the
n
–th tensor power
T^{n}(V)
of
V
. In particular, one has the super symmetrizer operator
\,\operatorname{Sym}_{n}:T^{n}(V)\longrightarrow T^{n}(V)\,
given by
\,\operatorname{Sym}_{n}:=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{S}_{n}}\mu\,
and the super antisymmetrizer operator
\,\operatorname{Alt}_{n}:T^{n}(V)\longrightarrow T^{n}(V)\,
given by
\,\operatorname{Alt}_{n}:=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{S}_{n}}{(-1)}^{\ell(\mu)}\mu\,
;  note that, in order to compute the action of the operators, one has to write each permutation as a product of transpositions. With this action in mind, the exterior powers
\,\Lambda^{n}(V)\,
of
V
are defined, as usual, as the subspace of
T^{n}(V)
given by the image of
\operatorname{Alt}_{n}\,
,  or more explicitly as

\Lambda^{n}(V)\,\;:=\;\,\big\{\,v_{(n)}\in T^{n}(V)\,\big|\,\mu\,.\,v_{(n)}={(-1)}^{\ell(\mu)}v_{(n)}\,\;\forall\;\mu\in\mathbb{S}_{n}\,\big\}

In particular,
\Lambda^{2}(V)=\big\{\,v_{(2)}\in V\otimes V\,\big|\,\sigma\big(v_{(2)}\big)=-v_{(2)}\,\big\}=\operatorname{Ker}(\operatorname{id}+\sigma)\,
.

Similarly one defines the symmetric powers
\,S^{n}(V):=\operatorname{Im}(\operatorname{Sym}_{n})\,
.

A
Lie superalgebra
is a pair
\,\big(\,\mathfrak{g}\,,[\,\ ,\ ]\,\big)\,
where
\,\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{g}_{{\bar{0}}}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{{\bar{1}}}\,
is a super vector space and
\,[\,\ ,\ ]:\mathfrak{g}\times\mathfrak{g}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\mathfrak{g}\,
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
–graded bilinear map, i.e.
\,[\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha},\mathfrak{g}_{\beta}]\subseteq\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha+\beta}\,
for all
\,\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}\,
,  called a (Lie) superbracket, satisfying the following conditions for any choice of homogeneous elements
\,x,y,z\in\mathfrak{g}\,
:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\,[x,y]+{(-1)}^{|x||y|}[y,x]

(3.1)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\,{(-1)}^{|x||z|}[x,[y,z]]+{(-1)}^{|y||x|}[y,[z,x]]+{(-1)}^{|z||y|}[z,[x,y]]

The first condition in (
3.1
) is called
super
antisymmetry
: it can be expressed in element-free form by saying that
\,[\,\ ,\ ]\,
is a map from
\,\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\,
to
\mathfrak{g}
such that

0\;=\;[\,\ ,\ ]\circ(\operatorname{id}+\,\sigma)\;=\;[\,\ ,\ ]\circ\operatorname{Sym}_{2}

Note that in general one has that
\;(\operatorname{id}-(1,2))\circ\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{n}}\,=\,\operatorname{Alt}_{n}\;
,  where
\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{n}}
is the symmetrizer of the alternating subgroup
\mathbb{A}_{n}\,
. In case
\,n=3\,
this reads

\operatorname{Alt}_{3}\,\;=\;\,(\operatorname{id}-(1,2))\circ\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\,\;=\;\,(\operatorname{id}-(1,2))\circ\big(\operatorname{id}+(1,2,3)+(3,2,1)\big)

The action of the operator
\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}
on
T(\mathfrak{g})
is also usually called
“(action by) cyclic permutations”
and written as
“c.p.”

The second condition in (
3.1
) is called the
super Jacobi
identity. One can express the latter by stating that the adjoint action of any element is a
super derivation
, i.e.

[x,[y,z]]\,\;=\;\,[[x,y],z]\,+\,{(-1)}^{|x||y|}[y,[x,z]]

or by using the antisymmetrizer operator

[[\,\ ,\ ]\,,\ ]\circ\operatorname{Alt}_{3}\;=\;0\qquad\text{which is equivalent to}\qquad[[\,\ ,\ ]\,,\ ]\circ\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\;=\;0

It is worth noting that the operators
\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{n}}
and
\operatorname{Alt}_{n}
actually commute with the action of
\mathfrak{g}
on tensor powers
T^{n}(\mathfrak{g})\,
. Indeed,
\mathfrak{g}
acts on itself by the adjoint action
\,x\,.\,y\,:=\,\operatorname{ad}(x)(y)=[x,y]\,
for all
\,x,y\in\mathfrak{g}\,
. This action extends to the super vector space
T^{n}(\mathfrak{g})
by

x\,.\,(y_{1}\otimes y_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes y_{n})\,\;:=\;\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}}\,{(-1)}^{|x|\sum_{k=1}^{i-1}|y_{k}|}y_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes[x,y_{i}]\otimes\cdots\otimes y_{n}

(3.2)

for all elementary tensors
\,y_{1}\otimes y_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes y_{n}\in T^{n}(\mathfrak{g})\,
with homogeneous factors. For example, the adjoint action on
\,\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\,
reads

x\,.\,(y\otimes z)\,\;=\;\,[x,y]\otimes z\,+\,{(-1)}^{|x||y|}y\otimes[x,z]

One can show that this action commutes with the action of the elementary transposition, whence with the operators
\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{n}}
and
\operatorname{Alt}_{n}\,
.

A
Lie supercoalgebra
is a pair
\,(\mathfrak{g}\,,\delta\,)
where
\,\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{g}_{{\bar{0}}}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{\bar{1}}\,
is a super vector space and
\delta:\mathfrak{g}\longrightarrow\Lambda^{2}(\mathfrak{g})\,
is a morphism of super vector spaces, called a
super (Lie) cobracket
, that satisfies the
super co-Jacobi
identity, namely

\operatorname{Alt}_{3}\circ\,\big((\operatorname{id}\otimes\delta)\circ\delta\big)\;=\;0\!\!\qquad\text{which is equivalent to}\qquad\!\!\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\circ\,\big((\operatorname{id}\otimes\delta)\circ\delta\big)\;=\;0

Equivalently again, this amounts to be the same as requiring that the pair
\,\big(\mathfrak{g}^{*},\delta^{*}\big)\,
be a Lie superalgebra. Indeed, let
\,\langle-\,,-\rangle:\mathfrak{g}^{*}\times\mathfrak{g}\longrightarrow\Bbbk\,
be the canonical evaluation map. If we identify
\,\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{*}\,
with a subspace of
\,{\big(\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\big)}^{*}\,
via

\big\langle\alpha\otimes\beta,x\otimes y\big\rangle\;=\;{(-1)}^{|\beta||x|}\langle\alpha,x\rangle\langle\beta,y\rangle

for all (homogeneous)
\,x,y\in\mathfrak{g}\,
,
\,\alpha,\beta\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}\,
, we have
\,\big\langle\alpha\otimes\beta,\delta(x)\big\rangle=\big\langle\delta^{*}(\alpha\otimes\beta),x\big\rangle\,
. Then, the image of
\delta
is contained in
\Lambda^{2}(\mathfrak{g})
if and only if
\,(\operatorname{id}+\sigma)\circ\delta=0\,
if and only if
\,\delta^{*}\circ(\operatorname{id}+\sigma)=0\,
if and only if
\delta^{*}
satisfies the super
antisymmetry condition. Moreover,
\delta
satisfies the super co-Jacobi if and only if
\delta^{*}
satisfies the super Jacobi.

To express the equations above on elements, it is better to consider
\Lambda^{2}(\mathfrak{g})
as the subspace of
\,V\otimes V\,
given by the image of
\operatorname{Alt}_{2}\,
. Then, for all (homogeneous)
\,x,y\in\mathfrak{g}\,
,  we set

x\wedge y\,\;:=\;\,2^{-1}\operatorname{Alt}_{2}(x\otimes y)\,=\,2^{-1}\big(x\otimes y-{(-1)}^{|x||y|}y\otimes x\big)

Finally, as a matter of notation, all along the paper we shall use a Sweedler’s-like notation
\,\delta(x)=x_{[1]}\wedge x_{[2]}\,
(summation being understood) for any
\,x\in\mathfrak{g}\,
.

A
Lie superbialgebra
is a triple
\,\big(\,\mathfrak{g}\,,[\,\ ,\ ]\,,\,\delta\,\big)\,
where

\,\big(\,\mathfrak{g}\,,[\,\ ,\ ]\,\big)\,
is a Lie superalgebra,
\,(\,\mathfrak{g}\,,\delta\,)\,
is a Lie supercoalgebra, and
\delta
is a
1
–cocycle for the Lie superalgebra cohomology, cf.
[
Le
]
. This is a compatibility condition that, for any homogeneous
\,x,y\in\mathfrak{g}\,
,  reads as follows

\delta\big([x,y]\big)\;=\;x\,.\,\delta(y)-{(-1)}^{|x||y|}y\,.\,\delta(x)

where
\,x\,.\,\delta(y)\,
denotes the action of
\,\mathfrak{g}\,
onto
\Lambda^{2}(\mathfrak{g})\,
induced by the adjoint action on
\,\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\,
as in (
3.2
). Taking this into account, the compatibility condition above reads

\displaystyle\delta\big([x,y]\big)

\displaystyle\;=\;\big[x,y_{[1]}\big]\wedge y_{[2]}\,+\,{(-1)}^{|x||y_{1}|}y_{[1]}\wedge\big[x,y_{[2]}\big]\,-

\displaystyle\quad-\,{(-1)}^{|x||y|}\big[y,x_{[1]}\big]\wedge x_{[2]}\,-\,{(-1)}^{|x_{2}||y|}x_{[1]}\wedge\big[y,x_{[2]}\big]

If we write the action above by
\,x\,.\,(y\otimes z)=\big[\Delta(x),y\otimes z\big]\,
,  we have

\delta\big([x,y]\big)\;=\;\big[\Delta(x),\delta(y)\big]\,-\,{(-1)}^{|x||y|}\big[\Delta(y),\delta(x)\big]\;=\;\big[\Delta(x),\delta(y)\big]\,+\,\big[\delta(x),\Delta(y)\big]

So, the element-free formulation of the compatibility condition reads

\delta\circ[\,\ ,\ ]\;=\;[\,\ ,\ ]\circ\big(\Delta\otimes\delta\big)\circ\operatorname{Alt}_{2}\qquad\text{ inside }\operatorname{End}(\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g})

When
\,\big(\,\mathfrak{g}\,,[\,\ ,\ ]\,,\delta\,\big)\,
is a Lie superbialgebra, the same holds for
\,\big(\,\mathfrak{g}^{*},\delta^{*},{[\,\ ,\ ]}^{*}\,\big)\,
— up to topological technicalities, if
\mathfrak{g}
is infinite-dimensional — which is called the
dual
Lie superbialgebra to
\,\big(\,\mathfrak{g}\,,[\,\ ,\ ]\,,\delta\,\big)\,
. We simply write
\mathfrak{g}
for a Lie superbialgebra if the structure maps are clear from the context.

As claimed before, the super Lie cobracket in a Lie superbialgebra satisfies a
1
–cocycle condition. Thus, as in the non-super case, one may define super cobrackets by taking a
1
–cochain: one then says that
\,\big(\,\mathfrak{g}\,,[\,\ ,\ ]\,,\delta\,\big)\,
is a
coboundary
Lie superbialgebra if there exists
\,c\in\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\,
such that
\,\delta=\partial{}c\,
,  that is, for all (homogeneous)
\,x\in\mathfrak{g}\,
one has

\delta(x)\;=\;(\partial{}c)(x)\;=\;x\,.\,c\;=\;[x,c_{1}]\otimes c_{2}+{(-1)}^{|x||c_{1}|}c_{1}\otimes[x,c_{2}]

Dually,
\,\big(\,\mathfrak{g}\,,[\,\ ,\ ]\,,\delta\,\big)\,
is a
boundary
Lie superbialgebra if there exists
\,\chi\in{(\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g})}^{*}\,
such that
\,[\,\ ,\ ]=\partial_{*}{}\chi\,
,  that is, for all (homogeneous)
\,x,y\in\mathfrak{g}\,
,  one has

[x,y]\;=\;(\partial_{*}{}\chi)(x\otimes y)\;=\;x_{[1]}\,\chi(x_{[2]},y)\,+\,{(-1)}^{|x||y_{[1]}|}\,y_{[1]}\,\chi(x,y_{[2]})

where
\,\partial_{*}\,
is the coboundary map for the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}^{*}\,
.

Finally, given
\,r=r_{1}\otimes r_{2}\,
(summation understood) and
\,s=s_{1}\otimes s_{2}\,
in
\,\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\,
,  set

[\![\,r\,,s\,]\!]\,:=\,{(-1)}^{|s_{1}||r_{2}|}\big[\,r_{1}\,,s_{1}\big]\otimes r_{2}\otimes s_{2}+r_{1}\otimes\big[\,r_{2}\,,s_{1}\big]\otimes s_{2}+{(-1)}^{|s_{1}||r_{2}|}\,r_{1}\otimes s_{1}\otimes\big[\,r_{2}\,,s_{2}\big]

which in compact form reads

[\![\,r\,,s\,]\!]\,:=\,(-1)^{|s_{1}||r_{2}|}\,[\,r_{1,2}\,,s_{1,3}\,]\,+\,[\,r_{1,2}\,,s_{2,3}\,]\,+\,{(-1)}^{|s_{1}||r_{2}|}\,[\,r_{1,3}\,,s_{2,3}\,]

The following proposition is a
super
–version of
[
Mj
, Proposition 8.1.3]
:

Proposition 3.1.2
.

(a)  Let
\,\big(\,\mathfrak{g}\,,[\,\ ,\ ]\,\big)\,
be a Lie superalgebra and
\,c\in\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\,
. Then
\,\big(\mathfrak{g}\,,[\,\ ,\ ]\,,\partial c\,\big)\,
is a Lie superbialgebra if and only if
for all
\,x\in\mathfrak{g}\,
one has

x\,.\,\big((\operatorname{id}+\sigma)(c)\big)\,=\,0\qquad\text{ and }\qquad x\,.\,[\![\,c\,,c\,]\!]\,=\,0

(b)  Let
\,(\,\mathfrak{g}\,,\delta\,)\,
be a Lie supercoalgebra and

\,\chi\in{(\,\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g})}^{*}\,
. Then
\,(\,\mathfrak{g}\,,\partial_{*}\chi\,,\delta\,)\,
is a Lie superbialgebra if and only if for all
\,\alpha\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}\,
one has

\alpha\,.\,\big(\chi(\operatorname{id}+\sigma)\big)\,=\,0\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\alpha\,.\,{[\![\,\chi\,,\chi\,]\!]}_{*}\,=\,0

where
\,{[\![\,\alpha\,,\beta\,]\!]}_{*}\,
stands for the corresponding object defined above in
\,{(\,\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\,)}^{*}\,
associated with any pair
\,\alpha,\beta\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}\,
.

Proof.

We prove only item
(a)
, and we leave instead
(b)
to the reader as it follows quite directly from duality.

The first equality is the one that guarantees that the image of
\partial c

is in
\,\Lambda^{2}(\mathfrak{g})\,
:  for any
\,x\in\mathfrak{g}\,
we have

(\operatorname{id}+\sigma)\big((\partial c)(x)\big)\,=\,\operatorname{Sym}_{2}(x.c)\,=\,x.\operatorname{Sym}_{2}(c)\,=\,x.\big((\operatorname{id}+\sigma)(c)\big)

Hence,
\,(\partial c)(x)\in\operatorname{Ker}(\operatorname{id}+\sigma)=\Lambda^{2}(\mathfrak{g})\,
if and
only if
\,x\,.\,\big((\operatorname{id}+\sigma)(c)\big)=0\,
.

To check the super co-Jacobi identity, we proceed as follows. Write
\,c=c^{+}+c^{-}\,
with
\,c^{+}\in S^{2}(\mathfrak{g})\,
being the symmetric part of
c
and
\,c^{-}\in\Lambda^{2}(\mathfrak{g})\,
the antisymmetric part. Then
one has that
\,(\partial c)(x)=(\partial c^{-})(x)\,
for all
\,x\in\mathfrak{g}\,
. Indeed, as
\,\operatorname{Sym}_{2}(c^{+})=2\,c^{+}\,
and
\,\operatorname{Sym}_{2}(c^{-})=0\,
,  the equality above tells us that
\,x\,.\,c^{+}=0\,
. Thus we have that

\,(\partial c)(x)=x\,.\,c=x\,.\,c^{-}=(\partial c^{-})(x)\,
. Now we show that

\big(\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\circ\,\big(\operatorname{id}\otimes(\partial c)\big)\circ(\partial c)\big)(x)\,\;=\;\,x\,.\,[\![\,c^{-},c^{-}\,]\!]

for all
\,x\in\mathfrak{g}\,
;  the claim then will follow from this.

Take
\,x\in\mathfrak{g}\,
. We begin by checking the following:

\displaystyle\big(\big(\operatorname{id}\otimes(\partial c^{-})\big)\circ(\partial c^{-})\big)(x)\;=\;\big(\operatorname{id}\otimes(\partial c^{-})\big)(x\,.\,c^{-})\;=

\displaystyle\qquad=\;\big(\operatorname{id}\otimes(\partial c^{-})\big)\Big([\,x\,,c^{-}_{1}]\otimes c^{-}_{2}\,+\,{(-1)}^{|x||c^{-}_{1}|}\,c^{-}_{1}\otimes[\,x\,,c^{-}_{2}]\Big)\;=

\displaystyle\qquad=\;[\,x\,,c^{-}_{1}]\otimes(c^{-}_{2}.\,c^{-})+{(-1)}^{|x||c^{-}_{1}|}\,c^{-}_{1}\otimes\big(\,[\,x\,,c^{-}_{2}]\,.\,c^{-}\big)\;=

\displaystyle\qquad=\;[\,x\,,c^{-}_{1}]\otimes\big[\,c^{-}_{2},{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\big]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\,+\,{(-1)}^{|c^{-}_{2}||{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}|}[\,x\,,c^{-}_{1}]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\otimes\big[\,c^{-}_{2},{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\big]\,+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\,{(-1)}^{|x||c^{-}_{1}|}\,c^{-}_{1}\otimes\big[[\,x\,,c^{-}_{2}]\,,{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\big]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\,+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\,{(-1)}^{|x||c^{-}_{1}|+|x||{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}|+|c^{-}_{2}||{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}|}\,c^{-}_{1}\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\otimes\big[[\,x\,,c^{-}_{2}]\,,{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\big]

Then we get

\hfil\displaystyle\big(\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\circ\,\big(\operatorname{id}\otimes(\partial c^{-})\big)\circ(\partial c^{-})\big)(x)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad=\;\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\Big(\,[\,x\,,c^{-}_{1}]\otimes\big[\,c^{-}_{2},{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\big]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\,+\,{(-1)}^{|c^{-}_{2}||{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}|}\,[\,x\,,c^{-}_{1}]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\otimes\big[\,c^{-}_{2},{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\big]\,+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\,{(-1)}^{|x||c^{-}_{1}|}\,c^{-}_{1}\otimes\big[[\,x\,,c^{-}_{2}]\,,{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\big]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\,+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad+\,{(-1)}^{|x||c^{-}_{1}|+|x||{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}|+|c^{-}_{2}||{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}|}\,c^{-}_{1}\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\otimes\big[[\,x\,,c^{-}_{2}]\,,{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\big]\,\Big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad=\;\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\Big(\,[\,x\,,c^{-}_{1}]\otimes\big[\,c^{-}_{2},{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\big]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\,+\,{(-1)}^{|c^{-}_{2}||{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}|}\,[\,x\,,c^{-}_{1}]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\otimes\big[\,c^{-}_{2},{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\big]\,+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\;\;+{(-1)}^{|x||c^{-}_{1}|+|c^{-}_{1}|(|x|+|c^{-}_{2}|+|{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}|+|{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}|)}\,\big[[\,x\,,c^{-}_{2}]\,,{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\big]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\otimes c^{-}_{1}\,+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\;\;\;\;+{(-1)}^{|x|(|c^{-}_{1}|+|{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}|)+|c^{-}_{2}||{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}|+(|x|+|c^{-}_{2}|+|{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}|)(|{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}|+|c^{-}_{1}|)}\,\big[[\,x\,,c^{-}_{2}]\,,{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\big]\otimes c^{-}_{1}\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\,\Big)\,=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad=\;\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\Big(\,[\,x\,,c^{-}_{1}]\otimes\big[\,c^{-}_{2},{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\big]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\,+\,{(-1)}^{|c^{-}_{2}||{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}|}\,[\,x\,,c^{-}_{1}]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\otimes\big[\,c^{-}_{2},{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\big]\,+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\,{(-1)}^{|c^{-}_{1}||c^{-}_{2}|}\,\big[[\,x\,,c^{-}_{2}]\,,{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\big]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\otimes c^{-}_{1}\,+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad+\,{(-1)}^{|{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}||{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}|+|c^{-}_{2}||c^{-}_{1}|+|{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}||c^{-}_{1}|}\,\big[[\,x\,,c^{-}_{2}]\,,{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\big]\otimes c^{-}_{1}\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\,\Big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad=\;\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\Big(\,[\,x\,,c^{-}_{1}]\otimes\big[\,c^{-}_{2},{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\big]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\,+\,{(-1)}^{|c^{-}_{2}||{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}|}\,[\,x\,,c^{-}_{1}]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\otimes\big[\,c^{-}_{2},{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\big]\,+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\,{(-1)}^{||c^{-}_{1}||c^{-}_{2}|}\,\big[\,x\,,\big[\,c^{-}_{2},{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\big]\big]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\otimes c^{-}_{1}\,-\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad-\,{(-1)}^{|c^{-}_{1}||c^{-}_{2}|+|x||c^{-}_{2}|}\,\big[\,c^{-}_{2},\big[\,x\,,{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\big]\big]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\otimes c^{-}_{1}\,+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\,{(-1)}^{|x||{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}|+|{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}||{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}|}\,\big[\,{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2},[\,x\,,c^{-}_{1}]\big]\otimes c^{-}_{2}\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\,\Big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad=\;\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\Big(\,[\,x\,,c^{-}_{1}]\otimes\big[\,c^{-}_{2},{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\big]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\,+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad+\,{(-1)}^{|c^{-}_{2}||{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}|}\,[\,x\,,c^{-}_{1}]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\otimes\big[\,c^{-}_{2},{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\big]\,+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\,{(-1)}^{|c^{-}_{1}||c^{-}_{2}|}\,\big[\,x\,,\big[\,c^{-}_{2},{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\big]\big]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\otimes c^{-}_{1}\,\Big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad=\;\Big(\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\circ\,\big(\,[\,x\,,-\,]\otimes\operatorname{id}\otimes\operatorname{id}\big)\Big)\big(\,c^{-}_{1}\otimes\big[\,c^{-}_{2},{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\big]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\,+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\,{(-1)}^{|c^{-}_{2}||{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}|}\,c^{-}_{1}\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\otimes\big[\,c^{-}_{2},{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\big]\,+\,{(-1)}^{|c^{-}_{1}||{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}|}\,\big[\,{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1},c^{-}_{1}\big]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\otimes c^{-}_{2}\,\big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad=\;\Big(\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\circ\,\big(\,[\,x\,,-\,]\otimes\operatorname{id}\otimes\operatorname{id}\big)\Big)\big(\,{(-1)}^{|c^{-}_{1}||{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}|}\,\big[\,{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1},c^{-}_{1}\big]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\otimes c^{-}_{2}\,\big)\,+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\,c^{-}_{1}\otimes\big[\,c^{-}_{2},{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\big]\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\,+\,{(-1)}^{|c^{-}_{2}||{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}|}\,c^{-}_{1}\otimes{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{1}\otimes\big[\,c^{-}_{2},{c^{\prime}}^{-}_{2}\big]\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\hskip 101.0pt=\;\Big(\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\circ\,\big(\,[\,x\,,-\,]\otimes\operatorname{id}\otimes\operatorname{id}\big)\Big)\big([\![\,c^{-},c^{-}\,]\!]\big)\;=\;x\,.\,[\![\,c^{-},c^{-}\,]\!]

Note that, although it is written in a compact form, the last equality involves a long computation where a lot of signs are involved.

Finally, the cocycle condition follows from the fact that
\partial c
is a
coboundary. Indeed, by using the co-Jacobi identity, for all
\,x,y\in\mathfrak{g}\,
we have

\hfil\displaystyle(\partial c)\big([x,y]\big)\;=\;[x,y]\,.\,c\;=\;\big[[x,y]\,,c_{1}\big]\otimes c_{2}\,+\,{(-1)}^{(|x|+|y|)|c_{1}|}\,c_{1}\otimes\big[[x,y]\,,c_{2}\big]\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad=\;\big[x,[y,c_{1}]\big]\otimes c_{2}\,-\,{(-1)}^{|x||y|}\,\big[y,[x,c_{1}]\big]\otimes c_{2}\;+\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill+\;{(-1)}^{(|x|+|y|)|c_{1}|}\,c_{1}\otimes\big[x,[y,c_{2}]\big]\,-\,{(-1)}^{(|x|+|y|)|c_{1}|+|x||y|}\,c_{1}\otimes\big[y,[x,c_{2}]\big]\;=\quad\qquad

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;x\,.\big((\partial c)(y)\big)\,-\,{(-1)}^{|x||y|}\,y\,.\big((\partial c)(x)\big)\quad\qed

3.1.3
.

Deformations of Lie superbialgebras.

To the best of the our knowledge, there is a lack of reference and bibliography about
a general theory of deformations for Lie superbialgebras. The reason may lie in the fact that
Lie superbialgebras may be related to Lie bialgebras through the process of bosonization. Moreover,
the theory of deformations of Lie bialgebras can be considered as a sub-theory of that of Lie algebras. For
the latter, we refer to

[
CG
]
,
[
MW
]
, and references therein.
In fact, as in the present work we are mainly interested in two special kinds of deformations performed by

even
objects,
where either the Lie cobracket or the Lie bracket alone is deformed, leaving the “other side” of the overall structure untouched,
we rely heavily on definitions and basic results on Lie bialgebra cohomology.

We study first a deformation of the super cobracket. Let
(\mathfrak{g},[\,\ ,\ ],\,\delta)

be a Lie superbialgebra. A homogeneous even element
\,c\in\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\,
is
called a
twist
if

x\,.\,\big((\operatorname{id}+\sigma)(c)\big)\;=\;0\quad\text{ and }\quad x\,.\,\big(\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}(\operatorname{id}\otimes\delta)(c)-[\![\,c\,,c\,]\!]\,\big)\;=\;0

(3.3)

for all
\,x\in\mathfrak{g}\,
;  here the (adjoint) action of
\,x\in\mathfrak{g}\,
is onto
\,\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\,
— cf. the action onto
\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\,
above. As a side note, observe that this definition coincides with that of twist for Lie bialgebras, but with respect to a different symmetric braiding.

Proposition 3.1.4
.

Let
\,\big(\,\mathfrak{g}\,,[\,\ ,\ ]\,,\,\delta\,\big)\,
be a Lie superbialgebra and
\,c\in\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\,
a twist. Then the map

\,\delta^{\,c}:\mathfrak{g}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\mathfrak{g}\wedge\mathfrak{g}\,
defined by

\delta^{\,c}(x)\;:=\;\delta(x)-(\partial c)(x)\;=\;\delta(x)-x\,.\,c\qquad\forall\;\;x\in\mathfrak{g}

(3.4)

is a new super cobracket on the Lie superalgebra
\,\big(\,\mathfrak{g}\,;\,[\,\ ,\,\ ]\,\big)\,
making
\,\big(\,\mathfrak{g}\,;\,[\,\ ,\ ]\,,\,\delta^{c}\,\big)\,
into a new Lie superbialgebra.

Proof.

The proof follows
[
Mj
, Theorem 8.1.7]
, mutatis mutandis, taking into account the sign changes.

As the image of
\delta
lies in
\Lambda^{2}(\mathfrak{g})\,
, the first condition in (
3.3
) (which is the same as the first in Proposition
3.1.2
) ensures that also the image of
\delta^{c}
is contained in
\Lambda^{2}(\mathfrak{g})\,
.

In order to prove that
\delta^{c}
is a Lie super-cobracket, we have to check the identity
\,\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\!\big((\operatorname{id}\otimes\delta^{c})\circ\delta^{c}\,\big)(x)=0\,
for all
\,x\in\mathfrak{g}\,
. But
\,\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\!\big((\operatorname{id}\otimes\delta)\circ\delta\,\big)(x)=0\,
,  hence

\displaystyle(\operatorname{id}\otimes\delta^{c})\circ\delta^{c}

\displaystyle\;=\;\big(\operatorname{id}\otimes(\delta-\partial c)\big)\circ(\delta-\partial c)\;=

\displaystyle=\;(\operatorname{id}\otimes\delta\,)\circ\delta\,-\,(\operatorname{id}\otimes\delta\,)\circ(\partial c)\,-\,(\operatorname{id}\otimes\partial c)\circ\delta\,+\,(\operatorname{id}\otimes\partial c)\circ(\partial c)

and we have

\,\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\!\big((\operatorname{id}\otimes\partial c)\circ(\partial c)\big)(x)=x.[\![\,c\,,c\,]\!]\,
,
by the proof of Proposition
3.1.2
:
thus it is enough to prove that

\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\!\big((\operatorname{id}\otimes\delta)\circ(\partial c)+(\operatorname{id}\otimes\partial c)\circ\delta\,\big)(x)\;=\;x\,.\,\big(\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}(\operatorname{id}\otimes\delta)(c)\big)

for all
\,x\in\mathfrak{g}\,
. Set
\,\delta(x)=x_{[1]}\otimes x_{[2]}\in\Lambda^{2}(\mathfrak{g})\,
:  then by the cocycle condition we have

\displaystyle\big((\operatorname{id}\otimes\delta)\circ(\partial c)+(\operatorname{id}\otimes\partial c)\circ\delta\,\big)(x)\;=

\displaystyle\qquad=\;[x,c_{1}]\otimes\delta(c_{2})+(-1)^{|x||c_{1}|}c_{1}\otimes\delta([x,c_{2}])+x_{[1]}\otimes[x_{[2]},c_{1}]\otimes c_{2}\,+

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+(-1)^{|x_{[2]}||c_{1}|}x_{[1]}\otimes c_{1}\otimes[x_{[2]},c_{2}]\;=

\displaystyle\qquad=\;[x,c_{1}]\otimes\delta(c_{2})+(-1)^{|x||c_{1}|}c_{1}\otimes\big(x\,.\,\delta(c_{2})\big)-c_{1}\otimes\big(c_{2}\,.\,\delta(x)\big)\,+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+x_{[1]}\otimes[x_{[2]},c_{1}]\otimes c_{2}+(-1)^{|x_{[2]}||c_{1}|}x_{[1]}\otimes c_{1}\otimes[x_{[2]},c_{2}]\;=

\displaystyle\qquad=\;x\,.\,\big(c_{1}\otimes\delta(c_{2})\big)-c_{1}\otimes\big(c_{2}\,.\,\delta(x)\big)+x_{[1]}\otimes[x_{[2]},c_{1}]\otimes c_{2}\,+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+(-1)^{|x_{[2]}||c_{1}|}x_{[1]}\otimes c_{1}\otimes[x_{[2]},c_{2}]\;=

\displaystyle\qquad=\;x\,.\,\big((\operatorname{id}\otimes\delta)(c)\big)-c_{1}\otimes\big(c_{2}\,.\,\delta(x)\big)+x_{[1]}\otimes[x_{[2]},c_{1}]\otimes c_{2}\,+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+(-1)^{|x_{[2]}||c_{1}|}x_{[1]}\otimes c_{1}\otimes[x_{[2]},c_{2}]

Since
\,\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\!\big(x\,.\,\big((\operatorname{id}\otimes\delta)(c)\big)\big)=x\,.\,\big(\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}(\operatorname{id}\otimes\delta)(c)\big)\,
,  to finish this calculation it is enough to prove that

\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}\!\big(-c_{1}\otimes(c_{2}\,.\,\delta(x))+x_{[1]}\otimes[x_{[2]},c_{1}]\otimes c_{2}+(-1)^{|x_{[2]}||c_{1}|}x_{[1]}\otimes c_{1}\otimes[x_{[2]},c_{2}]\big)\;=\;0

which follows directly from a straightforward computation.
To finish the proof we just note that the super cobracket
\delta^{c}=\delta-\partial c

satisfies the cocycle condition since it is a difference of two functions that they do,

\delta
because it is a super cobracket for a Lie superbialgebra and
\partial c

because it is a coboundary.
∎

Definition 3.1.5
.

Let
(\mathfrak{g},[\,\ ,\ ],\,\delta)
be a Lie superbialgebra and

\,c\in\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\,
a twist. The associated Lie superbialgebra

\;\mathfrak{g}^{\,c}\,:=\,\big(\,\mathfrak{g}\,;\,[\,\ ,\,\ ]\,,\,\delta^{\,c}\,\big)\;

is called a
deformation by twist

(or “
twist deformation
”) of the original Lie superbialgebra

\mathfrak{g}\,
.
\diamondsuit

We introduce now the dual recipe of
deformation by
2
–cocycle
,
where one deforms the Lie superbracket while keeping the Lie supercobracket untouched.
Let again
\big(\,\mathfrak{g}\,;\,[\,\ ,\,\ ]\,,\,\delta\,\big)\,
be a Lie superbialgebra.

A homogeneous even element
\,\chi\in{(\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g})}^{*}\,
is called a
2
–cocycle
if

\alpha\,.\,\big(\,\chi\circ(\operatorname{id}+\,\sigma)\big)\,=\,0\quad\text{and}\quad\alpha\,.\,\big(\,\chi\circ\big(\operatorname{id}\otimes[\,\ ,\,\ ]\big)\circ\operatorname{Sym}_{\mathbb{A}_{3}}-\,[\![\,\chi\,,\chi\,]\!]_{*}\,\big)\;=\;0\;\;

(3.5)

for all
\,\alpha\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}\,
. The dual version of
Proposition
3.1.4
reads as follows.

Proposition 3.1.6
.

Let
\,(\mathfrak{g},[\,\ ,\ ],\,\delta)\,
be a Lie superbialgebra and

\,\chi\in(\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g})^{*}\,
a
2
–cocycle. Then the map

\,[\,\ ,\ ]_{\chi}:\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\mathfrak{g}\,

defined for all
\,x,y\in\mathfrak{g}\,
by

[x,y]_{\chi}:=\,[x,y]-(\partial_{*}\chi)(x\otimes y)\,=\,[x,y]-x_{[1]}\,\chi(x_{[2]},y)\,-\,{(-1)}^{|x||y_{[1]}|}\,y_{[1]}\,\chi(x,y_{[2]})

(3.6)

is a new Lie superbracket on the Lie supercoalgebra

\big(\,\mathfrak{g}\,;\,\delta\,\big)\,
making

\,\big(\,\mathfrak{g}\,;\,[\,\ ,\ ]_{\chi}\,,\,\delta\,\big)\,
into a (new) Lie superbialgebra.
∎

The proof is a sheer dualization of that for Proposition
3.1.4
,
hence is left to the reader. The relevant terminology is fixed as follows:

Definition 3.1.7
.

Let
\,(\mathfrak{g},[\,\ ,\ ],\,\delta)\,
be a Lie superbialgebra and

\,\chi\in(\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g})^{*}\,
be a
2
–cocycle for it.
Then the Lie superbialgebra
\,\mathfrak{g}_{\chi}\,:=\,\big(\,\mathfrak{g}\,;\,{[\,\ ,\ ]}_{\chi}\,,\,\delta\,\big)\,

is called a
deformation by 2–cocycle
(or “
2–cocycle deformation
”)

of the Lie superbialgebra
\mathfrak{g}\,
.
\diamondsuit

At last, we point out that the two notions of “twist” and of
“
2
–cocycle” for Lie superbialgebras,
as well as the associated deformations, are so devised as to be dual to each other.
The following result then holds, whose proof is left to the reader:

Proposition 3.1.8
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a Lie superbialgebra,
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
the dual Lie superbialgebra.

(a)  Let
c
be a twist for
\mathfrak{g}\,
, and
\chi_{c}
the image of

c
in
{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{*}\big)}^{*}
