for the natural composed embedding

\,\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{**}\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{**}\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{*}\big)}^{*}\,
.
Then
\chi_{c}
is a 2–cocycle for
\mathfrak{g}^{*}\,
, and there exists a canonical isomorphism

\,{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{*}\big)}_{\chi_{c}}\cong{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{c}\big)}^{*}\,
.

(b)  Let
\chi
be a 2–cocycle for
\mathfrak{g}\,
; assume that

\mathfrak{g}
is finite-dimensional, and let
c_{\,\chi}
be the image of
\,\chi

in the natural identification
\,{(\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g})}^{*}=\mathfrak{g}^{*}\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{*}\,
.
Then
c_{\,\chi}
is a twist for
\mathfrak{g}^{*}\,
,
and there exists a canonical isomorphism

\,{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{*}\big)}^{c_{\,\chi}}\cong{\big(\mathfrak{g}_{\chi}\big)}^{*}\,
.
∎

3.2.
Multiparameter Lie superbialgebras (=MpLSbA’s)

Let
\,(A\,,p)\,
be a fixed Cartan super-datum (cf. Definition
2.1.2
): we fix a matrix of Cartan type
\,P:={\big(\,p_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\in M_{n}(\Bbbk)\,
associated with
A\,
, i.e.
\,P_{s}:=2^{-1}\big(P+P^{\,\scriptscriptstyle T}\big)=DA=:P_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle A}}\,
, and we let
\,{\mathcal{R}}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,
be a realization of
P
.

With these data, we are going to associate some Lie superbialgebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\,
,
to be called “multiparameter Lie superbialgebra” (or MpLSbA’s in short): these will depend explicitly on the parameters in
P

(i.e. its entries), but also on the chosen realization
{\mathcal{R}}\,
.
Up to technicalities, these MpLSbA’s arise as deformations by twist of Lie superbialgebras of a special family,
which comes from quantum counterparts introduced by Yamane — hence we call them “of Yamane’s type”.

3.2.1
.

Yamane’s Lie superbialgebras.
In the present subsection, we refer to the notions in Definition
2.1.3

and all what follows in §
2
,
but

working now with
\Bbbk
as ground ring instead of
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
; then, since we are dealing with
\Bbbk
rather than
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
,
we identify the space
\mathfrak{h}
with
\overline{\mathfrak{h}}\,
, the roots
\alpha_{j}
with
\overline{\alpha}_{j}\,
,  etc.
(cf. §
2.1.3
). Following §
2.1
,
let
\,(A\,,p)\,
be a Cartan super-datum, let
\,P_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle A}}=DA\,
be the symmetrization of
A\,
,
and fix a realization
\,{\mathcal{R}}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,
of
P_{\!\scriptscriptstyle A}\,

which is
split
,
straight
and
minimal
.

We define the Lie superalgebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}
as being the one generated over

\Bbbk
by the
\Bbbk
–subspace
\mathfrak{h}
together with elements
E_{i}

and
F_{i}
(
\,i\in I\,
), whose parity is fixed by

\,|T|:={\bar{0}}\,
and
\,\big|E_{i}\big|:=p(i)=:\big|F_{i}\big|\,

(for all
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
and
\,i\in I\,
),
with relations as follows (for all
\;T\,,T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime}\in\mathfrak{h}\,
,

\;i,j,\ell,k\in I\,
,
\;i\neq\ell\,
):

\hfil\displaystyle\big[\,T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime}\,\big]\,=\,0\;\;,\qquad\big[\,T,E_{j}\,\big]\,-\,\alpha_{j}(\,T)\,E_{j}\,=\,0\;\;,\qquad\big[\,T,F_{j}\,\big]\,+\,\alpha_{j}(\,T)\,F_{j}\,=\,0

\hfil\displaystyle\big[\,E_{i}\,,F_{\ell}\,\big]\,=\,\delta_{i\ell}\,{{\;T_{i}^{+}\!+T_{i}^{-}\;}\over{\;2\,d_{i}\;}}\;\;,\;\qquad\big[E_{i}\,,E_{j}\big]\,=\,0\;\;,\quad\big[F_{i}\,,F_{j}\big]\,=\,0\!\qquad\text{if}\quad a_{i,j}=0

\hfil\displaystyle{\big(\operatorname{ad}\,(E_{i})\big)}^{1-a_{ij}}(E_{j})\,=\,0\;\;,\;\quad{\big(\operatorname{ad}\,(F_{i})\big)}^{1-a_{ij}}(F_{j})\,=\,0\qquad\text{with \ }p(i)={\bar{0}}\;,\;\;i\not=j

\hfil\displaystyle\text{and then also all of the following, with $\,X\in\{E\,,F\}\,$:}_{\phantom{|}}^{\phantom{\big|}}\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\Big[\big[[X_{i}\,,X_{j}]\,,X_{k}\big]\,,X_{j}\Big]\,=\,0\quad\text{in any of the following cases:}

i
j
k

i
j
k

i
j
k

\hfil\displaystyle\Big[\big[\big[X_{n-1}\,,X_{n}\big]\,,X_{n}\big]\,,X_{n}\Big]^{\phantom{\Big|}}=\;0

n\!-\!1
n
with
and
A
of type
B_{n}

\hfil\displaystyle\Big[\big[X_{n-2}\,,X_{n-1}\big]\,,X_{n}\Big]\;-\;\Big[\big[X_{n-2}\,,X_{n}\big]\,,X_{n-1}\Big]^{\phantom{\Big|}}\;=\;\,0

n
n\!-\!2
n\!-\!1
with
and
A
of type
\,D_{n}(I\!I)

\hfil\displaystyle\bigg[\Big[\big[\,[X_{n-2}\,,X_{n-1}]\,,X_{n}\big]\,,[X_{n-2},X_{n-1}]\Big]\,,\,X_{n-1}\,\bigg]\;=\;\,0

for

n\!-\!2
n\!-\!1
n

and
\,A\,
of type
\,C_{n}

\hfil\displaystyle\Bigg[\Bigg[\bigg[\Big[\big[\,[X_{n-3}\,,\,X_{n-2}]\,,\,X_{n-1}\big]\,,X_{n}\,\Big]\,,\,X_{n-1}\,\bigg]\,,X_{n-2}\,\Bigg]\,,\,X_{n-1}\,\Bigg]\,\;=\;\;0\quad

for

n\!-\!3
n\!-\!2
n\!-\!1
n

and
\,A\,
of type
\,C_{n}

This
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}
is nothing but the simple Lie superalgebra associated with

(A\,,p)\,
: its presentation given above comes from the presentation of a suitable
quantization of
U\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}\big)
— in the sense of §
4.1.2
—
introduced in
[
Ya1
]
— in a “quantum double version”, denoted in
[
loc. cit.
] by
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
.
Moreover
, out of that quantization

the Lie superalgebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}
inherits an additional Lie cobracket, which
makes it into a Lie superbialgebra
, given on generators by (for
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,

and
\,i\in I\,
)

\delta\big(\,T\big)\,=\,0\quad,\qquad\delta\big(E_{i}\big)\,=\,2\;T^{+}_{i}\!\wedge E_{i}\quad,\qquad\delta\big(F_{i}\big)\,=\,2\;T^{-}_{i}\!\wedge F_{i}

(3.7)

Let us now pick a realization
{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}
of
P_{\scriptscriptstyle A}
that is still
straight and minimal but not necessarily split; then we can still define a Lie superalgebra

\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle A}$}}}

with the same presentation as
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}
above (with a “Cartan subalgebra”

\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}
replacing
\mathfrak{h}\,
): indeed, the former will just be a quotient of the
latter. Moreover, the Lie cobracket of
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}
will clearly be pushed forward onto

\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle A}$}}}
,
so that the latter is indeed a Lie superbialgebra with the same description as

\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}\,
, but for changing
{\mathcal{R}}
into
{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}
. Finally, consider a realization

{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime}
of
P_{\scriptscriptstyle A}
that is still straight but not necessarily split nor minimal.
Then we can again define a Lie superalgebra

\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle A}$}}}
with the same presentation as

\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle A}$}}}
(with
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime\prime}
replacing
\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}\,
),
the former being an extension of the latter, i.e.
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle A}$}}}

is a Lie sub-superalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle A}$}}}\,
;
moreover, the Lie cobracket of
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle A}$}}}

uniquely extends to all of

\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle A}$}}}
,
so the latter is also a Lie super
bi
algebra with Lie cobracket as in
(
3.7
) again.

In the following, we will refer to any of the Lie superalgebras
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}

just described — for every straight realization
{\mathcal{R}}
of
P_{\scriptscriptstyle A}
—
as
“Yamane’s Lie superbialgebras”
.

3.2.2
.

Definition of multiparameter Lie superbialgebras.
Let
\,(A\,,p)\,
be a fixed
Cartan super-datum (as in Definition
2.1.2
), and let

\,P:={\big(\,p_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\in M_{n}(\Bbbk)\,

be a matrix of Cartan type associated with

A\,
, i.e.
\,P_{s}:=2^{-1}\big(P+P^{\,\scriptscriptstyle T}\big)=DA=:P_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle A}}\,
.

With these assumptions, let
\,{\mathcal{R}}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,

be a realization of
P
.

We define the Lie
\Bbbk
–superalgebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
as the one having the very
same presentation as
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}
above,
but
with respect to the
realization
{\mathcal{R}}\,
. Then we prove that this
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
actually bears a
structure of Lie super
bi
algebra, through a series of steps.

First of all,
let
{\mathcal{R}}
be a straight realization of

\,P_{\scriptscriptstyle A}:=D\,A\,
;  then Yamane’s Lie superbialgebra

\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}
is defined (see §
3.2.1
above).

Second, for any antisymmetric matrix

\;\Theta=\big(\theta_{gk}\big)_{g,k\in\mathcal{G}}\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}(\Bbbk)\;

we consider

j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}\;:=\;{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\theta_{gk}\,H_{g}\otimes H_{k}\;\in\;\mathfrak{h}\otimes\mathfrak{h}\;\subseteq\;\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}

(3.8)

This is indeed a
twist
element
(cf. (
3.3
) and Definition
3.1.5
)
for Yamane’s Lie superbialgebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}\,
:  we call this
\,j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}\,

the toral twist
(or
“twist of toral type”
) associated with

\,\Theta\,
. Then out of
\,\Theta\,
we can consider the deformed Lie superalgebra

\,{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}\big)}^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}\,
— as in §
3.1.3

— the “deformed” multiparameter matrix

\;P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}=\,{\big(\,p^{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\,:=\,P_{\scriptscriptstyle A}-\mathfrak{A}\,\Theta\,\mathfrak{A}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle T}\;

as in (
2.2
) — again of Cartan type
A\,
, the same as
P
—
and its corresponding “deformed” straight realization

\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}\!:={\big\{T^{+}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},i}\,,T^{-}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},i}\big\}}_{i\in I}\,\big)\,
—
see Definition
2.2.3
. Accordingly, we write
\mathfrak{g}_{P_{\Theta}}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},p}
for the Lie superalgebra associated with the realization
{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}
of
P_{\Theta}\,
.

Third, we find the following, strict link between all these “deformed objects”:

Proposition 3.2.3
.

With assumptions as above, the following hold:

(a)  there exists a Lie superalgebra isomorphism

\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}:\mathfrak{g}_{P_{\Theta}}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},p}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow}\limits^{\cong}}}\,{\Big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}\Big)}^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}\;,\qquad E_{i}\,\mapsto\,E_{i}\,,\;T\,\mapsto\,T\,,\;F_{i}\,\mapsto\,F_{i}\quad(\;i\in I\,,\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,)

(b)  there exists a unique structure of Lie super
bi
algebra onto
\,\mathfrak{g}_{P_{\Theta}}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},p}\,
such that the map
\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}

in (a) above is an isomorphism of Lie super
bi
algebras;

(c)  the Lie cobracket of the Lie super
bi
algebra structure of
\,\mathfrak{g}_{P_{\Theta}}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},p}\,
mentioned in (b) above is given by the formulas
(for all
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
,
\,i\in I
)

\delta\big(\,T\big)\,=\,0\quad,\qquad\delta\big(E_{i}\big)\,=\,2\;T^{+}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},i}\wedge E_{i}\quad,\qquad\delta\big(F_{i}\big)\,=\,2\;T^{-}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},i}\wedge F_{i}

Proof.

(a)
By construction, the Lie algebra structure in
{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}\big)}^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}
is the same of
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}\,
,
and the latter only depends on the
\alpha_{j}
’s and the sums

\,S_{j}:=2^{-1}\big(T_{j}^{+}\!+T_{j}^{-}\big)\,
(
\,j\in I\,
).
Now, both the
\alpha_{j}
’s and the
S_{j}
’s
do not change

(by construction) when we pass from
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}
to
\mathfrak{g}_{P_{\Theta}}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},p}

or viceversa; therefore, the formulas in the claim — mapping each generator of
\mathfrak{g}_{P_{\Theta}}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},p}
onto the same name generator of

\,\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}={\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}\big)}^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}\,
—
actually do provide an isomorphism
\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
of Lie superalgebras,
as claimed.

(b)
By pull-back through
\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
one gets onto

\mathfrak{g}_{P_{\Theta}}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},p}
the unique Lie cobracket which makes the former
into a Lie superbialgebra such that the map
f^{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}

is also an isomorphism of Lie super
bi
algebras.

(c)
For the toral twist

\;j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}:={\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\theta_{gk}\,H_{g}\otimes H_{k}\;

in (
3.8
), formula (
3.4
) yields

\delta^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}(x)\;:=\;\delta(x)-x.j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}\;=\;\delta(x)-{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\theta_{gk}\,\big(\big[x,H_{g}\big]\otimes H_{k}+H_{g}\otimes\big[x,H_{k}\big]\big)

for all
\,x\in\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}\,
. Now take
\,x:=E_{\ell}\,
(
\,\ell\in I\,
):  then computations give

\hfil\displaystyle\delta^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}(E_{\ell})\;:=\;\delta(E_{\ell})-{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\,\theta_{gk}\,\big(\big[E_{\ell}\,,H_{g}\big]\otimes H_{k}+H_{g}\otimes\big[E_{\ell}\,,H_{k}\big]\big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad=\;\delta(E_{\ell})-{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\,\theta_{gk}\,\big(\!-\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})E_{\ell}\otimes H_{k}-H_{g}\otimes\alpha_{\ell}(H_{k})E_{\ell}\,\big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\;T^{+}_{\ell}\otimes E_{\ell}-E_{\ell}\otimes T^{+}_{\ell}+{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\,\theta_{gk}\,\big(\,\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})E_{\ell}\otimes H_{k}+\alpha_{\ell}(H_{k})H_{g}\otimes E_{\ell}\,\big)\;=\quad

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\Big(\,T^{+}_{\ell}+{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\,\theta_{kg}\,\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})H_{k}\Big)\otimes E_{\ell}\,-\,E_{\ell}\otimes\Big(\,T^{+}_{\ell}+{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\,\theta_{kg}\,\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})H_{k}\Big)\;=

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;T^{+}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},\ell}\otimes E_{\ell}\,-\,E_{\ell}\otimes T^{+}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},\ell}\;=\;2\;T^{+}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},\ell}\wedge\!E_{\ell}

hence in short we get
\;\delta^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}(E_{\ell})\,=\,2\;T^{+}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},\ell}\wedge\!E_{\ell}\;
.
Similar computations give

\delta^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}(T)=0\;,\!\quad\delta^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}(F_{\ell})=2\,T^{-}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},\ell}\wedge\!F_{\ell}\;,\quad\forall\;\ell\in I\,,\,T\!\in\mathfrak{h}

Therefore, pulling back through
\phi_{\scriptscriptstyle P}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}\,
onto
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
the Lie cobracket of
{\Big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}\Big)}^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}
,  we find that this Lie cobracket is described by the formulas in claim
(c)
, q.e.d.
∎

With some extra effort we can get the following, key result:

Theorem 3.2.4
.

Every Lie superalgebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
is indeed a Lie superbialgebra,
whose Lie cobracket is given on generators by the formulas
(for all
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
,
\,i\in I
)

\delta\big(\,T\big)\,=\,0\quad,\qquad\delta\big(E_{i}\big)\,=\,2\;T^{+}_{i}\!\wedge E_{i}\quad,\qquad\delta\big(F_{i}\big)\,=\,2\;T^{-}_{i}\!\wedge F_{i}

(3.9)

Proof.

The result follows from the discussion above when the realization
{\mathcal{R}}
of
P
is straight. To prove the statement for general realizations
{\mathcal{R}}\,
, we proceed as follows. By assumption, we have a fixed Cartan super-datum
\,(A,p)\,
;
furthermore,
\,P_{s}:=2^{-1}\big(P+P^{\,\scriptscriptstyle T}\big)=DA=:P_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle A}}\,
,  and finally

\,{\mathcal{R}}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,
a realization of
P
.

By Lemma
2.1.8
, there is a
straight
realization
\;\tilde{{\mathcal{R}}}:=\big(\,\tilde{\mathfrak{h}}\,,\tilde{\Pi}\,,{\tilde{\Pi}}^{\vee}\,\big)\,
of
P
and an epimorphism of realizations
\;\underline{\pi}:\tilde{{\mathcal{R}}}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{R}}\;
;  moreover, up to possibly enlarging
\tilde{\mathfrak{h}}\,
, we can assume that
\,t:=\text{rk}\big(\tilde{\mathfrak{h}}\big)\geq 3n-\text{rk}\big(P_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle A}}\big)\,
. Then, by Proposition
2.2.6
(a)
— with
\,P^{\prime}=P_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle A}}:=DA\,
— there exists some
\,\Phi\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
such that
\,P_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle A}}=P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,
while
\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle A}:=\tilde{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\,\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,
is a straight realization of
\,P_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle A}}=P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,
. Hence, conversely, we have also
\,P={\big(P_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle A}}\big)}_{\scriptscriptstyle-\Phi}\,
and
\,\tilde{{\mathcal{R}}}={\big({\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle A}\big)}_{\scriptscriptstyle-\Phi}\,
. Then there exists an isomorphism of Lie superbialgebras

\;\phi^{\scriptscriptstyle-\Phi}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}:\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}^{\tilde{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}},p}=\mathfrak{g}_{({\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\scriptscriptstyle A})_{-{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}}}^{({\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle A})_{-{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}},p}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow}\limits^{\cong}}}\,{\Big(\mathfrak{g}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle A}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle A},p}\Big)}^{j_{-{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}}}\,
,
explicitly described as in Proposition
3.2.3
, up to switching
\Phi
to
-\Phi\,
;  moreover, the Lie cobracket in
\,\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}^{\tilde{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}},p}=\mathfrak{g}_{({\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\scriptscriptstyle A})_{-{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}}}^{({\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle A})_{-{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}},p}\,
is described as in Proposition
3.2.3
(c)
, which in terms of the defining generators of
\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}^{\tilde{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}},p}
reads exactly like (
3.9
) in the claim.

Now, the epimorphism of realizations
\;\underline{\pi}:\tilde{{\mathcal{R}}}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{R}}\;
“include” an epimorphism
\,\pi:\tilde{\mathfrak{h}}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{h}\,
of (Abelian) Lie
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–superalgebras; moreover, it follows at once from the presentation of both
\,\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}^{\tilde{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}},p}\,
and
\,\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\,
that
\pi
extends to an epimorphism of Lie superalgebras
\;\mathcal{L}_{\underline{\pi}}:\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}^{\tilde{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}},p}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
,  and that

\operatorname{Ker}\big(\mathcal{L}_{\underline{\pi}}\big)
is generated by
\,\operatorname{Ker}(\pi)\,
. Furthermore,

\,\operatorname{Ker}(\pi)\,
lies in the center of
\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}^{\tilde{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}},p}\,
,  by definitions and by Lemma
2.1.9
; moreover, the Lie cobracket of

\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}^{\tilde{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}},p}
kills
\operatorname{Ker}(\pi)\,
,  as it is trivial on
\tilde{\mathfrak{h}}\,
, so the latter is a Lie super
bi
ideal in the Lie superbialgebra
\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}^{\tilde{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}},p}\,
. Thus
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
inherits via

\mathcal{L}_{\underline{\pi}}
a
quotient Lie superbialgebra structure
from
\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}^{\tilde{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}},p}\,
, again described by (
3.9
),
q.e.d.
∎

Definition 3.2.5
.

Every such Lie superbialgebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
considered above will be called
multiparameter Lie superbialgebra
, in short
MpLSbA
.
In addition, we say that the MpLSbA
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
is
straight
,
or
small
, or
minimal
, or
split
, if such is
{\mathcal{R}}\,
.

Finally, we define the
rank
of
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
as
\;\operatorname{rk}\!\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\big):=\operatorname{rk}({\mathcal{R}})=\operatorname{rk}_{\Bbbk}(\mathfrak{h})\;
.
\diamondsuit

Remarks 3.2.6
.

Let
\mathfrak{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}_{+}\,
, resp.
\mathfrak{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}_{-}\,
, be the Lie sub-superalgebra of

\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}

generated by all the
E_{i}
’s, resp. all the
F_{i}
’s; and let
\,\mathfrak{b}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P},\pm}:=\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}_{\pm}=\mathfrak{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}_{\pm}\oplus\mathfrak{h}\,
. Then:

(a)

\mathfrak{b}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P},\pm}

is a Lie sub-superbialgebra of
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\,
,

(b)
there exist obvious
triangular decompositions

\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\;=\;\mathfrak{n}_{+}\oplus\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{n}_{-}\quad,\qquad\qquad\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\;=\;\mathfrak{n}_{-}\oplus\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{n}_{+}

(3.10)

The following, last two result stress the fact that the dependence of MpLSbA’s
on realizations — for a common, fixed multiparameter matrix
P
—
is
functorial
:

Proposition 3.2.7
.

Let
\,(A\,,p)\,
be a fixed Cartan super-datum and let

\,P\in M_{n}(\Bbbk)\,
be of Cartan type
A
.
If both
{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}
and
\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime}
are realizations of
P
and

\,\underline{\phi}:{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime}\,
is a morphism between them, then there exists a unique morphism of Lie superbialgebras

\;{\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\phi}}:\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},p}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\!\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime},p}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\;

that extends the morphism

\,\phi:\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}\!\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\mathfrak{h}^{\prime\prime}\,

given by
\underline{\phi}\,
;  moreover,

\,{\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\operatorname{id}}_{\mathcal{R}}}=\operatorname{id}_{\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}}\,
and

\;{\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\phi}^{\prime}\circ\,\underline{\phi}}={\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\phi}^{\prime}}\circ{\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\phi}}\;

(whenever
\,\underline{\phi}^{\prime}\circ\,\underline{\phi}\,
is defined).
In a nutshell, the construction
\,{\mathcal{R}}\mapsto\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\,
(for fixed
P
) is functorial in
{\mathcal{R}}\,
.

Further, if
\underline{\phi}
is an epimorphism, resp. a monomorphism, then

{\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\phi}}
is an epimorphism, resp. a monomorphism, as well.
Finally, for any morphism

\,\underline{\phi}:{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime}\,
,
the kernel
\,\operatorname{Ker}\big({\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\phi}}\,\big)
of

\,{\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\phi}}
coincides with
\,\operatorname{Ker}(\phi)\,
,
and the latter is central in

\,\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},p}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\,
.

In particular, when
\underline{\phi}\,
, and hence also

{\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\phi}}\,
, is an epimorphism, we have —
setting
\;\mathfrak{k}:=\operatorname{Ker}(\phi)\,
—
a central exact sequence of Lie superbialgebras

0\,\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\,\mathfrak{k}\,\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\,\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},p}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\,\;{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{{{\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\phi}}}}}}\;\,\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime},p}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\,\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\,0

Proof.

The existence of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\underline{\phi}}
is obvious, as well as all the other claims; we only spend a moment on
\operatorname{Ker}(\phi)
being central.
Lemma
2.1.9
implies

\;\operatorname{Ker}(\phi)\,\subseteq\,\bigcap\limits_{j\in I}\operatorname{Ker}(\alpha^{\prime}_{j})\;
;
then from the relations among the generators of

\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
we get that each element in
\operatorname{Ker}(\phi)
commutes with all generators of
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\,
, thus
\operatorname{Ker}(\phi)
is central.
∎

Corollary 3.2.8
.

With notation as above, if
\;{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\cong{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime}\,
then

\;\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},p}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\cong\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime},p}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\;
.

In particular, all MpLSbA’s built upon split realizations,
respectively small realizations, of the same matrix
P

and sharing the same rank of
\,\mathfrak{h}\,
, are isomorphic to each other,
hence they are independent (up to isomorphisms) of the specific realization,
but only depend on
P
and on the rank of
\,\mathfrak{h}\,
.

Proof.

This follows at once from Proposition
3.2.7

together with the uniqueness result in Proposition
2.1.5

and Proposition
2.1.7
.
∎

3.3.
Deformations of MpLSbA’s by toral twists

We will now go and study deformations of MpLSbA’s, following
§
3.1.3
.
We begin with deformations by twist, choosing the latter to be of a special type.

Let
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
be a MpLSbA as in §
3.2
above;
then
\mathfrak{h}
is a free
\Bbbk
–module of finite rank
\,t:=\operatorname{rk}(\mathfrak{h})\,
:
we fix in it a
\Bbbk
–basis
\,{\big\{H_{g}\big\}}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\,
, where

\mathcal{G}
is an index set with
\,|\mathcal{G}|=\operatorname{rk}(\mathfrak{h})=:t\,
.
We begin introducing the so-called “toral” twists for
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\,
.

Definition 3.3.1
.

For any
antisymmetric
matrix

\;\Theta=\big(\theta_{gk}\big)_{g,k\in\mathcal{G}}\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}(\Bbbk)\;
,  we set

j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}\;:=\;{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\theta_{gk}\,H_{g}\otimes H_{k}\;\in\;\mathfrak{h}\otimes\mathfrak{h}\;\subseteq\;\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}

(3.11)

and we call this
\,j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}\,

the toral twist

(or
“twist of toral type”
)
associated with
\,\Theta\,
.
\diamondsuit

Next result — which explains our use of terminology — follows by construction;
in particular, it makes use of the antisymmetry condition on
\Theta\,
.

Lemma 3.3.2
.

For any
\;\Theta=\big(\theta_{gk}\big)_{g,k\in\mathcal{G}}\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}(\Bbbk)\;
,  the element
\,j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}\,
in Definition
3.3.1
is a
twist
element for the Lie superbialgebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\,
,  in the sense of (
3.3
). ∎

Concerning deformations of MpLSbA’s by toral twists, our main result is the next one.
To settle its content, let
\,P\in M_{t}(\Bbbk)\,
be a multiparameter matrix of Cartan
type with associated Cartan super-datum
(A,p)\,
,
let
{\mathcal{R}}
be a realization of it, and let
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}

be the associated multiparameter Lie bialgebra; then, for any given antisymmetric matrix

\;\Theta=\big(\theta_{gk}\big)_{g,k\in\mathcal{G}}\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}(\Bbbk)\;
,  let

\;j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}\,:=\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{g,k=1}^{t}}\theta_{gk}\,H_{g}\otimes H_{k}\;

be the associated twist as in (
3.11
).
Moreover, we consider the “deformed” multiparameter matrix

\;P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}=\,{\big(\,p^{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\,:=\,P-\mathfrak{A}\,\Theta\,\mathfrak{A}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle T}\;

as in (
2.2
) — again of Cartan type, the same as
P
—
and its corresponding “deformed” realization

\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}\!:={\big\{T^{+}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},i}\,,T^{-}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},i}\big\}}_{i\in I}\,\big)\,
.

Theorem 3.3.3
.

There is a Lie superbialgebra isomorphism

\;f^{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}:\mathfrak{g}_{P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},p}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow}\limits^{\cong}}}\,{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\big)}^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}

(notation as above, with in right-hand side the twist deformation

\,{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\big)}^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}
of
\,\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}

by
j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}
occurs) given by

\;E_{i}\,\mapsto\,E_{i}\,,\;T\,\mapsto\,T\,
and

\;F_{i}\,\mapsto\,F_{i}\;

for all
\,i\in I\,
,
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
.

In particular, the class of all MpLSbA’s of any fixed Cartan type and
of fixed rank is stable by toral twist deformations.
Moreover, inside it the subclass of all such MpLSbA’s associated with

straight
, resp.
small
, realizations is stable as well.

Proof.

By definition — cf. §
3.1.3
—
the Lie superalgebra structure in
{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\big)}^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}

is the same as in
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\,
, and the latter only depends on the

\alpha_{j}
’s and the sums
\,S_{j}:=2^{-1}\big(T_{j}^{+}\!+T_{j}^{-}\big)\,

(
\,j\in I\,
). Now, both the
\alpha_{j}
’s and the
S_{j}
’s

do not change
(again by construction) when we pass from

\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
to
\mathfrak{g}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\Theta}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},p}
or viceversa;
therefore, the formulas in the claim (mapping each generator of

\mathfrak{g}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\Theta}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},p}
onto the same name generator of

\,\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}={\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\big)}^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}\,
) provide an isomorphism of Lie superalgebras.

Now consider the toral twist

\;j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}:={\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\theta_{gk}\,H_{g}\otimes H_{k}\;

given in (
3.11
). By (
3.4
)

\delta^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}(x)\;:=\;\delta(x)-x\,.\,j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}\;=\;\delta(x)-{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\theta_{gk}\,\big(\big[x,H_{g}\big]\otimes H_{k}+H_{g}\otimes\big[x,H_{k}\big]\big)

for all
\,x\in\mathfrak{g}\,
. Now take
\,x:=E_{\ell}\,
(
\,\ell\in I\,
):  then the previous formula reads

\hfil\displaystyle\delta^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}(E_{\ell})\;:=\;\delta(E_{\ell})-{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\,\theta_{gk}\,\big(\big[E_{\ell}\,,H_{g}\big]\otimes H_{k}+H_{g}\otimes\big[E_{\ell}\,,H_{k}\big]\big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad=\;\delta(E_{\ell})-{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\,\theta_{gk}\,\big(\!-\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})E_{\ell}\otimes H_{k}-H_{g}\otimes\alpha_{\ell}(H_{k})E_{\ell}\,\big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\;T^{+}_{\ell}\otimes E_{\ell}-E_{\ell}\otimes T^{+}_{\ell}+{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\,\theta_{gk}\,\big(\,\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})E_{\ell}\otimes H_{k}+\alpha_{\ell}(H_{k})H_{g}\otimes E_{\ell}\,\big)\;=\quad

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\Big(\,T^{+}_{\ell}+{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\,\theta_{kg}\,\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})H_{k}\Big)\otimes E_{\ell}\,-\,E_{\ell}\otimes\Big(\,T^{+}_{\ell}+{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\,\theta_{kg}\,\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})H_{k}\Big)\;=

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;T^{+}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},\ell}\otimes E_{\ell}\,-\,E_{\ell}\otimes T^{+}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},\ell}\;=\;2\;T^{+}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},\ell}\wedge\!E_{\ell}

hence in short we get
\;\delta^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}(E_{\ell})\,=\,2\;T^{+}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},\ell}\wedge\!E_{\ell}\;
.
Similar computations give

\delta^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}(T)=0\;,\!\quad\delta^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}(F_{\ell})=2\,T^{-}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},\ell}\wedge\!F_{i}\;,\quad\forall\;\ell\in I\,,\,T\!\in\mathfrak{h}

This means that,
through the Lie superalgebra isomorphism

f_{\scriptscriptstyle P}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}\,
,
the Lie coalgebra structure of

{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\big)}^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}
is described on generators exactly like that of

\mathfrak{g}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\Theta}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},p}\,
, with the new “coroots”

T^{\pm}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},\ell}
(
\,\ell\in I\,
) in

{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\big)}^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}
playing the role of the coroots

T^{\pm}_{\ell}
(
\,\ell\in I\,
) in

\mathfrak{g}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\Theta}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},p}\,
.
Thus

\;f^{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}:\mathfrak{g}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\Theta}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}\longrightarrow{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\big)}^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}\;

is an isomorphism of Lie super
bi
algebras.
∎

In fact,
the previous result can be reversed
, somehow.
Namely, our next result shows, in particular, that

every straight small MpLSbA can be realized as a toral twist
deformation of the Yamane’s MpLSbA
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}

(as in §
3.2.1
), cf. claim
(c)
below.

Theorem 3.3.4
.

With assumptions as above, let
P
and
P^{\prime}
be two matrices of Cartan type with the
same associated Cartan super-datum
(A,p)\,
,  i.e.
\,P_{s}=P^{\prime}_{s}\,
.

(a)  Let
\,{\mathcal{R}}
be a
straight
realization of
P
and let

\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}},p}

be the associated MpLSbA. Then there exists a matrix
\,\Theta\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}(\Bbbk)\,

such that
\,P^{\prime}=P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}\,
, the corresponding
{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}

is a
straight
realization of
\,P^{\prime}=P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}\,
,
and for the twist element

j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}
as in (
3.11
) we have

\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta},p}\;\cong\;{\big(\,\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}},p}\big)}^{j_{\Theta}}

In a nutshell, if
\,P^{\prime}_{s}=P_{s}\,
then from any straight MpLSbA over
P

we can obtain by toral twist deformation a straight MpLSbA (of the same rank) over
P^{\prime}\,
.

(b)  Let
{\mathcal{R}}
and
{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}
be
straight small
realizations of

P
and
P^{\prime}

respectively, with
\,\operatorname{rk}({\mathcal{R}})=\operatorname{rk}({\mathcal{R}}^{\prime})=:t\,
, and let
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
and

\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\!,p}
be the associated MpLSbA’s.
Then there exists a matrix
\,\Theta\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}(\Bbbk)\,

such that for the twist element

j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}
as in (
3.11
) we have

\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\!,p}\;\cong\;{\big(\,\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\big)}^{j_{\Theta}}

In a nutshell, if
\,P^{\prime}_{s}=P_{s}\,
then any straight small MpLSbA over

P^{\prime}
is isomorphic to a toral twist deformation of any straight small MpLSbA over
P
of the same rank.

(c)  Every straight small MpLSbA is isomorphic to some toral twist deformation of
Yamane’s MpLSbA
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{\raise-3.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\!\!A$}}}

(over
\,P_{\scriptscriptstyle A}=DA=P_{s}\,
, cf. §
3.2.1
)
of the same rank.

Proof.

(a)
By Theorem
3.3.3
it is enough to find

\,\Theta\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}(\Bbbk)\,
such that
\,P^{\prime}=P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}\,
,  that is

\;P^{\prime}=P-\mathfrak{A}\,\Theta\,\mathfrak{A}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle T}\,
;
but this is guaranteed by Lemma
2.2.5
, so we are done.

(b)
This follows from claim
(a)
, along with the uniqueness of straight small realizations — cf. Proposition
2.1.5
(b)
— and Proposition
3.2.7
.

(c)
This follows from
(b)
, once we take as

\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\!,p}

the given straight small MpLSbA and as
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
Yamane’s MpLSbA
\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\!,p}
over

\,P:=P_{\scriptscriptstyle A}=DA\,
.
∎

Observations 3.3.5
.

(a)
Theorems
3.3.3
and

3.3.4
allow the following interpretation.
Our MpLSbA’s
\,\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\,
depend on the multiparameter
P\,
;
at a further level, once we perform onto
\,\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\,
a deformation by
toral twist, the outcome

\;\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}},p}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P},{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}:={\big(\,\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\big)}^{j_{\Theta}}\;
depends on
two
multiparameters, namely

P

and

\Theta\,
. So all the

\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}},p}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P},{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}
’s form a
(seemingly) richer family of
“twice-multiparametric” Lie superbialgebras.
In spite of this, Theorem
3.3.3

proves that this family still
coincides

with our initial family of MpLSbA’s.

In short, Theorems
3.3.3
and

3.3.4
show the following. The dependence of the Lie
superbialgebra structure of
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}},p}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P},{\scriptscriptstyle\Theta}}

on the “double parameter”
(P\,,\Theta)
is “split” in the algebraic structure
(ruled by
P\,
) and in the coalgebraic structure (ruled by
\Theta
).
Now, Theorems
3.3.3
and
3.3.4

enable us to “polarize” this dependence so to codify it either entirely
within the algebraic structure (while the coalgebraic one is reduced to a “canonical form”)
— so the single multiparameter
P_{\Theta}
is enough to describe it —
or entirely within the coalgebraic structure (with the algebraic one being reduced
to the “standard” one) — so that one multiparameter
\Theta_{P}

is enough to describe it.

(b)
As we saw at the end of §
2.2.7
,
the (sub)class of
split

realizations is
not closed
under twist deformation; as a consequence,
the subclass of all MpLSbA’s that are “split” is not closed either
under twist deformation.

3.4.
Deformations of MpLSbA’s by toral
2
–cocycles

Let
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
be a MpLSbA as in §
3.2
,
and keep notation as above. Dually to what we did before in
§
3.3
, we consider now the so-called
“toral” 2–cocycles for
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
and the corresponding deformations by them.

Definition 3.4.1
.

Fix an antisymmetric
\Bbbk
–linear map
\,\chi:\mathfrak{h}\otimes\mathfrak{h}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\Bbbk\,

such that

\chi(S_{i}\,,\,-\,)\,=\,0\,=\,\chi(\,-\,,S_{i})\qquad\qquad\forall\;\;i\in I

(3.12)

where
\,S_{i}:=2^{-1}\big(\,T^{+}_{i}+T^{-}_{i}\big)\,

for all
\,i\in I\,
(cf. Definition
2.1.3
);
in other words, we have
\,\chi\in\text{\sl Alt}_{\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}}^{\,S}(\mathfrak{h})\,
, cf. (
2.5
).
Moreover, let

\,\;\pi^{\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}}_{\mathfrak{h}}:\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{h}\;\,

be the canonical linear projection induced by any one of the triangular
decompositions in
(
3.10
). We define

\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}:=\chi\circ\Big(\,\pi^{\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}}_{\mathfrak{h}}\otimes\pi^{\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}}_{\mathfrak{h}}\Big):\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{h}\otimes\mathfrak{h}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\Bbbk\qquad

and we call it
the toral 2–cocycle

(or
“2–cocycle of toral type”
)

associated with
\,\chi\,
.
\diamondsuit

The following lemma explains our use of terminology:

Lemma 3.4.2
.

For any antisymmetric

\Bbbk
–linear map
\,\chi:\mathfrak{h}\otimes\mathfrak{h}\longrightarrow\Bbbk\,
obeying (
3.12
),

\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}:=\chi\circ\Big(\pi^{\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}}_{\mathfrak{h}}\otimes\pi^{\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}}_{\mathfrak{h}}\,\Big):\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{h}\otimes\mathfrak{h}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\Bbbk

is a
2–cocycle
map for the Lie bialgebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\,
,  in the sense of (
3.5
).

Proof.

Follows from
[
GG4
, Lemma 3.4.2]

mutatis mutandis
.
∎

A dual analog of Theorems
3.3.3
and

3.3.4
asserts that one may obtain any (straight,
minimal, split) MpLSbA
as a
2
-cocycle deformation of some Yamane’s MpLSbA.
To state properly the result, we need some notation. Let
(A,p)
be a Cartan super-datum,

\,P\in M_{t}(\Bbbk)\,
a multiparameter matrix of Cartan type with Cartan matrix

A\,
,

{\mathcal{R}}
be a realization of it, and

\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
the associated multiparameter Lie superbialgebra.
For any
\,\chi\in\text{\sl Alt}_{\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}}^{\,S}(\mathfrak{h})\,

as in (
2.5
), let

\,\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}:\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\!\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\longrightarrow\Bbbk\,

be the
2
–cocycle as in Lemma
3.4.2
.

Consider the antisymmetric matrix

\,\mathring{X}:={\Big(\,\mathring{\chi}_{i{}j}=\chi\big(\,T_{i}^{+}\,,T_{j}^{+}\big)\Big)}_{i,j\in I}\!\in\mathfrak{so}_{n}(\Bbbk)\,
.
By Proposition
2.3.2
, we have a matrix
P_{(\chi)}
and a realization
{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)}
of it,
given by

P_{(\chi)}:=\,P\,+\,\mathring{X}\,=\,{\Big(\,p^{(\chi)}_{i{}j}:=\,p_{ij}+\mathring{\chi}_{i{}j}\Big)}_{\!i,j\in I}\;\;,\quad\Pi_{(\chi)}:=\,{\Big\{\,\alpha_{i}^{(\chi)}:=\,\alpha_{i}\pm\chi\big(\,\text{--}\,,T_{i}^{\pm}\big)\Big\}}_{i\in I}

Note that
P_{(\chi)}
is also a multiparameter matrix of Cartan type with Cartan
matrix
A
, for we are adding just an antisymmetric part.

Theorem 3.4.3
.

Keep notation as above.

(a)  There exists a Lie superbialgebra isomorphism

\mathfrak{g}_{P_{(\chi)}}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},p}\simeq{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\big)}_{\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}}\;
.

(b)  Let
P
and
P^{\prime}
be two matrices of Cartan type with the same associated Cartan matrix
A\,
. Then the following holds:

(b.1)  Let
\,{\mathcal{R}}
be a
split
realization of
P
and let

\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}

be the associated MpLSbA. Then there is
\,\chi\in\text{\sl Alt}_{\,\Bbbk}^{\,S}(\mathfrak{h})\,
such that

\,P^{\prime}=P_{(\chi)}\,
, the corresponding
{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)}
is a
split
realization of
\,P^{\prime}\,
,
and for the 2–cocycle
\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}
as in Definition
3.4.1
we have

\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},p}\;\cong\;{\big(\,\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}\big)}_{\chi_{{}_{\mathfrak{g}}}}

(b.2)  Let
{\mathcal{R}}
and
{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}
be
split minimal

realizations of
P
and
P^{\prime}

respectively, and let
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
and

\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},p}

be the associated MpLSbA’s.
Then there exists
\,\chi\in\text{\sl Alt}_{\,\Bbbk}^{\,S}(\mathfrak{h})\,

such that for the 2–cocycle
\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}

as in Definition
3.4.1
we have

\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},p}\;\cong\;{\big(\,\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\big)}_{\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}}

(b.3)  Every split minimal MpLSbA is isomorphic to some
toral 2–cocycle deformation of Yamane’s Lie superbialgebra.

Proof.

(a)
Since the Lie coalgebra structure in

{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\big)}_{\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}}
is the same as in
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\,
,
and the latter coincides with the one in

\mathfrak{g}_{P_{(\chi)}}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},p}
, we have a Lie coalgebra isomorphism among them.
Let us show that it is a Lie algebra isomorphism as well.
The Lie bracket in
{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\big)}_{\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}}

is a deformation of that of
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
by the formula
(
3.6
):

[x,y]_{\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}}:=\,[x,y]-(\partial_{*}\chi_{\mathfrak{g}})(x\otimes y)\,=\,[x,y]-x_{[1]}\,\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}(x_{[2]},y)\,-\,{(-1)}^{|x||y_{[1]}|}\,y_{[1]}\,\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,y_{[2]})

Let us check that the modified defining relations in

{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\big)}_{\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}}
coincide with the ones in

\mathfrak{g}_{P_{(\chi)}}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},p}\,
. First note that by the formula above,

[x,y]_{\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}}=[x,y]
whenever
x,y
are elements in the radical of

\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}
; in particular for all elements
E_{i},F_{i}
for all
i\in I
.
Hence, we need to check only those relations involving elements in
the Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}
of
\mathfrak{g}_{P_{(\chi)}}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},p}
.
As the Lie cobracket on this subalgebra is trivial, we get that

{[T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime}]}_{\chi_{{}_{\mathfrak{g}}}}\,=\;[T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime}]\,-\,T^{\prime}_{[1]}\,\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}\big(T^{\prime}_{[2]},T^{\prime\prime}\big)\,+\,T^{\prime\prime}_{[1]}\,\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}\big(T^{\prime\prime}_{[2]},T^{\prime}\big)\,=\,[T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime}]\,=\,0

for all
\,T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime}\in\mathfrak{h}\,
. Take now
\,i\in I\,
: since

\,\delta(T)=0\,
,
\,\delta(E_{i})=2\,T_{i}^{+}\!\wedge E_{i}\,
and

\,\delta(F_{i})=2\,T_{i}^{-}\!\wedge F_{i}\,
,  we get that

\displaystyle{[T,E_{i}]}_{\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle\,=\,[T,E_{i}]-E_{i}\,\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}\big(T_{i}^{+},T\big)\,=\,\alpha_{i}(T)E_{i}+\chi\big(T,T_{i}^{+}\big)E_{i}\,=\,\alpha_{i}^{(\chi)}(T)\,E_{i}

\displaystyle{[T,F_{i}]}_{\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle\,=\,[T,F_{i}]-F_{i}\,\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}\big(T_{i}^{-},T\big)\,=\,-\alpha_{i}(T)F_{i}+\chi_{\mathfrak{g}}\big(T,T_{i}^{-}\big)\,F_{i}\,=

\displaystyle=\,-\Big(\alpha_{i}(T)F_{i}-\chi\big(T,T_{i}^{-}\big)\Big)\,F_{i}\,=\,-\alpha_{i}^{(\chi)}(T)\,F_{i}

(b.1)
By
(a)
, it is enough to find an antisymmetric

\Bbbk
–linear

\,\chi:\mathfrak{h}\otimes\mathfrak{h}\!\longrightarrow\!\Bbbk\,
obeying
(
3.12
) such that

\,P^{\prime}=P_{(\chi)}\,
;
this is guaranteed by Proposition
2.3.5
. Now

(b.2)
follows from
(b.1)
,
and the uniqueness of split realizations
— cf. Proposition
2.1.5
(b)
, and

(b.3)
follows from
(b.2)
, taking as

\mathfrak{g}_{\scriptscriptstyle P^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},p}

the given split minimal MpLSbA and for
\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}
Yamane’s MpLSbA
which by definition is straight and split minimal.
∎

Remark 3.4.4
.

The theorem above tell us that the class of all MpLSbA’s of any fixed Cartan type and of fixed rank is stable by toral 2–cocycle deformations, and inside it, the subclass of all such MpLSbA’s associated with
split
, resp.
minimal
, realizations is stable as well. Also, for

P
and
P^{\prime}
two matrices of Cartan type with the same associated Cartan matrix
A\,
, one can construct by a toral 2–cocycle deformation a split MpLSbA over one of them from any split MpLSbA over the other. Besides, any split minimal MpLSbA over
P^{\prime}
is isomorphic to a toral 2–cocycle deformation of any split minimal MpLSbA over
P
.

3.4.5
.

Generalizations.
The entire analysis and constructions devised in this section can be applied
verbatim
to the setup of
affine
Lie superalgebras, starting form their presentation as provided in
[
Ya2
]
and
[
Ya3
]
. One has to take care of even more additional relations and their transformations, but this is indeed a matter of bookkeeping, nothing more. What is more relevant, though, is that the key ideas underlying the whole recipe can indeed be applied to a much larger framework (and specific examples): we expand on this in
[
GG5
]
.

4.
Formal Multiparameter QUE Superalgebras (=FoMpQUESA’s)

This section is devoted to introduce formal multiparameter quantized universal enveloping superalgebras (in short, FoMpQUESA’s) and to study their deformations.

4.1.
The Hopf (super)algebra setup and QUESA’s

We will deal in the following with Hopf superalgebras and Hopf algebras alike, for which we
will use standard notation. Most of the cases, the ground ring will be the ring
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
of
formal power series in the indeterminate
\hbar
with coefficients in the field
\Bbbk

(which is always assumed to have characteristic zero). For this ring, we will consider

\hbar
–adically complete
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–modules: these form a tensor category, whose tensor
product is nothing but the
\hbar
–adic completion of the standard (algebraic) tensor
product. When we deal with “super” objects (i.e., they are
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
–graded), the tensor
product is graded as well, so that the relevant category is indeed a (non-trivially) braided
symmetric category. In particular, in this category a “Hopf (super)algebra”
will be a Hopf algebra in the generalized, categorical sense
(i.e., with respect to the tensor category)
— in particular, it will be topological and
\hbar
–adically complete:
for further details, see for instance
[
CP
]
,
[
KS
]
, and
[
Ge1
]
for the super case.

4.1.1
.

Topological issues and topological Hopf (super)algebras.
The ring
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
mentioned
above carries a natural topology, called the
\hbar
–adic topology
, coming from
the so-called
\hbar
–adic norm
with respect to which it is complete, namely

\big\|\,a_{n}\hbar^{n}+a_{n+1}\hbar^{n+1}+\cdots\big\|\;:=\;C^{-n}\qquad\big(\,a_{n}\not=0\,\big)

where
\,C>1\,
is any fixed constant in
\mathbb{R}\,
.
In this sense, we shall consider
topological

{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–modules and the
completed
tensor products among them, which we denote by

\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
or simply by
\,\widehat{\otimes}\,
.
For any
\Bbbk
–vector space
V\,
, set

V[[\hbar]]\;:=\;\Big\{\,{\textstyle\sum_{n\geq 0}}v_{n}\hbar^{n}\,\Big|\,v_{n}\in V\,,\;\forall\,n\geq 0\,\Big\}

then
V[[\hbar]]
is a complete
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–module. We call a topological
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–module

topologically free
if it is isomorphic to
V[[\hbar]]
for some

\Bbbk
–vector space
V\,
. For two topologically free modules

V[[\hbar]]
and
W[[\hbar]]
one has that

\;V[[\hbar]]\,\widehat{\otimes}\,W[[\hbar]]\,\cong\,\big(V\otimes W\big)[[\hbar]]\,
.

All completed tensor products between
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–modules
will be denoted simply by
\otimes\,
,
unless we intend to stress the topological aspect. In particular,
we make no distinction on the notation
between Hopf superalgebras and topological
Hopf superalgebras; we assume it is well-understood from the context.

4.1.2
.

Quantized Universal Enveloping Superalgebras.

Recall that a
deformation superalgebra
is a topological, unital, associative

{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–superalgebra
A
which is topologically free as a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–module.
Conversely, a deformation of a (unital, associative)
\Bbbk
–superalgebra
A_{0}
is
by definition a deformation algebra
A
such that

\;A_{0}\,\cong\,A\big/\hbar\,A\;
. The same criteria apply to the notion of
“deformation Hopf superalgebra”, just replacing “topological, unital, associative

{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–superalgebra” with “topological Hopf
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–superalgebra”.
Following Geer (cf.
[
Ge1
]
, §2.3, and references therein),
we say that a deformation Hopf superalgebra
H
is a

quantized universal enveloping superalgebra
(or
QUESA
in short) if

\;H\big/\hbar\,H\,\cong\,U(\mathfrak{g})\;
for some Lie superalgebra
\mathfrak{g}\,
.
In particular, the Lie superbracket in
\mathfrak{g}
comes from the multiplication in

\;U(\mathfrak{g})\,\cong\,H\big/\hbar H\;
.
Moreover, this
\mathfrak{g}
inherits a Lie supercoalgebra structure from the QUESA,
making it into a Lie
superbialgebra
, thanks to the following result:

Theorem 4.1.3
.

(cf.
[
Ge1
, §2.3]
)
Let
H
be a quantized universal enveloping superalgebra with

\;H\big/\hbar\,H\,\cong\,U(\mathfrak{g})\;
.
Then the Lie superalgebra
\mathfrak{g}
is naturally equipped with a
Lie superbialgebra structure, whose Lie cobracket is defined by

\delta(\text{\rm x})\,:=\,\frac{\,\Delta(x)-\Delta^{\operatorname{op}}(x)\,}{\,\hbar\,}\qquad\big(\hskip-10.0pt\mod\hbar\,\big)

(4.1)

where
\,x\in H\,
is any lifting of
\;\text{\rm x}\in\mathfrak{g}\subseteq U(\mathfrak{g})\,\cong\,H\big/\hbar H\,
. ∎

Definition 4.1.4
.

[
CP
,
Ge1
]

The semiclassical limit of a quantized universal enveloping superalgebra

H
is the Lie superbialgebra
\,\big(\,\mathfrak{g}\,,\,[-,-]\,,\,\delta\,\big)\,

where
\mathfrak{g}
is the Lie superalgebra such that

\,H\big/\hbar\,H\cong U(\mathfrak{g})\,
and
\delta
is defined as above.
Conversely, we say that
H
is a quantization of the Lie superbialgebra

\big(\,\mathfrak{g}\,,\,[-,-]\,,\,\delta\,\big)\,
.
\diamondsuit

4.1.5
.

Hopf superalgebra deformations.

There exist two standard methods to deform Hopf algebras in braided (symmetric) categories,
thus in particular Hopf superalgebras
(and even quasi-Hopf superalgebras, more in general),
leading to so-called “twist deformations” and to “
2
–cocycle
deformations”: hereafter we recall both procedures — which are naturally
dual to each other,
in a very precise sense — adapting them to the setup of
topological

Hopf superalgebras, then later on we apply them to formal quantum supergroups.
We will be somewhat sketchy, since these objects for a Hopf algebra in any symmetric tensor category are defined in the same way as in the category of vector spaces; further details can be found in literature.

\underline{\text{Twist deformations}}
:

Let
H
be a Hopf superalgebra (possibly in topological sense). We call
“
twist element
”, or just a “
twist
”, of
H
any even (hence homogeneous) , invertible element
\,{\mathcal{F}}\in H\otimes H\,
such that

{\mathcal{F}}_{1,2}\,\big(\Delta\otimes\text{id}\big)({\mathcal{F}})\,=\,{\mathcal{F}}_{2,3}\,\big(\text{id}\otimes\Delta\big)({\mathcal{F}})\quad,\qquad\big(\epsilon\otimes\text{id}\big)({\mathcal{F}})=1=\big(\text{id}\otimes\epsilon\big)({\mathcal{F}})

Then
H
bears a second Hopf superalgebra structure, denoted
H^{\mathcal{F}}

and called
twist deformation
of the old one, with the old product,
unit and counit, but with new “twisted” coproduct
\Delta^{\mathcal{F}}
and antipode

\mathcal{S}^{\mathcal{F}}
given by

\qquad\Delta^{\!{\mathcal{F}}}(x)\,:=\,{\mathcal{F}}\,\Delta(x)\,{\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\quad,\qquad\mathcal{S}^{\mathcal{F}}(x)\,:=\,v\,\mathcal{S}(x)\,v^{-1}\quad\qquad\forall\;\;x\in H

where
\,v:=\sum_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{S}(f^{\prime}_{1})\,f^{\prime}_{2}\,
— with

\,\sum_{\mathcal{F}}f^{\prime}_{1}\otimes f^{\prime}_{2}={\mathcal{F}}^{-1}\,
—
is invertible in
H\,
.

When
H
is in fact a
topological
Hopf superalgebra

— meaning that, in particular, its coproduct
\Delta

takes values into
\,H\otimes H\,
where now “
\,\otimes\,
”
stands for a suitable topological tensor product —
then the same notions still make sense, and the related results apply again,
up to properly reading them. Moreover, if
H
is only a superbialgebra
(possibly topological), then the same recipe makes
H^{\mathcal{F}}

into a superbialgebra again.

\underline{\text{Cocycle deformations}}
:

Let
\,\big(H,m\,,1\,,\Delta\,,\epsilon\big)\,

be a superbialgebra over a ring
\Bbbk\,
. We call “(normalized)
Hopf 2-cocycle
”
(or just a “
2
–cocycle” if no confusion arises) any convolution invertible,
parity-preserving map
\sigma
in
\,\operatorname{Hom}_{\Bbbk}(H\otimes H,\Bbbk\,)\,

such that

{(-1)}^{|b_{(2)}||c_{(1)}|}\,\sigma\big(b_{(1)},c_{(1)}\big)\,\sigma\big(a,b_{(2)}c_{(2)}\big)\,\;=\;\,{(-1)}^{|a_{(2)}||b_{(1)}|}\,\sigma\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,\sigma\big(a_{(2)}b_{(2)},c\big)

and
\,\sigma(a,1)=\epsilon(a)=\sigma(1,a)\,
for all
\,a,b,c\in H\,
.

Using a
2
–cocycle
\sigma
it is possible to define a new
superalgebra structure on
H
by deforming the multiplication.
Indeed, define

\,m_{\sigma}=\sigma*m*\sigma^{-1}:H\otimes H\longrightarrow H\,
by

m_{\sigma}(a,b)\,=\,a\cdot_{\sigma}b\,=\,{(-1)}^{|a_{(2)}||b_{(1)}|+|a_{(3)}||b_{(1)}|+|a_{(3)}||b_{(2)}|}\,\sigma\big(a_{(1)},b_{(1)}\big)\,a_{(2)}\,b_{(2)}\,\sigma^{-1}\big(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}\big)

for all
\,a,b\in H\,
. If in addition
H
is a Hopf superalgebra with antipode
\mathcal{S}\,
, then define also the “deformed antipode”
\,\mathcal{S}_{\sigma}:H\longrightarrow H\,
by

\mathcal{S}_{\sigma}(a)\,=\,\sigma\big(a_{(1)},\mathcal{S}(a_{(2)})\big)\,\mathcal{S}(a_{(3)})\,\sigma^{-1}\big(\mathcal{S}(a_{(4)}),a_{(5)}\big)

[formula]

It is known that
\,\big(H,m_{\sigma},1,\Delta,\epsilon\big)\,

is in turn a superbialgebra, and
\,\big(H,m_{\sigma},1,\Delta,\epsilon,\mathcal{S}_{\sigma}\big)
is a Hopf superalgebra: we shall call such a new structure on
H
a

cocycle deformation
of the old one, and we shall graphically denote it by

H_{\sigma}\,
.

N.B.:
in §
5.2
later on, we will actually apply a suitable generalization of this procedure, namely the process of “deformation by
polar–2–cocycle
”: this is introduced in
[
GG4
]
for Hopf algebras that are quantum groups, we shall presently extend it (in a specific example) to the context of quantum
super
groups.

4.1.6
.

Some
q
–numbers.

Let
{\mathbb{Z}\big[\hskip 1.0ptq,q^{-1}\big]}
be the ring of Laurent polynomials with integral coefficients in the indeterminate
q\,
. For every
\,n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\,
we define

\hfil\displaystyle{(0)}_{q}\,:=\;1\;\;,\quad{(n)}_{q}\,:=\;\frac{\,q^{n}-1\,}{\,q-1\,}\;=\;1+q+\cdots+q^{n-1}\;=\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{s=0}^{n-1}}\,q^{s}\qquad\big(\,\in\,\mathbb{Z}[q]\,\big)

\hfil\displaystyle{(n)}_{q}!\,:=\;{(0)}_{q}{(1)}_{q}\cdots{(n)}_{q}:=\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{s=0}^{n}}{(s)}_{q}\;\;,\quad{\binom{n}{k}}_{\!q}\,:=\;\frac{\,{(n)}_{q}!\,}{\;{(k)}_{q}!{(n-k)}_{q}!\,}\qquad\big(\,\in\,\mathbb{Z}[q]\,\big)

\hfil\displaystyle{[0]}_{q}:=\,1\;,\;\;{[n]}_{q}:=\,\frac{\,q^{n}-q^{-n}\,}{\,q-q^{-1}\,}=\,q^{-(n-1)}+\cdots+q^{n-1}={\textstyle\sum\limits_{s=0}^{n-1}}\,q^{2\,s-n+1}\!\quad\big(\in{\mathbb{Z}\big[\hskip 1.0ptq,q^{-1}\big]}\,\big)

\hfil\displaystyle{[n]}_{q}!\,:=\;{[0]}_{q}{[1]}_{q}\cdots{[n]}_{q}=\,{\textstyle\prod\limits_{s=0}^{n}}{[s]}_{q}\;\;,\quad{\bigg[\,{n\atop k}\,\bigg]}_{q}\,:=\;\frac{\,{[n]}_{q}!\,}{\;{[k]}_{q}!{[n-k]}_{q}!\,}\qquad\big(\,\in\,{\mathbb{Z}\big[\hskip 1.0ptq,q^{-1}\big]}\,\big)

In particular, we have the identities

{(n)}_{q^{2}}=q^{n-1}{[n]}_{q}\;\;,\qquad{(n)}_{q^{2}}!=q^{\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}{[n]}_{q}!\;\;,\qquad{\binom{n}{k}}_{\!\!q^{2}}=q^{k(n-k)}{\bigg[\,{n\atop k}\,\bigg]}_{q}\;\;.

Moreover, for any field
\mathbb{F}
we can think of Laurent polynomials as functions on
\mathbb{F}^{\times}
,
hence for any
\,q\in\mathbb{F}^{\times}\,
we shall read every symbol above as a suitable element in
\,\mathbb{F}\,
.

4.2.
Formal multiparameter QUESA’s (=FoMpQUESA’s)

We introduce now the notion of
formal multiparameter quantum universal enveloping
superalgebra
— or just “FoMpQUESA”, in short —
in particular proving, somewhat indirectly, that it carries a structure of Hopf superalgebra.

Hereafter,
\Bbbk
is again a field of characteristic zero,

{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
the ring of formal power series in
\hbar
with coefficients in
\Bbbk\,
.
In any topological,
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–adically complete
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–algebra
\mathcal{A}\,
,
if
\,X\in\mathcal{A}\,
we set

\;e^{\hbar\,X}:=\exp(\hbar\,X)\,=\,{\textstyle\sum\limits_{n=0}^{+\infty}}\,\hbar^{n}X^{n}\big/n!\;\in\,\mathcal{A}\;
.

Definition 4.2.1
.

For later use, we fix some more notation:

(a)
Let
\,A:={\big(\,a_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\,

be a fixed Cartan matrix as in Definition
2.1.2
,
with
\,I:=\{1,\dots,n\}\,
, and let

\,P:={\big(\,p_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,

be a square matrix of Cartan type associated with
A

(cf. Definition
2.1.3
(d)
), so

\,P+P^{\,\scriptscriptstyle T}=2\,D\,A\,
,
i.e.
\,p_{ij}+p_{ji}=2\,d_{i}\,a_{ij}\,
for all

\,i,j\in I\,
, which implies

\,p_{ii}=2\,d_{i}\,
for all
\,i\in I\,

(notation of §
2.1
).

We define the following elements in
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
,  for all
\,i,j\in I\,
:

\hfil\displaystyle q:=e^{\hbar}=\exp(\hbar)\;\;,\quad q_{i}:=e^{\hbar\,d_{i}}=q^{d_{i}}\;\;,\quad q_{ij}:=e^{\hbar\,p_{ij}}=q^{p_{ij}}\;\;,\quad q_{ij}^{1/2}:=e^{\hbar\,p_{ij}/2}

\hfil\displaystyle k_{ij}\,:=\,e^{\hbar\,p_{i,j}/2}e^{-\hbar\,p_{j,i}/2}\,=\,q^{+p_{ij}/2}\,q^{-p_{ji}/2}\,=\,q_{ij}^{1/2}\,q_{ji}^{-1/2}

In particular we have
\,q_{ii}^{1/2}=e^{\hbar{}d_{i}}=q_{i}\,
and

\;q_{ij}\,q_{ji}=q_{ii}^{\,a_{ij}}\;
for all
\,i,j\in I\,
.

For any
\,i\in I\,
, we set
\,\nu_{i}:=q^{\varepsilon_{i}}\,
,
where the
\varepsilon_{i}
’s are given as follows:

\displaystyle(\varepsilon_{1}\,,\varepsilon_{2}\,,\varepsilon_{3}\,,\varepsilon_{4})

\displaystyle:=\;(6,-2,-2,-2)\qquad\text{ if }\ A=F_{4}\;\;,

(4.2)

\displaystyle(\varepsilon_{1}\,,\varepsilon_{2}\,,\varepsilon_{3})

\displaystyle:=\;(-2,2,6)\qquad\text{ if }\ A=G_{3}\;\;,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{i}\,:=\;\pm 1

\displaystyle\forall\;\;i\quad\text{otherwise, {but\/} for the conditions}

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{n}\,=\;+\varepsilon_{n-1}

\displaystyle\text{\ \ if }\ A=D_{n}(I)\;,\quad\varepsilon_{n}\,=\;-\varepsilon_{n-1}\text{\ \ if }\ A=D_{n}(I\!I)

In fact this corresponds to choosing an orthogonal bilinear form
— in some vector space containing the roots of the Lie superalgebra built out of
A
and some parity function — so that

\,\varepsilon_{i}=(\epsilon_{i}\,,\epsilon_{i})\,
,  cf.
[
Ya2
]
.

(b)
Given any superalgebra
A
over some ring
R\,
,
we define the “supercommutator”

\>{[X,Y]}_{c}:=X\,Y-c\,{(-1)}^{p(X){}p(Y)}\,Y\,X\;

for any
\,c\in R\,
and for any homogeneous
X
,
Y\in A\,
,
and then extend by linearity as usual. Moreover, for
\,c=1\,

we adopt the simpler notation
\;[X,Y]:={[X,Y]}_{1}\;
.
\diamondsuit

We can now define our FoMpQUESA’s, using notation as in
Definition
4.2.1
above.

Definition 4.2.2
.

Let
\,\big(\,A:={\big(\,a_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\,,\,p\,\big)\,

be a Cartan super-datum, as in Definition
2.1.2
, and

\,P:={\big(\,p_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,

a matrix of Cartan type associated with
A
.
We fix a realization
\,{\mathcal{R}}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,

of
P
as in Definition
2.1.3
.

(a)
We define the
formal multiparameter quantum universal super
enveloping algebra
— in short
formal MpQUESA, or simply FoMpQUESA
—
with multiparameter
P
and realization
{\mathcal{R}}
as follows.
It is the unital, associative, topological,
\hbar
–adically complete

{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–superalgebra
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
generated (in topological sense) by the

{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–submodule
\mathfrak{h}
together with elements

\,E_{i}\,
,
F_{i}\,
(for all
\,i\in I\,
),
with the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
–grading given by setting parity on generators

|T|:={\bar{0}}\quad\forall\;\;T\in\mathfrak{h}\;\;,\qquad|E_{i}|:=p(i)=:|F_{i}|\quad\forall\;\;i\in I

and with relations (for all
\,T,T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime}\in\mathfrak{h}\,
,
\,i\,,j\in I\,
)

\displaystyle T\,E_{j}-E_{j}\,T\,=\,+\alpha_{j}(T)\,E_{j}\;,\quad T^{\prime}\,T^{\prime\prime}\,=\,T^{\prime\prime}\,T^{\prime}\;,\quad T\,F_{j}-F_{j}\,T\,=\,-\alpha_{j}(T)\,F_{j}

(4.3)

\displaystyle[E_{i}\,,F_{j}]\,\;=\;\,\delta_{i,j}\,{{\;e^{+\hbar\,T_{i}^{+}}-\,e^{-\hbar\,T_{i}^{-}}\;}\over{\;q_{i}^{+1}-\,q_{i}^{-1}\;}}\hskip 93.0pt

(4.4)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{s=0}^{1-a_{ij}}{(-1)}^{s}{\left[{{1-a_{ij}}\atop s}\right]}_{\!q_{i}}

\displaystyle k_{ij}^{\,s}\,E_{i}^{1-a_{ij}-s}E_{j}E_{i}^{s}\;=\;0\quad\;\;\big(\,i\neq j\,,\,p(i)={\bar{0}}\,\big)\hskip 3.0pt

(4.5)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{s=0}^{1-a_{ij}}{(-1)}^{s}{\left[{{1-a_{ij}}\atop s}\right]}_{\!q_{i}}

\displaystyle k_{ji}^{\,s}\,F_{i}^{1-a_{ij}-s}F_{j}F_{i}^{\,s}\;=\;0\quad\;\;\big(\,i\neq j\,,\,p(i)={\bar{0}}\,\big)\enskip

(4.6)

\displaystyle\big[E_{i}\,,E_{j}\big]_{k_{ij}}=\,0\;\;,\quad\big[F_{i}\,,F_{j}\big]_{k_{ji}}=\,0\qquad\qquad\text{if}\quad a_{i,j}=0

(4.7)

\displaystyle{\Big[\big[{[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]}_{\nu_{j}k_{ij}}\,,E_{k}\big]_{\nu_{j+1}k_{ik}k_{jk}}\,,E_{j}\Big]}_{k_{ij}k_{kj}}\!=\,0\;\;\;\text{in any of the following cases:}

(4.8)

\displaystyle-\;\Big[\big[E_{n-2}\,,E_{n}\big]_{\nu_{n-1}k_{n-2,n}}\,,E_{n-1}\Big]_{\nu_{n}k_{n-2,n-1}k_{n,n-1}}\;=\;0

\displaystyle E_{n}\,{\Big]}_{2\,\nu_{n}\,k_{n-3,n}\,k_{n-2,n}\,k_{n-1,n}}\,,\,E_{n-1}\,{\bigg]}_{\nu_{n}\,k_{n-3,n-1}\,k_{n-2,n-1}\,k_{n,n-1}}\,,

\displaystyle E_{n-2}\,{\Bigg]}_{\nu_{n-1}\,k_{n-3,n-2}\,k_{n-1,n-2}^{\,2}k_{n,n-2}}\,,\,E_{n-1}\,{\Bigg]}_{k_{n-3,n-1}\,k_{n-2,n-1}^{\,2}\,k_{n,n-1}}\,\;=\;\;0

for
n\!-\!3

n

and
\,A\,
of type
\,C_{n}

and also the like of relations (

), (
4.9
), (
4.10
), (
4.11
), (

) with the

’s replaced by

’s, the
q_{ij}
’s by
q_{ji}
’s and the
k_{ij}
’s by
k_{ji}
’s.

\underline{\text{N.B.}}
:
In (
4.12
) we have removed a subscript “2” which appears in Yamane’s original formula in
[
Ya1
]
, seemingly as a misprint. Also, the like of formulas (
4.5
) and (
4.6
) in [
loc. cit.
] have “
\,+\big|a_{ij}\big|\,
” instead of “
\,-a_{ij}\,
”,  yet the two do coincide because of the assumption
\,p(i)={\bar{0}}\,
.

(b)
We say that
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
is
straight
, or
small
,
or
minimal
, or
split
,
if such is
{\mathcal{R}}\,
; also, we define the
rank
of
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
as

\;\operatorname{rk}\!\big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big):=\operatorname{rk}({\mathcal{R}})=\operatorname{rk}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}(\mathfrak{h})\,
.

(c)
We define the
Cartan subalgebra

U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})\,
, or just
U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\,
, of a FoMpQUESA
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})

as being the unital,
\hbar
–adically complete topological

{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–subsuperalgebra of
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})

generated by the
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–submodule
\mathfrak{h}\,
.

(d)
We define the
positive, resp. negative, Borel subsuperalgebra

U^{\,{\mathcal{R}},p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{b}_{+})
, resp.
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}},p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{b}_{-})
,
of
\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
to be
the unital,
\hbar
–adically complete topological
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–subsuperalgebra of

U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
generated by
\mathfrak{h}
and the
E_{i}
’s, resp. by
\mathfrak{h}
and the

F_{i}
’s (
\,i\in I\,
).

(e)
We define the
positive, resp. negative, nilpotent subsuperalgebra

U^{\,{\mathcal{R}},p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{n}_{+})
, resp.
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}},p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{n}_{-})
,
of a FoMpQUESA
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
to be the unital,
\hbar
–adically complete topological

{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–subsuperalgebra
of
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
generated by all the
E_{i}
’s, resp. all the
F_{i}
’s
(
\,i\in I\,
).
\diamondsuit

Remark 4.2.3
.

In Definition
4.2.2
(a)
, all relations (
4.7
)
through (
4.12
), as well as their counterparts with the
F_{\ell}
’s
replacing the
E_{\ell}
’s, are all given in terms of “iterated

q
–supercommutators” (either identifying such a
q
–supercommutator to zero,
or identifying two of them). Similarly, it is important to stress that also

the quantum Serre’s relations, namely (
4.5
) and
(
4.6
), can be expressed in that way
.
We prove this for (
4.5
), the case of (
4.6
) being entirely similar, up to switching every
q_{ij}\,
, resp. every
k_{ij}\,
, to
q_{ji}\,
, resp. to
k_{ji}\,
.

Indeed, let
E_{i,j}
denote the left-hand side of (
4.5
). Then with notation as in Definition
4.2.1
(b)
, straightforward computations yield

\displaystyle a_{i,j}=0

\displaystyle\quad\Longrightarrow\quad E_{i,j}\;=\;{[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]}_{q_{ij}}\qquad

(4.13)

\displaystyle a_{i,j}=-1

\displaystyle\quad\Longrightarrow\quad E_{i,j}\;=\;\big[E_{i}\,,{[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]}_{q_{ij}}\big]_{q_{ii}\,q_{ij}}

(4.14)

\displaystyle a_{i,j}=-2

\displaystyle\quad\Longrightarrow\quad E_{i,j}\;=\;\Big[E_{i}\,,\big[E_{i}\,,{[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]}_{q_{ij}}\big]_{q_{ii}\,q_{ij}}\Big]_{q_{ii}^{\,2}\,q_{ij}}

(4.15)

\displaystyle a_{i,j}=-3

\displaystyle\quad\Longrightarrow\quad E_{i,j}\;=\;\bigg[E_{i}\,,\Big[E_{i}\,,\big[E_{i}\,,{[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]}_{q_{ij}}\big]_{q_{ii}\,q_{ij}}\Big]_{q_{ii}^{\,2}\,q_{ij}}\bigg]_{q_{ii}^{\,3}\,q_{ij}}

(4.16)

In more general terms, the iterated
q
–supercommutators
arise from a
braided
adjoint action.
This braiding is the canonical one given in the category of super
Yetter-Drindfeld modules over the Cartan subalgebra.

The following two results underscore that the dependence of FoMpQUSEA’s
on realizations (which includes that on the multiparameter matrix) is functorial:

Proposition 4.2.4
.

Let
\,P\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
, and

\,p:I\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{2}\,
a parity function.
If both
{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}
and
\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime}
are realizations of
P
and

\,\underline{\phi}:{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime}\,

is a morphism between them, then there exists a unique morphism

\;U_{\underline{\phi}}:U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\!,\,p}_{\!P,\,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime}\!,\,p}_{\!P,\,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\;
of unital topological

\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–algebras
that extends the morphism
\,\phi:\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\mathfrak{h}^{\prime\prime}\,

of
\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–modules given by

\underline{\phi}\,
;  moreover,

\,U_{\underline{\operatorname{id}}_{\mathcal{R}}}=\operatorname{id}_{U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})}\,
and

\;U_{\underline{\phi}^{\prime}\circ\,\underline{\phi}}=U_{\underline{\phi}^{\prime}}\circ U_{\underline{\phi}}\;

(whenever
\,\underline{\phi}^{\prime}\circ\,\underline{\phi}\,
is defined).
Therefore, the construction
\,{\mathcal{R}}\mapsto U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,

— for any fixed
P
and
p
— is functorial in
{\mathcal{R}}\,
.

Moreover, if
\underline{\phi}
is an epimorphism,
resp. a monomorphism, then
U_{\underline{\phi}}

is an epimorphism, resp. a monomorphism, as well.

Finally, for any morphism
\,\underline{\phi}:{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime}\,
,
the kernel
\,\operatorname{Ker}\big(U_{\underline{\phi}}\big)

of
\,U_{\underline{\phi}}
is the two-sided ideal in

\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\!,\,p}_{\!P,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})
generated by

\,\operatorname{Ker}(\phi)\,
, and the latter is central in
\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\!,\,p}_{\!P,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\,
.

Proof.

This is just Proposition 4.2.3 in
[
GG3
]
, but for the
addition of the parity function, which nevertheless is harmless.
∎

Corollary 4.2.5
.

With notation as above, if
\;{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\cong{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime}\,
then
\;U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\!,\,p}_{P,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\cong U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime}\!,\,p}_{P,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\;
.

In particular, all FoMpQUSEA’s built upon split realizations, respectively small realizations, of the same matrix
P
and sharing the same rank of
\,\mathfrak{h}
and the same parity function
p\,
, are isomorphic to each other — hence they are independent (up to isomorphisms) of the specific realization, but only depend on

P
, on the rank of
\,\mathfrak{h}\,
, and on the parity function.

Proof.

Again, this is essentially Corollary 4.2.4 in
[
GG3
]
.
∎

Examples 4.2.6
.

(Yamane’s QUESA’s as examples of FoMpQUESA’s)

Fix a Cartan super-datum
(A,p)
as in Definition
2.1.2
, with

D
the diagonal matrix such that
\,DA\,
is symmetric, and a realization

\;{\mathcal{R}}\,:=\,\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\;
of
\,P:=DA\,
.

(a)
Assume that
{\mathcal{R}}
is straight and such that
\,T_{i}^{+}=T_{i}^{-}\,

for all
\,i\in I\,
. Then
\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
is nothing but the quantized universal enveloping superalgebra
(=QUESA) introduced by Yamane in
[
Ya1
]
, and denoted by
\,U_{\hbar}(\mathcal{G})=U_{\hbar}(\Pi,p)\,
.
See also
[
Ge1
]
for a further study of these QUESA’s.

(b)
Assume that
{\mathcal{R}}
is straight and split. Then
\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,

is nothing but the “quantum double version” of Yamane’s
\,U_{\hbar}(\mathcal{G})=U_{\hbar}(\Pi,p)\,
,
again introduced in
[
Ya1
]
where it is denoted by
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\,
.

(c)
Assume that
{\mathcal{R}}
is just straight. Then
\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,

is a suitable quotient (by an ideal generated by a submodule of the “Cartan subalgebra”) of
the “quantum double version” of Yamane’s QUESA, i.e. the
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
mentioned in
(b)
above.
We refer to such an example as a “Yamane’s QUESA”.

4.2.7
.

Hopf superalgebra structure on FoMpQUESA’s.
