The examples of FoMpQUESA’s from Yamane’s work can be modified so to yield more examples.

Let us begin with
(A\,,p\,)
,
\,P_{A}:=DA\,
and a realization

\;{\mathcal{R}}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\;
of
P_{A}\,
, as in Definition
4.2.2
;
we assume in addition that
\,{\mathcal{R}}
be straight, so that
\,U_{P_{A},\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\,

is just a “Yamane’s QUESA”, as in Example
4.2.6
(c)
above.
From Yamane’s work
[
Ya1
]
we know that
\,U_{P_{A},\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\,

bears indeed a
Hopf structure
which is given on (super)algebra generators by the formulas

\begin{aligned} \Delta\big(E_{\ell}\big)\,=\,E_{\ell}\otimes 1\,+\,e^{+\hbar\,T_{\ell}^{+}}\otimes E_{\ell}\\
\Delta\big(T\big)\,=\,T\otimes 1\,+\,1\otimes T\hskip 25.0pt\\
\Delta\big(F_{\ell}\big)\,=\,F_{\ell}\otimes e^{-\hbar\,T_{\ell}^{-}}\,+\,1\otimes F_{\ell}\end{aligned}\qquad

(4.17)

(for all
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
,
\,\ell\in I
) for the coproduct,
and for the counit and antipode by

\displaystyle\epsilon\big(E_{\ell}\big)\,=\,0\;\;\;,\qquad

\displaystyle\;\quad\epsilon\big(T\big)\,=\,0\;\;\;,

\displaystyle\;\qquad\epsilon\big(F_{\ell}\big)\,=\,0\;\;\;\;

(4.18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}\big(E_{\ell}\big)\,=\,-e^{-\hbar\,T_{\ell}^{+}}E_{\ell}\;\;,

\displaystyle\;\;\;\mathcal{S}\big(T\big)\,=\,-T\;\;,

\displaystyle\;\;\;\mathcal{S}\big(F_{\ell}\big)\,=\,-F_{\ell}\,e^{+\hbar\,T_{\ell}^{-}}\qquad

(4.19)

Now we fix in
\mathfrak{h}
any
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–basis
\,{\big\{H_{g}\big\}}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\,
,
where
\,|\mathcal{G}|=\operatorname{rk}(\mathfrak{h})=t\,
. Pick an antisymmetric
(t\times t)
–matrix

\;\Phi=\big(\phi_{gk}\big)_{g,k\in\mathcal{G}}\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\;
,  and set

\mathfrak{J}_{\Phi}\;:=\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{g,k=1}^{t}}\phi_{gk}\,H_{g}\otimes H_{k}\;\in\;\mathfrak{h}\otimes\mathfrak{h}\;\subseteq\;U^{\mathcal{R}}_{\!P_{A},\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\otimes U^{\mathcal{R}}_{\!P_{A},\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})

By direct check, we see that the element

{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}\,\;:=\;\,e^{\,\hbar\,2^{-1}\mathfrak{J}_{\Phi}}\,\;=\;\,\exp\Big(\hskip 1.0pt\hbar\,\,2^{-1}\,{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\phi_{gk}\,H_{g}\otimes H_{k}\Big)

(4.20)

in
\,U^{\mathcal{R}}_{\!P_{A},\,\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\,\widehat{\otimes}\,U^{\mathcal{R}}_{\!P_{A},\,\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\,

is actually a
twist
for
U_{P_{A},\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\,
, in the sense of §
4.1.5
.
Using it, we construct a new (topological) Hopf algebra
\,{\big(\,U_{P_{A},\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\,
,
isomorphic to
U_{P_{A},\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})
as an algebra but with a new, twisted coalgebra structure,
as in §
4.1.5
. A direct calculation yields formulas for the new coproduct on generators, namely

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad\Delta^{\!{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(E_{\ell}\big)\;=\;E_{\ell}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}^{+1}\,+\,e^{+\hbar\,T^{+}_{\ell}}\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,\ell}^{+1}\otimes E_{\ell}\quad\qquad\big(\,\forall\;\;\ell\in I\,\big)

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\Delta^{\!{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(T\big)\;=\;T\otimes 1\,+\,1\otimes T\quad\quad\qquad\qquad\big(\,\forall\;\;T\in\mathfrak{h}\,\big)

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad\Delta^{\!{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(F_{\ell}\big)\;=\;F_{\ell}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}^{-1}\,e^{-\hbar\,T^{-}_{\ell}}+\,\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,\ell}^{-1}\otimes F_{\ell}\quad\qquad\big(\,\forall\;\;\ell\in I\,\big)

with

\;\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}:=e^{+\hbar\,2^{-1}\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}H_{k}}\;
,

\;\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,\ell}:=e^{+\hbar\,2^{-1}\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})\,\phi_{kg}H_{k}}=\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}^{-1}\;
for
\,\ell\in I\,
.
Similarly, the “twisted” antipode
\mathcal{S}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}
and the
counit
\epsilon^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\!:=\epsilon
are given by

\begin{matrix}\qquad\mathcal{S}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(E_{\ell}\big)\,=\,-e^{-\hbar\,T^{+}_{\ell}}\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,\ell}^{-1}\,E_{\ell}\,\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}^{-1}\quad,&\qquad\epsilon^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(E_{\ell}\big)\,=\,0\qquad\qquad\big(\,\forall\;\;\ell\in I\,\big)\\
\qquad\mathcal{S}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(T\big)\,=\,-T\quad,\phantom{\Big|^{|}}&\qquad\epsilon^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(T\big)\,=\,0\qquad\qquad\big(\,\forall\;\;T\in\mathfrak{h}\,\big)\\
\qquad\mathcal{S}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(F_{\ell}\big)\,=\,-\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,\ell}^{+1}\,F_{\ell}\,\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}^{+1}\,e^{+\hbar\,T^{-}_{\ell}}\quad,&\qquad\epsilon^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(F_{\ell}\big)\,=\,0\qquad\qquad\big(\,\forall\;\;\ell\in I\,\big)\end{matrix}

In short, starting from Yamane’s QUESA
U_{P_{A},\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})
we get,
via a twist deformation process, a new Hopf superalgebra
{\big(U_{P_{A},\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\,
;
moreover, one immediately see that, by construction, this
{\big(U_{P_{A},\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\,

is yet another QUESA (in the sense of §
4.1.2
).
Again by construction, this QUESA is of “multiparametric nature”, in that it depends on the entries of

\Phi
as “parameters”. Indeed, by means of a suitable change of generators
(hence a change of presentation), the following result shows that

{\big(U_{P_{A},\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}

is in fact a FoMpQUESA in the sense of Definition
4.2.2
:

Proposition 4.2.8
.

Let
(A,p)
be a Cartan super-datum, and
{\mathcal{R}}
a realization of
\,P_{A}:=DA\,
;
we assume that
\,{\mathcal{R}}
be straight, so

\,U_{P_{A},\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\,
is just a “Yamane’s QUESA”, as in
Example
4.2.6
(c). We fix a twist

\;{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}\,:=\,\exp\Big(\hskip 1.0pt\hbar\,\,2^{-1}\,{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\phi_{gk}\,H_{g}\otimes H_{k}\Big)\;

as above, and we let
\,{\big(U_{P_{A},\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\,
be the corresponding deformed
Hopf superalgebra.

Then there exists an isomorphism of topological superalgebras

f^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\;:\;U_{\!{(P_{A})}_{\Phi},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\,\;{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow}\limits^{\cong}}}\;\,{\big(\,U_{\!P_{A},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}

given by

\;E_{i}\,\mapsto\,E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}:=\,\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,i}^{-1}\,E_{i}\;,\,\;T\,\mapsto\,T\,
and
\;F_{i}\,\mapsto\,F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}:=\,F_{i}\,\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,i}^{+1}\;

with

\;\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,i}:=e^{+\hbar\,2^{-1}\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{i}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}H_{k}}\;

and

\;\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,i}:=e^{+\hbar\,2^{-1}\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{i}(H_{g})\,\phi_{kg}H_{k}}\,\big(=\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}^{-1}\,\big)\;

for all
\,i\in I\,
,
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
,  where
\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}
and
P_{\Phi}
are the twist deformations (via
\Phi
) of
\,{\mathcal{R}}
and
P\,
, as in Definition
2.2.3
.

Proof.

It is clear that the
E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}
’s, the
T
’s and the
F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}
’s (for
\,i\in I\,
and
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
) altogether generate
{\big(\,U_{\!P_{A},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}
, as the latter as an algebra is just
U_{\!P_{A},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\,
. So we find a complete presentation of this algebra just by rewriting with respect to these new generators the old relations among the old ones, i.e. the
E_{i}
’s, the
T
’s and the
F_{i}
’s. Hereafter we present the explicit computations for some of these relations, the other ones can be easily filled in by the interested reader.

First of all, from the relations in (
4.3
) one gets at once identical relations with the
E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}
’s, resp. the
F_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}
’s, replacing the
E_{j}
’s, resp. the
F_{j}
’s.

Second, direct computations give

\hfil\displaystyle[E_{i}\,,F_{j}]\,-\,\delta_{i,j}\,\frac{\,e^{+\hbar\,T_{i}^{+}}-e^{-\hbar\,T_{i}^{-}}\,}{\,q_{i}^{+1}-q_{i}^{-1}\,}\;=\;\big[\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,,F_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,\mathcal{K}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{-1}\big]\,-\,\delta_{i,j}\,\frac{\,e^{+\hbar\,T_{i}^{+}}-e^{-\hbar\,T_{i}^{-}}\,}{\,q_{i}^{+1}-q_{i}^{-1}\,}\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\;\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,F_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,\mathcal{K}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{-1}\,-\,{(-1)}^{p(i)\,p(j)}\,F_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,\mathcal{K}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{-1}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,-\,\delta_{i,j}\,\frac{\,e^{+\hbar\,T_{i}^{+}}-e^{-\hbar\,T_{i}^{-}}\,}{\,q_{i}^{+1}-q_{i}^{-1}\,}\;=\qquad

\hfil\displaystyle=\;\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,F_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,\mathcal{K}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{-1}\,-\,{(-1)}^{p(i)\,p(j)}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,F_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,\mathcal{K}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{-1}\,-\,\delta_{i,j}\,\frac{\,e^{+\hbar\,T_{i}^{+}}-e^{-\hbar\,T_{i}^{-}}\,}{\,q_{i}^{+1}-q_{i}^{-1}\,}\;=\quad

\hfil\displaystyle\quad=\;\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,\big(E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,F_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,-\,{(-1)}^{p(i)\,p(j)}\,F_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)\,\mathcal{K}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{-1}\,-\,\delta_{i,j}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,\frac{\,e^{+\hbar\,T_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+}}-e^{-\hbar\,T_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{-}}\,}{\,q_{i}^{+1}-q_{i}^{-1}\,}\,\mathcal{K}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{-1}\;=

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,\left(E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,F_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,-\,{(-1)}^{p(i)\,p(j)}\,F_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,-\,\delta_{i,j}\,\frac{\,e^{+\hbar\,T_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+}}-e^{-\hbar\,T_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{-}}\,}{\,q_{i}^{+1}-q_{i}^{-1}\,}\right)\,\mathcal{K}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{-1}\;=

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,\left(\big[E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},F_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big]\,-\,\delta_{i,j}\,\frac{\,e^{+\hbar\,T_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+}}-e^{-\hbar\,T_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{-}}\,}{\,q_{i}^{+1}-q_{i}^{-1}\,}\right)\,\mathcal{K}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{-1}

from which we see that the “old” relation

\,\;\displaystyle{[E_{i}\,,F_{j}]\,-\,\delta_{i,j}\,\frac{\,e^{+\hbar\,T_{i}^{+}}-e^{-\hbar\,T_{i}^{-}}\,}{\,q_{i}^{+1}-q_{i}^{-1}\,}\;=\;0}\;\,

is equivalent to the new one, that is

\,\;\displaystyle{\big[E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},F_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big]\,-\,\delta_{i,j}\,\frac{\,e^{+\hbar\,T_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+}}-e^{-\hbar\,T_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{-}}\,}{\,q_{i}^{+1}-q_{i}^{-1}\,}\;\,=\;\,0}\;\,
.

Third, recall that all relations from (
4.5
) through (
4.12
) can be written in terms of iterated
q
–supercommutators (cf. §
4.2.3
). Now, for a single such
q
–supercommutator we have, for all
\,i,j\in I\,
and
\,\nu\in{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
,

\hfil\displaystyle{\big[E_{i}\,,E_{j}\big]}_{\nu}=\,{\big[\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{+1}\,E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big]}_{\nu}=\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{+1}\,E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}-{(-1)}^{p(i)\,p(j)}\nu\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{+1}\,E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad=\;e^{-2^{-1}\hbar\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{j}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}\,\alpha_{i}(H_{k})}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{+1}\,E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,-\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill-\,{(-1)}^{p(i)\,p(j)}\nu\,e^{-2^{-1}\hbar\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{i}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}\,\alpha_{j}(H_{k})}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{+1}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\;=\qquad

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad=\;e^{-2^{-1}\hbar\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{j}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}\,\alpha_{i}(H_{k})}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{+1}\,\times\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill\times\,\Big(E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,-\,{(-1)}^{p(i)\,p(j)}\nu\,e^{+\hbar\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{j}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}\,\alpha_{i}(H_{k})}\,E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\Big)\;=\qquad

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;e^{-2^{-1}\hbar\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{j}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}\,\alpha_{i}(H_{k})}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{+1}\,{\big[E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big]}_{\nu\,e^{+\hbar\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{j}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}\,\alpha_{i}(H_{k})}}

i.e., using notation
\;k_{i{}j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}:=e^{+\hbar\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{j}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}\,\alpha_{i}(H_{k})}\;
,  we find

{\big[E_{i}\,,E_{j}\big]}_{\nu}\,\;=\;\,{\big(k_{i{}j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}^{-1/2}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{+1}\,{\big[E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big]}_{\nu\,k_{i{}j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}}

(4.21)

Similarly, a parallel calculation gives

{\big[F_{i}\,,F_{j}\big]}_{\nu}\,\;=\;\,{\big[F_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},F_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big]}_{\nu\,k_{j{}i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}}\,\mathcal{K}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{-1}\,\mathcal{K}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{-1}\,{\big(k_{j{}i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}^{-1/2}

(4.22)

More in general, a similar computation yields

E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i_{1}}\cdots E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i_{\ell}}\;=\;\left({\textstyle\prod\limits_{1\leq r\leq s\leq\ell}}q^{-2^{-1}\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{i_{s}}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}\,\alpha_{i_{r}}(H_{k})}\right)\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i_{1}}^{+1}\cdots\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i_{\ell}}^{+1}\,E_{i_{1}}\cdots E_{i_{\ell}}

(4.23)

and then from this one can deduce formulas generalizing (
4.21
)
that express any
q
–supercommutator of products of the
E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}
’s
in terms of a similar supercommutator of products of the
E_{i}
’s. A similar outcome holds with “
F
” replacing “
E
” throughout.

Applying these arguments — in particular, applying the like of
(
4.21
) — one can deduce all relations from
(
4.5
) through (
4.12
) for the
E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}
’s,
resp. the
F_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}
’s, from the parallel relations for the
E_{j}
’s,
resp. the
F_{j}
’s. Hereafter we show that in a few cases.

For instance, the original relation (
4.7
) in

\,U_{\!P_{A},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\,
reads

\,{\big[E_{i}\,,E_{j}\big]}=0\,
because
\,k_{i,j}=1\,
when

\,a_{i,j}=0\,
for the matrix
P_{\scriptscriptstyle A}
(as it is symmetric).
But then this relation is
equivalent
, by (
4.21
),
to
\,{\big[E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big]}_{k_{i,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}}=0\,
,  q.e.d.

Similarly, consider the case of the quantum Serre relation (
4.5
)
for
\,a_{i,j}=-2\,
. Direct computations — taking into account
(
4.15
), (
4.21
) and (
2.2
),
that in particular imply
\,q_{i,j}\,k_{i,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}=e^{+\hbar\,p_{i,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}}=:q_{i,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,
— give us

\hfil\displaystyle E_{i,j}\;=\;\Big[E_{i}\,,\big[E_{i}\,,{[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]}_{q_{ij}}\big]_{q_{ii}\,q_{ij}}\Big]_{q_{ii}^{\,2}\,q_{ij}}\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\;{\big(k_{i,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}^{-1/2}\,\Big[E_{i}\,,\big[E_{i}\,,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{+1}\,{[E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,,E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}]}_{q_{i,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}}\big]_{q_{ii}\,q_{ij}}\Big]_{q_{ii}^{\,2}\,q_{ij}}\;=\hskip 45.0pt

\hfil\displaystyle=\;{\big(k_{i,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}^{-1}\,\Big[E_{i}\,,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+2}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{+1}\,\big[E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,,{[E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,,E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}]}_{q_{i,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}}\,\big]_{q_{ii}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,q_{ij}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}}\,\Big]_{q_{ii}^{\,2}\,q_{ij}}\;=

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;{\big(k_{i,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}^{-3/2}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+3}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{+1}\,\Big[E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,,\big[E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,,{[E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,,E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}]}_{q_{i,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}}\,\big]_{q_{ii}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,q_{ij}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}}\,\Big]_{{(q_{ii}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi})}^{\,2}\,q_{ij}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}}\;=\qquad

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;{\big(k_{i,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}^{-3/2}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+3}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{+1}\,E_{i,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}

from which we infer that the quantum Serre relation
\,E_{i,j}=0\,
for the
E_{\ell}
’s
is equivalent to the similar quantum Serre relation
\,E_{i,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}=0\,
for the
E_{\ell}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}
’s.

The other quantum Serre relations are disposed of in a similar way.

Finally, as to the other relations we consider for instance the case of (
4.8
).
In case
\,\,P=P_{\scriptscriptstyle A}\,
(which is symmetric, hence the
k_{i,j}
’s are one)
relation (
4.8
) reads just

\;\Big[{\big[{[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]}_{\nu_{j}},E_{k}\big]}_{\nu_{j+1}},E_{j}\Big]\,=\,0\;
.
Now, direct computations give

\hfil\displaystyle\Big[\big[{[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]}_{\nu_{j}},E_{k}{\big]}_{\nu_{j+1}},E_{j}\Big]\,\;=\;\,q^{-1}_{ij}\,\Big[{\big[\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,i}^{+1}\,\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,j}^{+1}\,{\big[E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big]}_{\nu_{i}k_{ij}},E_{k}\big]}_{\nu_{j+1}},E_{j}\,\Big]\,\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle=\;\,q^{-1}_{ij}\,q^{-1}_{ik}\,q^{-1}_{jk}\,\Big[\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,k}^{+1}\,\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,i}^{+1}\,\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,j}^{+1}\,{\big[{\big[E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big]}_{\nu_{j}k_{ij}},E_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big]}_{\nu_{j+1}k_{ik}k_{jk}},E_{j}\,\Big]\,\;=\qquad

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,q^{-2}_{ij}\,q^{-1}_{ik}\,q^{-1}_{jk}\,q_{kj}^{-1}\,q^{-1}_{jj}\,\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,k}^{+1}\,\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,i}^{+1}\,\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,j}^{+2}\,{\Big[{\big[{\big[E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big]}_{\nu_{j}k_{ij}},E_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big]}_{\nu_{j+1}k_{ik}k_{jk}},E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,\Big]}_{k_{ij}k_{jj}k_{kj}}\,=

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;\,q^{-2}_{ij}\,q^{-1}_{ik}\,q^{-1}_{jk}\,q_{kj}^{-1}\,q^{-1}_{jj}\,\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,k}^{+1}\,\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,i}^{+1}\,\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,j}^{+2}\,{\Big[{\big[{\big[E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big]}_{\nu_{j}k_{ij}},E_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big]}_{\nu_{j+1}k_{ik}k_{jk}},E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,\Big]}_{k_{ij}k_{kj}}

from which we eventually see that relation (
4.8
) for the
E_{\ell}
’s
is equivalent to relation (
4.8
) for the
E_{\ell}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}
’s.
Similarly one deals with relations (
4.9
) through (
4.12
).

As a different method, one can adopt the following. For any
\,b,d\in\mathbb{N}\,
a special instance of (
4.23
) reads just

\;{\big(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}\big)}^{b}\,E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j}\,{\big(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}\big)}^{d}\,=\,q^{\,\epsilon(i,j)}\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\,i}^{d+b}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\,j}^{+1}\,E_{i}^{\,b}\,E_{j}\,E_{i}^{\,d}\;

with

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\epsilon(i,j)\,:=\,-2^{-1}{{d+b}\choose 2}{\textstyle\sum\limits_{g,k=1}^{t}}\alpha_{i}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}\,\alpha_{i}(H_{k})\,-\,2^{-1}\,d{\textstyle\sum\limits_{g,k=1}^{t}}\alpha_{i}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}\,\alpha_{j}(H_{k})\,-\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill-\,2^{-1}\,b{\textstyle\sum\limits_{g,k=1}^{t}}\alpha_{j}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}\,\alpha_{i}(H_{k})\,=\,2^{-1}\,(d-b){\textstyle\sum\limits_{g,k=1}^{t}}\alpha_{j}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}\,\alpha_{i}(H_{k})

so that
\;q^{\,\epsilon(i,j)}=e^{\hbar\,2^{-1}(d-b)\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{j}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}\,\alpha_{i}(H_{k})}={\big(k_{ij}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}^{(d-b)/2}\,
. Reversing that identity, we get

\;E_{i}^{\,b}\,E_{j}\,E_{i}^{\,d}={\big(k_{ij}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}^{(d-b)/2}\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\,i}^{-(d+b)}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\,j}^{-1}\,{\big(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}\big)}^{b}\,E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j}\,{\big(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}\big)}^{d}\,
,
which for the pair
\,(b\,,d\,)=\big(1\!-\!a_{ij}\!-\!s\,,s\big)\,
reads

E_{i}^{\,1-a_{ij}-s}\,E_{j}\,E_{i}^{\,s}\;=\,{\big(k_{ij}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}^{(a_{ij}-1)/2}\,{\big(k_{ij}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}^{s}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\,i}^{\,a_{ij}-1}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\,j}^{\,-1}\,{\big(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}\big)}^{1-a_{ij}-s}\,E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j}\,{\big(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}\big)}^{s}

Finally, replacing this last identity in the left-hand side of (
4.5
) we get

\hfil\displaystyle\sum\limits_{s=0}^{1-a_{ij}}{(-1)}^{s}{\left[{{1\!-\!a_{ij}}\atop s}\right]}_{\!q_{i}}k_{ij}^{\,s}\,E_{i}^{1-a_{ij}-s}E_{j}E_{i}^{s}\;=\;{\big(k_{ij}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}^{(a_{ij}-1)/2}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\,i}^{\,a_{ij}-1}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\,j}^{\,-1}\,\;\times\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill\times\,\sum\limits_{s=0}^{1-a_{ij}}{(-1)}^{s}{\left[{{1\!-\!a_{ij}}\atop s}\right]}_{\!q_{i}}q_{ij}^{+s/2\,}q_{ji}^{-s/2}\,{\big(k_{ij}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}^{s}\,{\big(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}\big)}^{1-a_{ij}-s}\,E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j}\,{\big(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}\big)}^{s}\;=\quad

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad=\;{\big(k_{ij}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}^{(a_{ij}-1)/2}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\,i}^{\,a_{ij}-1}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\,j}^{\,-1}\,\;\times\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill\times\,\sum\limits_{s=0}^{1-a_{ij}}{(-1)}^{s}{\left[{{1\!-\!a_{ij}}\atop s}\right]}_{\!q_{i}}{\big(q^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{ij}\big)}^{+s/2\,}{\big(q^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{ji}\big)}^{-s/2}\,{\big(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}\big)}^{1-a_{ij}-s}\,E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j}\,{\big(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}\big)}^{s}

— since
\,k_{ij}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}={\big(k_{ji}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}^{-1}\,
and

\,q_{ij}\,k_{ij}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}=q_{i,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,
— and then we notice that the last factor
(i.e., the last line above) is just the left-hand side of the quantum Serre relation (
4.5
)
for the
E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}
’s: so we deduce that (
4.5
) for the
E_{\ell}
’s
— inside
U_{\!P_{A},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}(\mathfrak{g})
— is indeed equivalent to (
4.5
)
for the
E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}
’s inside
{\big(U_{\!P_{A},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}
,
and we are done.

In the same way, replacing the
E_{k}
’s with the
F_{k}
’s, one disposes of all relations involving the

F_{k}
’s (alone) turning into relations involving the
F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{k}
’s (again alone).

To sum up, the previous analysis prove that the
E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}
’s, the
T
’s and the

F_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}
’s altogether do generate the algebra
{\big(U_{\!P_{A},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}

and
the original ideal of defining relations of
U_{\!P_{A},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}(\mathfrak{g})

— described in terms of the original generators — is also generated by the same relations
but

written
in terms of the
new
generators and of the deformed multiparameter matrix

P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,
.
In a nutshell, using
\big\{\,E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,,T,F_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,\big|\,i\in I,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,\big\}\,

as set of generators, we get for
{\big(U_{\!P_{A},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}

nothing but the defining presentation of
\,U_{\!P_{\Phi},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\,
.
In a different wording, this means exactly that there exists a well-defined isomorphism

\,f_{\scriptscriptstyle P}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,
of topological superalgebras as given in the claim.
∎

A direct consequence of Proposition
4.2.8
is that the FoMpQUESA

\,U_{\!P_{\Phi},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\,

inherits, via pull-back through the isomorphism
\,f^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\,
,
a full structure of Hopf superalgebra, more precisely one of QUESA. In fact, by a “reverse–engineering argument”,
we see that this result extends to
all
FoMpQUESA’s
(hence the very use of the termi-nology “FoMpQUESA” is indeed correct!).
This is the content of our next result:

Theorem 4.2.9
.

Every FoMpQUESA
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
is indeed a (topological) Hopf superalgebra, whose Hopf structure
is given on (super)algebra generators by the formulas

\begin{aligned} \Delta\big(E_{\ell}\big)\,=\,E_{\ell}\otimes 1\,+\,e^{+\hbar\,T_{\ell}^{+}}\otimes E_{\ell},\\
\Delta\big(T\big)\,=\,T\otimes 1\,+\,1\otimes T,\hskip 25.0pt\\
\Delta\big(F_{\ell}\big)\,=\,F_{\ell}\otimes e^{-\hbar\,T_{\ell}^{-}}\,+\,1\otimes F_{\ell},\end{aligned}\qquad

(4.24)

(for all
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
,
\,\ell\in I
) for the coproduct, and for the counit and antipode by

\displaystyle\epsilon\big(E_{\ell}\big)\,=\,0\;\;\;,\qquad

\displaystyle\;\quad\epsilon\big(T\big)\,=\,0\;\;\;,

\displaystyle\;\qquad\epsilon\big(F_{\ell}\big)\,=\,0\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(4.25)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}\big(E_{\ell}\big)\,=\,-e^{-\hbar\,T_{\ell}^{+}}E_{\ell}\;\;,

\displaystyle\;\;\;\mathcal{S}\big(T\big)\,=\,-T\;\;,

\displaystyle\;\;\;\mathcal{S}\big(F_{\ell}\big)\,=\,-F_{\ell}\,e^{+\hbar\,T_{\ell}^{-}}\;\;\;

(4.26)

Proof.

By assumption, we have a fixed Cartan super-datum
\,(A,p)\,
, as in Definition
2.1.2
, such that the symmetric part of
P
is
\,P_{s}:=2^{-1}\big(P+P^{\,\scriptscriptstyle T}\big)=D\,A=:P_{A}\,
, with
\,P={\big(\,p_{i,j}\,\big)}_{i,j\in I}\,
and
\,{\mathcal{R}}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,
a realization of
P
.

By Lemma
2.1.8
, there is a
straight
realization
\;\tilde{{\mathcal{R}}}:=\big(\,\tilde{\mathfrak{h}}\,,\tilde{\Pi}\,,{\tilde{\Pi}}^{\vee}\,\big)\,
of the matrix
P
and an epimorphism of realizations
\;\underline{\pi}:\tilde{{\mathcal{R}}}\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{R}}\;
. Up to possibly enlarging
\tilde{\mathfrak{h}}\,
, we can assume that
\,t:=\text{rk}\big(\tilde{\mathfrak{h}}\big)\geq 3n-\text{rk}\big(P_{A}\big)\,
. Then, by Proposition
2.2.6
(a)
— with
\,P^{\prime}=P_{A}:=DA\,
— we get that there exists some
\,\Phi\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
such that
\,P_{A}=P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,
while
\,{\mathcal{R}}_{A}:=\tilde{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\,\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,
is a straight realization of
\,P_{A}=P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,
. Hence, conversely, we have also
\,P={\big(P_{A}\big)}_{\scriptscriptstyle-\Phi}\,
and
\,\tilde{{\mathcal{R}}}={\big({\mathcal{R}}_{A}\big)}_{\scriptscriptstyle-\Phi}\,
. Then by Proposition
4.2.8
there exists an isomorphism of topological superalgebras

f^{\scriptscriptstyle-\Phi}_{\scriptscriptstyle P_{A}}\;:\;U_{\!P,\,\hbar}^{\tilde{R},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})=U_{\!{(P_{A})}_{-\Phi},\,\hbar}^{{({\mathcal{R}}_{A})}_{\scriptscriptstyle-\Phi},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\,\;{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow}\limits^{\cong}}}\;\,{\big(\,U_{\!P_{A},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{A},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{-\Phi}}

which is described by explicit formulas as in Proposition
4.2.8
— up to paying attention to switching
\Phi
to
-\Phi\,
. Eventually, we can pull back onto
U_{\!P,\,\hbar}^{\tilde{R},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})
— via
f^{\scriptscriptstyle-\Phi}_{\scriptscriptstyle P_{A}}
— the Hopf structure of
{\big(\,U_{\!P_{A},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{A},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{-\Phi}}\,
:   then, when tracking the whole construction, the explicit description of this Hopf structure on the generators of
U_{\!P,\,\hbar}^{\tilde{R},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})
is given exactly by the formulas in the claim.

Finally, Proposition
4.2.4
gives an epimorphism
\;U_{\underline{\pi}}:U^{\,\tilde{{\mathcal{R}}},\,p}_{\!P,\,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\;
of unital topological
\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–algebras that extends the epimorphism
\,\pi:\tilde{\mathfrak{h}}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{h}\,

of
\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–modules given by
\underline{\pi}\,
;  moreover,
\,\operatorname{Ker}\!\big(\,\underline{\pi}\,\big)\,
is the two–sided ideal in
U^{\,\tilde{{\mathcal{R}}},\,p}_{\!P,\,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})
generated by
\operatorname{Ker}(\pi)\,
,  and the latter is central in
U^{\,\tilde{{\mathcal{R}}},\,p}_{\!P,\,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\,
. But then, since
\operatorname{Ker}(\pi)
is made of primitive elements,
\,\operatorname{Ker}\!\big(\,\underline{\pi}\,\big)\,
is automatically a Hopf ideal: therefore, the quotient algebra
\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})=U^{\,\tilde{{\mathcal{R}}},\,p}_{\!P,\,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\Big/\operatorname{Ker}\!\big(\,\underline{\pi}\,\big)\,
inherits via the algebra epimorphism
U_{\underline{\pi}}\,
the whole Hopf structure of
U^{\,\tilde{{\mathcal{R}}},\,p}_{\!P,\,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\,
,  hence we are done.
∎

Corollary 4.2.10
.

The Cartan subalgebra
U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})
and the Borel subsuperalgebras
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}},p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{b}_{\pm})
are actually (topological)
Hopf
subsuperalgebras of
\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
, their Hopf structure being described again via formulas (
4.24
), (
4.25
) and (
4.26
). ∎

The next result is a straightforward “upgrading” Proposition
4.2.4
:

Proposition 4.2.11
.

Let
\,\underline{\phi}:{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\!\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\!{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime}\,

be a morphism between realizations of a same matrix
\,P\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
.
Then the morphism of unital topological
\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–superalgebras

\,U_{\underline{\phi}}:U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},\,p}_{\!P,\,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime},\,p}_{\!P,\,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\,

granted by Proposition
4.2.4
is indeed a morphism of (unital topological)
Hopf

\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–superalgebras.

If we set
\;\mathfrak{k}:=\operatorname{Ker}(\phi)\,
, then
\,U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{k})\,
is a unital,
\hbar
–adically complete
\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–subsuper-algebra of
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},\,p}_{\!P,\,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})
which is a central Hopf subsuperalgebra, isomorphic to a quantum Cartan (in the sense of Definition
4.2.2
(c)), and
\;\operatorname{Ker}(U_{\underline{\phi}})=U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},p}_{\!P,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\,{U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{k})}^{+}\,
— where
\,{U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{k})}^{+}\,
is the augmentation ideal of
\,U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{k})\,
. In particular, if
\,U_{\underline{\phi}}
is an
epi
morphism, then
\;U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime\prime}}_{\!P,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\,\cong\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},\,p}_{\!P,\,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\Big/U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},\,p}_{\!P,\,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\,{U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{k})}^{+}\;
.
\square

We conclude this subsection proving that every FoMpQUESA is indeed a QUESA in the sense of §
4.1.2
: later on, in §
6
, we will study in detail their semiclassical limit.

Theorem 4.2.12
.

Every FoMpQUESA is a QUESA.

Proof.

Let
\,U_{\hbar}:=U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
be any given FoMpQUESA, and let
\;U_{0}:=U_{\hbar}\Big/\hbar\,U_{\hbar}\;
denote its semiclassical limit; also, for any
\,X\in U_{\hbar}\,
we set
\,\overline{X}:=X\;\big(\,\text{mod}\;\hbar\,U_{\hbar}\,\big)\,
for its coset modulo
\,\hbar\,U_{\hbar}\,
in
U_{0}\,
.

First of all,
U_{\hbar}
is topologically free (as a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–module). Indeed, assume first that
\,{\mathcal{R}}
is straight and
\mathfrak{h}
is “large enough”, namely
\,\text{rk}(\mathfrak{h})\geq 3\,n-\text{rk}(DA)\
:  then — see the proof of Theorem
4.2.9
—
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
is isomorphic (as a topological algebra) to some Yamane’s QUESAs, and the latter is known to be topologically free, by
[
Ya1
]
, hence the same holds true for
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
. The general case (for any
\mathfrak{h}
and any
\,{\mathcal{R}}\,
) then is obtained like in the proof of Theorem
4.2.9
by reducing to that of some straight realization with large enough Cartan (sub)algebra (for more details, see the proof of the non-super case in
[
GG3
]
, Theorem 6.1.4 — the present case is similar).

Second, as
U_{\hbar}
is generated by the
E_{i}
’s,
F_{i}
’s and
T
’s (
\,i\in I\,
,
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
),  it follows that
U_{0}
is generated by the
\overline{E_{i}}
’s,
\overline{F_{i}}
’s and
T
’s. Moreover, the formulas for the coproduct in
U_{\hbar}
imply that the latter generators are
primitive
in
U_{0}\,
. Therefore, the Hopf algebra
U_{0}
is generated by its subspace
P(U_{0})
of primitive elements, hence — by Milnor–Moore Theorem for Hopf superalgebras cf.
[
Ko
, Proposition 3.2]
— we conclude that
P(U_{0})
is a Lie superalgebra and
U_{0}
is its universal enveloping superalgebra, hence we are done.
∎

We finish with a typical “triangular decomposition result” for our FoMpQUESA’s:

Theorem 4.2.13
.

Every FoMpQUESA
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
admits
triangular decompositions
such as

U^{\,{\mathcal{R}},p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{n}_{+})\otimes U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})\otimes U^{\,{\mathcal{R}},p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{n}_{-})\,\cong\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,\cong\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}},p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{n}_{-})\otimes U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})\otimes U^{\,{\mathcal{R}},p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{n}_{+})

and similarly for Borel sub-superalgebras we have

U^{\,{\mathcal{R}},p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{n}_{\pm})\otimes U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})\,\;\cong\;\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}},p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{b}_{\pm})\,\cong\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})\otimes U^{\,{\mathcal{R}},p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{n}_{\pm})

Proof.

The claim follows from a standard, fairly “classical” argument, which is detailed in the
Third Proof
in
[
GG3
, §4.4.9]
; that one is written there for the non-super setup, but it applies
verbatim
to the present context as well.
∎

5.
Deformations of FoMpQUESA’s

After introducing formal MpQUEAs in the previous section, now we go and study their deformations, either by twist or by polar–
2
–cocycle (both of “toral type”, say).

5.1.
Deformations of FoMpQUESAs by toral twists

We discuss now suitable twist deformations (of “toral type”) of FoMpQUESAs,
proving that they are again FoMpQUESAs.
In fact, this is but a generalization of the argument that we already used in
§
4.2
to
define
our FoMpQUESAs,
as they are indeed introduced as deformations by toral twists of Yamane’s QUESAs.

5.1.1
.

Toral twist deformations of
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
.

We fix a Cartan super-datum
\,(A\,,p)\,
as in Definition
2.1.2
,
a multiparameter matrix
\,P:={\big(\,p_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,

associated with
A\,
, and a realization
\,{\mathcal{R}}\,:=\,\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,
of it,
as in §
2
. In particular, we have
\,d_{i}:=p_{ii}/2\,
(
\,i\in I\,
),
and
\mathfrak{h}
is a free
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–module of finite rank
\,t:=\operatorname{rk}(\mathfrak{h})\,
. We fix in

\mathfrak{h}
any
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–basis
\,{\big\{H_{g}\big\}}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\,
, where
\,|\mathcal{G}|=\operatorname{rk}(\mathfrak{h})=t\,
.

Now let
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
be the FoMpQUESA given in Definition §
4.2.2
.
We pick
\;\Phi=\big(\phi_{gk}\big)_{g,k\in\mathcal{G}}\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\;
,  and set

\mathfrak{J}_{\Phi}\;:=\;{\textstyle\sum\limits_{g,k=1}^{t}}\phi_{gk}\,H_{g}\otimes H_{k}\;\in\;\mathfrak{h}\otimes\mathfrak{h}\;\subseteq\;U^{\mathcal{R}}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\otimes U^{\mathcal{R}}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\;.

By direct check, we see that the element

{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}\,\;:=\;\,e^{\,\hbar\,2^{-1}\mathfrak{J}_{\Phi}}\,\;=\;\,\exp\Big(\hskip 1.0pt\hbar\,\,2^{-1}\,{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\phi_{gk}\,H_{g}\otimes H_{k}\Big)

(5.1)

in
\,U^{{\mathcal{R}},p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\,\widehat{\otimes}\,U^{{\mathcal{R}},p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\,
is actually a
twist
for
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
in the sense of §
4.1.5
. Using it, we construct a new (topological) Hopf algebra
\,{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\,
, isomorphic to
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
as an algebra but with a new, twisted coalgebra structure, as in §
4.1.5
.

Our key result now is that this new Hopf algebra
\,{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\,
is yet another FoMpQUESA, and even more we know exactly which one it is:

Theorem 5.1.2
.

There exists an isomomorphism of topological Hopf algebras

f^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\;:\;U_{\!P_{\Phi},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\,\;{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow}\limits^{\cong}}}\;\,{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}

given by

\;E_{\ell}\,\mapsto\,E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}:=\,\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}^{-1}\,E_{\ell}\,,\;T\,\mapsto\,T\,
,
\;F_{\ell}\,\mapsto\,F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}:=\,F_{\ell}\,\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,\ell}^{+1}\;\;\;(\,\forall\;\ell\in I\,,\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,)\,
,
with

\;\;\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}:=e^{+\hbar\,2^{-1}\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}H_{k}}\;
,

\;\;\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,\ell}:=e^{+\hbar\,2^{-1}\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})\,\phi_{kg}H_{k}}\;\big(\!=\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}^{\,-1}\,\big)\;
.

In particular, the class of all FoMpQUESAs of any fixed Cartan type and of fixed rank
is stable by toral twist deformations. Moreover, inside it the subclass of all such
FoMpQUESAs associated with
straight
, resp.
small
, realizations is stable as well.

Similar, parallel statements hold true for the Borel FoMpQUEAs, i.e. there exist isomorphisms

\;f^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\!\scriptscriptstyle P,\pm}:U_{\!P_{\Phi},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\,p}(\mathfrak{b}_{\pm})\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow}\limits^{\cong}}}\,{\big(\,U_{\!P,\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\mathfrak{b}_{\pm})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\;

given by formulas as above.

Proof.

The proof follows closely in the footprints of Proposition
4.2.8
, so we can be somewhat quicker now. First of all,
{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}
coincides with
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
as an algebra, so we can take the generators of the latter as generators of the former too.

Direct calculation yields explicit formulas for the new coproduct
\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}
,
namely

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(E_{\ell}\big)\;=\;E_{\ell}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}^{+1}\,+\,e^{+\hbar\,T^{+}_{\ell}}\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,\ell}^{+1}\otimes E_{\ell}\quad\qquad\big(\,\forall\;\;\ell\in I\,\big)

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(T\big)\;=\;T\otimes 1\,+\,1\otimes T\quad\quad\qquad\qquad\big(\,\forall\;\;T\in\mathfrak{h}\,\big)

\hfil\displaystyle\qquad\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(F_{\ell}\big)\;=\;F_{\ell}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}^{-1}\,e^{-\hbar\,T^{-}_{\ell}}+\,\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,\ell}^{-1}\otimes F_{\ell}\quad\qquad\big(\,\forall\;\;\ell\in I\,\big)

with

\,\;\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}:=e^{+\hbar\,2^{-1}\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}H_{k}}\;
,

\,\;\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,\ell}:=e^{+\hbar\,2^{-1}\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})\,\phi_{kg}H_{k}}\;\big(\!=\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}^{\,-1}\,\big)
for
\ell\!\in\!I
.
Similarly, the “twisted” antipode
\mathcal{S}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}
and
the counit
\epsilon^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\!:=\epsilon
are given by

\begin{matrix}\qquad\mathcal{S}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(E_{\ell}\big)\,=\,-e^{-\hbar\,T^{+}_{\ell}}\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,\ell}^{-1}\,E_{\ell}\,\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}^{-1}\quad,&\qquad\epsilon^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(E_{\ell}\big)\,=\,0\qquad\qquad\big(\,\forall\;\;\ell\in I\,\big)\quad,\\
\qquad\mathcal{S}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(T\big)\,=\,-T\quad,\phantom{\Big|^{|}}&\qquad\epsilon^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(T\big)\,=\,0\qquad\qquad\big(\,\forall\;\;T\in\mathfrak{h}\,\big)\quad,\\
\qquad\mathcal{S}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(F_{\ell}\big)\,=\,-\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,\ell}^{+1}\,F_{\ell}\,\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}^{+1}\,e^{+\hbar\,T^{-}_{\ell}}\quad,&\qquad\epsilon^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(F_{\ell}\big)\,=\,0\qquad\qquad\big(\,\forall\;\;\ell\in I\,\big)\quad.\end{matrix}

Tiding up, from the explicit description of the coproduct
\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\,
, it follows that
\,{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\,
is generated by group-likes and skew-primitive elements; hence, it is a
pointed
Hopf superalgebra. Moreover, both Hopf superalgebras
\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
and
\,{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\,
have the same coradical and the same space of skew-primitive elements. As the coproduct is changed by twist, one sees that the skew-primitive generators of
\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
, which are
(1,g)
–primitive or
(g,1)
–primitive with respect to
\Delta\,
, for some
\,g\in G\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)\,
, become
(h,k)
–primitive for
\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\,
. Looking at the coradical filtration, and the associated graded Hopf superalgebra, one finds from that set of generators some new
(1,\ell)
–primitive or
(\ell,1)
–primitives for
{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}
. This leads to devise (new)
twisted generators
and a corresponding presentation for
{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}
, which yields a Hopf superalgebra isormorphism between
{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}
and a new FoMpQUESA with suitable multiparameter matrix and realization.

Motivated by the above analysis, we introduce now in
{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\phi}}
the “twisted” generators

\;E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}:=\,\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}^{-1}\,E_{\ell}\;
,

\;F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}:=\,F_{\ell}\,\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,\ell}^{+1}\;

(for all
\,\ell\in I\,
); clearly, these along with all the
T
’s in
\mathfrak{h}
still generate
\,{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}=U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
. Moreover, we introduce the twisted “distinguished toral elements” (or “coroots”) that were already defined in (
2.1
), i.e.
\;T^{\pm}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\ell}:=\,T^{\pm}_{\ell}\,\pm{\textstyle\sum\limits_{g,k=1}^{t}}\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})\,\phi_{kg}\,H_{k}\;
.

Finally, again from §
2.2.1
, we recall also

\,P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}:={\big(\,p^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\,
and

\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\Phi}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,\big)\,
,
with the latter being a realization of the former indeed.

As a second step, the defining relations in the superalgebra
\,{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\,
give new relations between twisted generators. Namely, by straightforward computations — for instance using that
\;\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,j}\,E_{i}=e^{\hbar\,2^{-1}\,\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{j}(H_{g})\,\phi_{kg}\alpha_{i}(H_{k})}E_{i}\,\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,j}\;
and that
\;e^{+\hbar\,T^{\pm}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}}=e^{+\hbar\,T_{i}^{\pm}}\big(\,\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,i}\,\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,i}^{-1}\,\big)^{\pm 1}\;
— one proves that inside
\,{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\,
the following identities hold true (for all
\,T,T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime}\in\mathfrak{h}\,
,
\,i\,,j\in I\,
):

\displaystyle T\,E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j}\,-\,E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j}\,T\;=\;+\alpha_{j}(T)\,E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j}\quad,\qquad T\,F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j}\,-\,F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j}\,T\;=\;-\alpha_{j}(T)\,F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j}

\displaystyle T^{\prime}\,T^{\prime\prime}\;=\;T^{\prime\prime}\,T^{\prime}\quad,\quad\qquad\big[E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i},F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j}\big]\;=\;\delta_{i,j}\,{{\;e^{+\hbar\,T^{+}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}}\,-\,e^{-\hbar\,T^{-}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}}\;}\over{\;q_{i}^{+1}-\,q_{i}^{-1}\;}},

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{s=0}^{1-a_{ij}}{(-1)}^{s}{\left[{{1-a_{ij}}\atop s}\right]}_{\!q_{i}}\!{\big(k_{ij}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}^{\!s}{\big(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}\big)}^{1-a_{ij}-s}E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j}{\big(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}\big)}^{s}\;=\;0\quad\Bigg({{i\neq j}\atop{\,p(i)\!=\!{\bar{0}}}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{s=0}^{1-a_{ij}}{(-1)}^{s}{\left[{{1-a_{ij}}\atop s}\right]}_{\!q_{i}}\!{\big(k_{ji}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}^{\!s}{\big(F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}\big)}^{1-a_{ij}-s}F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j}{\big(F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i}\big)}^{s}\;=\;0\quad\Bigg({{i\neq j}\atop{\,p(i)\!=\!{\bar{0}}}}\Bigg)

— with
\;q^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i,j}:=e^{\hbar\,p^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i,j}}\;(\,i,j\in I\,)\,
, so that
\,q^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i,i}=e^{\hbar\,p^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i,i}}=e^{\hbar\,p_{i,i}}=e^{\hbar\,2d_{i}}=q_{i}^{\,2}\,
,  as well as
\;k^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i,j}:=q^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i,j}\,q^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j,i}=e^{\hbar\,(p^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i,j}-p^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j,i})}\;(\,i,j\in I\,)\,
— and also

\big[E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i},E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j}\big]_{k^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{ij}}\,=\,0\;\;,\qquad\big[F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{i},F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{j}\big]_{k^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{ji}}\,=\,0\qquad\qquad\text{if}\quad a_{i,j}=0

as well as all other relations listed in Definition
4.2.2
, namely (
4.8
) through (
4.12
) — including their “counterparts” with the
F_{\ell}
’s replacing the
E_{\ell}
’s throughout. Indeed, apart from the first two lines (which follow from direct approach), all the other formulas follows from iterated applications of (
4.21
) — that is

\;{\big[E_{i}\,,E_{j}\big]}_{\nu}=\,{\big(k_{i,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big)}^{-1/2}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},i}^{+1}\,\mathcal{L}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},j}^{+1}\,{\big[E_{i}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},E_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big]}_{\nu\,k_{i{}j}^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}}\;

where the left-hand side now is to be read inside
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
— and (
4.22
) alike, just sticking to the very same argument that was followed in the proof of Proposition
4.2.8
.

Third, the Hopf operators on the “twisted” generators read (for
\,\ell\in I\,
,
T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
)

\begin{matrix}\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}\big)\,=\,E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}\otimes 1\,+\,e^{+\hbar\,T^{+}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\ell}}\otimes E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}\;,&\hskip 1.0pt\mathcal{S}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}\big)\,=\,-e^{-\hbar\,T^{+}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\ell}}E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}\;,&\hskip 1.0pt\epsilon^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}\big)\,=\,0\\
\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(T\big)\,=\,T\otimes 1\,+\,1\otimes T\;,&\hskip 1.0pt\mathcal{S}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(T\big)\,=\,-T\;,&\hskip 1.0pt\epsilon^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(T\big)\,=\,0\\
\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}\big)\,=\,F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}\otimes e^{-\hbar\,T^{-}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\ell}}\,+\,1\otimes F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}\;,&\hskip 1.0pt\mathcal{S}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}\big)\,=\,-F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}\,e^{+\hbar\,T^{-}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi},\ell}}\;,&\hskip 1.0pt\epsilon^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\big(F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}\big)\,=\,0\end{matrix}

At last, it is now immediate to check that mapping

E_{\ell}\,\mapsto\,E^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}:=\,\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}^{-1}\,E_{\ell}\;,\quad T\,\mapsto\,T\;,\quad F_{\ell}\,\mapsto\,F^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}_{\ell}:=\,F_{\ell}\,\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,\ell}^{+1}

[formula]

does indeed provide a well-defined isomorphism of (topological)
superalgebras that is in fact also one of Hopf superalgebras, q.e.d.
∎

As a matter of fact,
the previous result can be somehow reversed
,
as the following shows: in particular, loosely speaking, we end up
finding that
every straight small FoMpQUESA can be realized as a
toral twist deformation of the “standard” Yamane’s QUESA

— cf. claim
(c)
in Theorem
5.1.3
here below.

Theorem 5.1.3
.

With assumptions as above, let
P
and
P^{\prime}
be two matrices
of Cartan type with the same associated Cartan matrix
A\,
,
namely
\,P_{s}=P^{\prime}_{s}\,
.

(a)  Let
{\mathcal{R}}
be a
straight
realization of
P
and let

U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
be the associated FoMpQUESA. Then there is a
straight
realization
\check{{\mathcal{R}}}^{\prime}
of

P^{\prime}
and a matrix

\,\Phi\in\!\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
such that for the
associated twist element
{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}
as in
(
5.1
) we have

U_{\!P^{\prime}\!,\,\hbar}^{\check{{\mathcal{R}}}^{\prime}\!,\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\;\cong\;{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}

In a nutshell, if
\,P^{\prime}_{s}=P_{s}\,
then from any

straight
FoMpQUESA over
P
we can obtain by toral twist deformation a

straight
FoMpQUESA (of the same rank) over
P^{\prime}
.

Conversely, if
\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}
is any
straight

realization of
P^{\prime}
and
U_{\!P^{\prime}\!,\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\!,\,p}(\mathfrak{g})

is the associated FoMpQUESA, then there exists a
straight

realization
\hat{{\mathcal{R}}}
of
P
and a matrix

\,\Phi\in\!\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,

such that for the associated twist element
{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}

as in (
5.1
) we have

U_{\!P^{\prime}\!,\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\!,\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\;\cong\;{\big(\,U_{\!P,\,\hbar}^{\hat{{\mathcal{R}}},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}

(b)  Let
{\mathcal{R}}
and
{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}
be
straight small

realizations of
P
and
P^{\prime}
respectively, with

\,\operatorname{rk}({\mathcal{R}})=\operatorname{rk}({\mathcal{R}}^{\prime})=t\,
, and let
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
and

U_{\!P^{\prime}\!,\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\!,\,p}(\mathfrak{g})
be the associated FoMpQUESAs.
Then there exists a matrix
\,\Phi\in\!\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
such that for

{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}
as in (
5.1
) we have

U_{\!P^{\prime}\!,\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\!,\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\;\cong\;{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}

In short, if
\,P^{\prime}_{s}=P_{s}\,
, any
straight small

FoMpQUESA over
P^{\prime}
is isomorphic to a toral twist deformation
of any
straight small
FoMpQUESA over
P
of same rank.

(c)  Every
straight small
FoMpQUESA is
isomorphic to some toral twist deformation of Yamane’s standard
FoMpQUESA (over
\,DA=P_{s}\,
) of the same rank.

(d)  Similar, parallel statements hold true for the Borel FoMpQUESAs.

Proof.

(a)
By Theorem
5.1.2

it is enough to find
\,\Phi\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}(\Bbbk)\,
such that

\,P^{\prime}=P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\,
,  that is

\;P^{\prime}=P-\mathfrak{A}\,\Phi\,\mathfrak{A}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle T}\,
;
but this is guaranteed by Proposition
2.2.6
(a)
.

(b)
This follows directly from claim
(a)

and Proposition
2.2.6
(b)
.

(c)
This follows from
(b)
, with

U_{\!P^{\prime}\!,\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\!,\,p}(\mathfrak{g})
the given straight small FoMpQUESA and

U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
the standard FoMpQUESA
over
\,P:=DA\,

as in Yamane’s definition.

(d)
This is clear from definitions.
∎

Observation 5.1.4
.

Theorems
5.1.2
and
5.1.3

have the following interpretation. Our FoMpQUESAs
\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,

are quantum objects depending on the multiparameter
P\,
;
but when we perform onto
\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
a deformation by twist as
in §
5.1.1
, the output

\,{U_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar,\Phi}^{{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})}:={\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}\,

seemingly depends on
two
multiparameters, namely
P

and

\Phi\,
. Thus all these

{U_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar,\Phi}^{{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})}
’s form a seemingly

richer
family of “twice-multiparametric” formal QUESAs.
Nevertheless, Theorem
5.1.2
above proves
that this family actually
coincides
with the family of all FoMpQUESAs,
although the latter seems a priori smaller.

In short, Theorems
5.1.2
and
5.1.3

show the following. The dependence of the Hopf structure of

{U_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}},\Phi}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})}
on the
“double parameter”
(P\,,\Phi)
is “split” in the algebraic structure
(ruled by
P
) and in the coalgebraic structure (ruled by
\Phi
);
now Theorems
5.1.2
and
5.1.3

enable us to “polarize” this dependence so to codify it either entirely
within the algebraic structure (while the coalgebraic one is cast into a “canonical form”),
so that the single multiparameter
P_{\Phi}
is enough to describe it,
or entirely within the coalgebraic structure (with the algebraic one being
reduced to the “standard” Yamane’s one), hence one multiparameter alone, say
\Phi_{P}\,
,
is enough.

Remark 5.1.5
.

Since the subclass of
split
realizations is
not closed
under twist
(cf. the end of §
2.2.7
),
the subclass of all “split” FoMpQUESAs is not closed too under twist deformation;
this is a quantum analogue of Observations
3.3.5
(b)
.

5.2.
Deformations of FoMpQUESAs by toral polar–
2
–cocycles

We discuss now suitable deformations of FoMpQUESAs by
polar–
2
–cocycles (of “toral type”), proving that they are again FoMpQUESAs.

5.2.1
.

Deformations of
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
by toral polar–
2
–cocycles.

In §
4.1.5
we introduced the notion of “
2
–cocycle”
and the procedure of deformation by a
2
–cocycle for a given Hopf superalgebra.
In the setup of QUESAs, the construction has an important variation,
leading to the notion of “polar–
2
–cocycle”
for which the procedure of deformation mentioned above still applies.
This theory is developed in
[
GG4
]
for the case of quantized universal enveloping algebras (=QUEAs),
i.e. those QUESAs whose super-structure is trivial (namely, their odd part is zero).
The task of extending the results in
[
GG4
]

— stated and proved for QUEAs: see, in particular, §3.3 therein —
to the setup of QUESAs is harmless, though technical.
However, we do not need that: instead, we will introduce special
“polar–
2
–cocycles” for our FoMpQUESAs (a special class of QUESAs, indeed)
and then we will show by direct check that the procedure of deformation by

2
–cocycles still work if we use instead these
polar
–
2
–cocycles.
Thus we will be applying a general fact, but proving its validity “on the fly”
instead of relying on a general, theoretical proof.

5.2.2
.

Polar–
2
–cocycles of
U_{\!P,\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\,
.

Let
(A,p)
be a Cartan super-datum. Fix again

\,P:={\big(\,p_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
of
Cartan type with associated Cartan matrix
A\,
, a realization

\;{\mathcal{R}}\,:=\,\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\;

of it and the (topological) Hopf algebra
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
,
as in §
2
and §
4.2
.
We consider special 2–cocycles of
\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
,
called “toral” as they are induced from the quantum torus.
To this end, like in §
5.1.1
, we fix in
\mathfrak{h}
a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–basis

\,{\big\{H_{g}\big\}}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\,
,
where
\mathcal{G}
is an index set with
\,|\mathcal{G}|=\operatorname{rk}(\mathfrak{h})=t\,
.

Like in §
2.3.1
,
we fix an antisymmetric,
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
—bilinear map

\,\chi:\mathfrak{h}\times\mathfrak{h}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
,
that corresponds to some

\,X={\big(\,\chi_{g{}\gamma}\big)}_{g,\gamma\in\mathcal{G}}\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,

via
\,\chi_{g{}\gamma}=\chi(H_{g}\,,H_{\gamma})\,
.
We also consider the antisymmetric matrix

\,\mathring{X}:={\Big(\,\mathring{\chi}_{i{}j}=\chi\big(\,T_{i}^{+}\,,T_{j}^{+}\big)\Big)}_{i,j\in I}\!\in\mathfrak{so}_{n}({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]})\,
.
Any such map
\chi
induces uniquely an antisymmetric,
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–bilinear map

\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}\,:\,U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\times U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}

as follows. By definition,
\,U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{h}):=U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})\,
is an
\hbar
–adically
complete topologically free Hopf algebra isomorphic to

\,\widehat{S}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}(\mathfrak{h}):=\widehat{\bigoplus\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}S_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}^{\,n}(\mathfrak{h})}\,

— the
\hbar
–adic completion of the symmetric algebra

\,S_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}(\mathfrak{h})=\bigoplus\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}S_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}^{\,n}(\mathfrak{h})\,
—
hence the following makes sense:

Definition 5.2.3
.

We define
\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}
as the unique
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–linear
(hence
\hbar
–adically continuous) map

\;U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\otimes U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}}}}\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\;

such that (with identifications as above)

\hfil\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(z,1):=\epsilon(z)=:\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(1,z)\;\;,\quad\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(x,y):=\chi(x,y)\quad\forall\;\;z\in\widehat{S}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}(\mathfrak{h})\,,\;x,y\in S_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}^{\,1}(\mathfrak{h})

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill\qquad\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(x,y):=0\quad\qquad\forall\;\;x\in S_{\Bbbk}^{\,r}(\mathfrak{h})\,,\;y\in S_{\Bbbk}^{\,s}(\mathfrak{h})\,:\,r,s\geq 1\,,\;r+s>2\hfill\diamondsuit

By construction,
\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}
is a normalized Hochschild 2–cocycle on
U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\,
,  that is

\epsilon(x)\,\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(y,z)\,-\,\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(xy,z)\,+\,\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(x,yz)\,-\,\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(x,y)\,\epsilon(z)\;=\;0\qquad\forall\;\;x,y,z\in U_{\!P,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}}(\mathfrak{h})

Given two linear maps

\,\eta,\vartheta:U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\otimes U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
,
one defines the
convolution product

\;\eta\ast\vartheta:{U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})}^{\otimes 2}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\;

by
\;(\eta\ast\vartheta)(x\otimes y)\,:=\,\eta(x_{(1)},y_{(1)})\,\vartheta(x_{(2)},y_{(2)})\;

for all
\,x,y\in U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\,

— using the coalgebra structure of

\,{U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})}^{\otimes 2}\cong{\widehat{S}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}(\mathfrak{h})}^{\otimes 2}\cong\widehat{S}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}(\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{h})\,
. Then by
\,\eta^{\ast\,m}\,

we denote the
m
–th power with respect to the convolution
product of any map
\eta
as above; in particular, we set
\,\eta^{\ast\,0}:=\epsilon\otimes\epsilon\,
.

The following result, proved by direct computation, describes the powers of
\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}\,
:

Lemma 5.2.4
.

For all
\,H_{+},H_{-}\in\mathfrak{h}\,
and
\,k,\ell,m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\,
,  we have

\quad{\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}}^{\,\ast\,m}\big(H_{+}^{\,k},H_{-}^{\,\ell}\,\big)\;=\;\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{lr}\delta_{k,m}\,\delta_{\ell,m}\,{\big(m!\big)}^{2}\,\chi(H_{+},H_{-})^{m}&\quad\text{for}\;\;\;\;m\geq 1\;,\hfill\\
\delta_{k,0}\,\delta_{\ell,0}&\quad\text{for}\;\;\;\;m=0\;.\quad\hskip 7.0pt\qquad\;\hfill\square\end{array}\end{cases}

Definition 5.2.5
.

Keep notation as above. We define
\,\chi_{\scriptscriptstyle U}\,
as the unique map from
\,U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\!\mathop{\otimes}\limits_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\!U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{h})\,
to
{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}

given by the exponentiation of
\,\hbar^{-1}\,2^{-1}\,\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}\,
, i.e.

\chi_{\scriptscriptstyle U}\;:=\;e^{\hbar^{-1}2^{-1}\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}}\;=\;{\textstyle\sum_{m\geq 0}}\,\hbar^{-m}\,{\tilde{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}}^{\,\ast\,m}\!\Big/2^{m}\,m!

[formula]

Lemma 5.2.6
.

The map
\,\chi_{\scriptscriptstyle U}
is a well-defined, normalized,

{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}
–valued Hopf 2–cocycle for
\,U_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{h}):=U_{\!P,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}}(\mathfrak{h})\,
, such that, for all

H_{+}\,,H_{-}\in\mathfrak{h}\,
and
\,K_{\pm}:=e^{\,\hbar\,H_{\pm}}\,
,

\chi_{\scriptscriptstyle U}^{\pm 1}(H_{+},H_{-})\,=\,\pm\hbar^{-1}\,2^{-1}\chi(H_{+},H_{-})\quad,\qquad\chi_{\scriptscriptstyle U}(K_{+},K_{-})\,=\,e^{\hbar\,2^{-1}\chi(H_{+},H_{-})}

Proof.

This is just Lemma 5.2.5 in
[
GG3
]
.
∎

5.2.7
.

Toral polar–
2
–cocycles of
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
.

The previous construction provides, starting from
\chi\,
, a normalized Hopf
2
–cocycle

\;\chi_{\scriptscriptstyle U}:U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})\times U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}\;
.

We assume now that the map
\,\chi
satisfies the additional requirement (
2.3
), in other words we require that
\,\chi\in\text{\sl Alt}_{\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}}^{\,S}(\mathfrak{h})\,
—

notation of §
2.3
. The latter map canonically induces a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–bilinear map

\;\overline{\chi}:\overline{\mathfrak{h}}\times\overline{\mathfrak{h}}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\;
,  where
\,\overline{\mathfrak{h}}:=\mathfrak{h}\big/\mathfrak{s}\,
with

\,\mathfrak{s}:=\textsl{Span}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big({\{\,S_{i}\,\}}_{i\in I}\big)\,
,  given by

\overline{\chi}\,\big(\,T^{\prime}\!+\mathfrak{s}\,,T^{\prime\prime}\!+\mathfrak{s}\,\big)\,:=\,\chi\big(\,T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime}\big)\qquad\forall\;\;T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime}\in\mathfrak{h}

Now, replaying the construction above but with
\overline{\mathfrak{h}}
and

\overline{\chi}
replacing
\mathfrak{h}
and
\chi\,
,
we construct a normalized Hopf
2
–cocycle

\;\overline{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}\!:U_{\!P,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}},\,p}\big(\,\overline{\mathfrak{h}}\,\big)\times U_{\!P,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}},\,p}\big(\,\overline{\mathfrak{h}}\,\big)\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}\;
;
for the latter, the analogue of Lemma
5.2.6
holds true again. Moreover,
note that
\,U_{\!P,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}},\,p}\big(\,\overline{\mathfrak{h}}\,\big)\cong\widehat{S}_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big(\,\overline{\mathfrak{h}}\,\big)\,
,  and,
thanks to (
2.4
),
there exists a Hopf superalgebra epimorphism

\;\pi:U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow U_{\!P,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}},\,p}\big(\,\overline{\mathfrak{h}}\,\big)\;

given by
\,\pi(E_{i}):=0\,
,
\,\pi(F_{i}):=0\,
— for
\,i\in I\,
— and

\,\pi(T):=(T+\mathfrak{s})\,\in\,\overline{\mathfrak{h}}\subseteq U_{\!P,\hbar}^{\mathcal{R}}\big(\,\overline{\mathfrak{h}}\,\big)\,

— for
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
.
Then we consider

\sigma_{\chi}\,:=\,\overline{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}\circ(\pi\times\pi)\,:\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\times U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}

which is
automatically
a normalized,
{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}
–valued Hopf
2
–cocycle on
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
.

Definition 5.2.8
.

We call “
polar–2–cocycles
of
toral
type”, or “
toral

polar–
2
–cocycles
”, of
\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
all normalized Hopf 2–cocycles
\,\sigma_{\chi}
obtained
— from all
\,\chi\in\text{\sl Alt}^{\,S}_{\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}}(\mathfrak{h})\,
— via the above construction. We denote by
\,{\mathcal{Z}}_{\,2}\big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)\,
the set of all of them — which is actually independent of the multiparameter
P
, indeed.

N.B.:
in fact,
the
\sigma_{\chi}
’s above are “polar–
2
–cocycles of
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
” in the sense of the definition given in
[
GG4
]
, once the latter is adapted to QUESAs.
\diamondsuit

5.2.9
.

Formulas for the
\sigma_{\chi}
–deformed product.

Let
\,\sigma_{\chi}\in{\mathcal{Z}}_{\,2}\big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)\,
be a toral
2
–cocycle as above.
Following §
4.1.5
, using
\sigma_{\chi}
we introduce in
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
a “deformed product”,
hereafter denoted by
\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}\,
;  then

\,X^{(n)_{\sigma_{\chi}}}=X\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}\cdots\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}X\,

will denote the
n
–th power of any
\,X\in U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
with respect to this deformed product.

Directly from definitions, sheer computation yields the following formulas,
relating the deformed product with the old one (for all
\;T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime},T\in\mathfrak{h}\;
,
\;i\,,\,j\in I\,
,
\,m,n\in\mathbb{N}\,
):

\hfil\displaystyle T^{\prime}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}T^{\prime\prime}\,=\;T^{\prime}\,T^{\prime\prime}\;\;\;,\;\quad T^{(n)_{\sigma_{\chi}}}=T^{n}\;\;\;,\;\quad E_{i}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}F_{j}\;=\,E_{i}\,F_{j}\;\;\;,\;\quad F_{j}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{i}\;=\,F_{j}\,E_{i}

\hfil\displaystyle T\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{j}\;=\;T\,E_{j}\,+\,2^{-1}\chi\big(T,T_{j}^{+}\big)\,E_{j}\;\;\;,\qquad E_{j}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}T\;=\;E_{j}\,T\,+\,2^{-1}\chi\big(T_{j}^{+},T\big)\,E_{j}

\hfil\displaystyle T\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}F_{j}\;=\;T\,F_{j}\,+\,2^{-1}\chi\big(T,T_{j}^{-}\big)\,F_{j}\;\;\;,\qquad F_{j}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}T\;=\;F_{j}\,T\,+\,2^{-1}\chi\big(T_{j}^{-},T\big)\,F_{j}

\hfil\displaystyle E_{i_{1}}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{i_{2}}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}\cdots\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{i_{t-1}}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{i_{t}}\;=\;e^{\,+\hbar\,2^{-1}\sum_{c<e}\mathring{\chi}_{\!\raise-2.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle c\,e$}}}\,E_{i_{1}}\,E_{i_{2}}\cdots E_{i_{t-1}}\,E_{i_{t}}

\hfil\displaystyle E_{i}^{\,(m)_{\sigma_{\chi}}}\,=\;{\textstyle\prod_{\ell=1}^{m-1}}\sigma_{\chi}\Big(e^{\,+\hbar\,\ell\,T_{i}^{+}}\!,\,e^{\,+\hbar\,T_{i}^{+}}\Big)\,E_{i}^{\,m}\;=\;E_{i}^{\,m}

\hfil\displaystyle E_{i}^{\,m}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{j}^{\,n}\;=\;\sigma_{\chi}\Big(e^{\,+\hbar\,m\,T_{i}^{+}}\!,\,e^{\,+\hbar\,n\,T_{j}^{+}}\Big)\,E_{i}^{\,m}\,E_{j}^{\,n}\;=\;e^{\,+\hbar\,m\,n\,2^{-1}\mathring{\chi}_{ij}}E_{i}^{\,m}\,E_{j}^{\,n}

\hfil\displaystyle E_{i}^{\,(m)_{\sigma_{\chi}}}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}\,E_{j}\,\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}\,E_{k}^{\,(n)_{\sigma_{\chi}}}\,=\;{\textstyle\prod_{\ell=1}^{m-1}}\sigma_{\chi}\Big(e^{\,+\hbar\,\ell\,T_{i}^{+}}\!,\,e^{\,+\hbar\,T_{i}^{+}}\Big)\cdot{\textstyle\prod_{t=1}^{n-1}}\sigma_{\chi}\Big(e^{\,+\hbar\,t\,T_{k}^{+}}\!,\,e^{\,+\hbar\,T_{k}^{+}}\Big)\,\cdot\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill\cdot\;\sigma_{\chi}\Big(e^{\,+\hbar\,m\,T_{i}^{+}}\!,\,e^{\,+\hbar\,T_{j}^{+}}\Big)\,\sigma_{\chi}\Big(e^{\,+\hbar\,(m\,T_{i}^{+}+T_{j}^{+})}\!,\,e^{\,+\hbar\,n\,T_{k}^{+}}\Big)\,E_{i}^{\,m}\,E_{j}\,E_{k}^{\,n}

\hfil\displaystyle F_{i_{1}}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}F_{i_{2}}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}\cdots\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}F_{i_{t-1}}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}F_{i_{t}}\;=\;e^{\,-\hbar\,2^{-1}\sum_{c<e}\mathring{\chi}_{\!\raise-2.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle c\,e$}}}\,F_{i_{1}}\,F_{i_{2}}\cdots F_{i_{t-1}}\,F_{i_{t}}

\hfil\displaystyle F_{i}^{\,(m)_{\sigma_{\chi}}}\,=\;{\textstyle\prod_{\ell=1}^{m-1}}\sigma_{\chi}^{-1}\Big(e^{\,-\hbar\,\ell\,T_{i}^{-}}\!,\,e^{\,-\hbar\,T_{i}^{-}}\Big)\,F_{i}^{\,m}\;=\;F_{i}^{\,m}

\hfil\displaystyle F_{i}^{\,m}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}F_{j}^{\,n}\;=\;\sigma_{\chi}^{-1}\Big(e^{\,-\hbar\,m\,T_{i}^{-}}\!,\,e^{\,-\hbar\,n\,T_{j}^{-}}\Big)\,F_{i}^{\,m}\,F_{j}^{\,n}\;=\;e^{\,-\hbar\,m\,n\,2^{-1}\mathring{\chi}_{ij}}F_{i}^{\,m}\,F_{j}^{\,n}

\hfil\displaystyle F_{i}^{\,(m)_{\sigma_{\chi}}}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}\,F_{j}\,\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}\,F_{k}^{\,(n)_{\sigma_{\chi}}}\,=\;{\textstyle\prod_{\ell=1}^{m-1}}\sigma_{\chi}^{-1}\Big(e^{\,-\hbar\,\ell\,T_{i}^{-}}\!,\,e^{\,-\hbar\,T_{i}^{-}}\Big)\cdot{\textstyle\prod_{t=1}^{n-1}}\sigma_{\chi}^{-1}\Big(e^{\,-\hbar\,t\,T_{k}^{-}}\!,\,e^{\,-\hbar\,T_{k}^{-}}\Big)\,\cdot\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill\cdot\;\sigma_{\chi}^{-1}\Big(e^{\,-\hbar\,m\,T_{i}^{-}}\!,\,e^{\,-\hbar\,T_{j}^{-}}\Big)\,\sigma_{\chi}^{-1}\Big(e^{\,-\hbar\,(m\,T_{i}^{-}+T_{j}^{-})}\!,\,e^{\,-\hbar\,n\,T_{k}^{-}}\Big)\,F_{i}^{\,m}\,F_{j}\,F_{k}^{\,n}

Note also that from the identities
\;T^{(n)_{\sigma_{\chi}}}=T^{n}\;
(for
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
and
\,n\in\mathbb{N}\,
) it follows that the exponential of any toral element with respect to the deformed product
\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}
is the same as with respect to the old one.

Observation 5.2.10
.

The whole procedure of
2
–cocycle deformation by
\sigma_{\chi}
should apply to the scalar extension
\,{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}\otimes_{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
, as
a priori

\sigma_{\chi}
takes values in
{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}
rather than in
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
. Nevertheless, the formulas above show that

U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
is actually
closed
for the deformed product “
\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}
” provided by this procedure, hence the deformation does “restrict” to
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
itself, so that we eventually end up with a well-defined
2
–cocycle deformation
{\big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\!\sigma_{\chi}}\,
.

A first, direct consequence of these formulas is the following:

Proposition 5.2.11
.

The deformed algebra
{\big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}\!
is still generated by the elements
E_{i}\,
,
F_{i}
and
T
(with
\,i\in I\,
and
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
) of
\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
.

5.2.12
.

Deformations of
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
by toral polar–
2
–cocycle.

Our key result concerns polar–
2
–cocycle deformations by means of
toral polar–
2
–cocycles. In order to state it,
we need some more notation, which we now settle.

Let
(A,p)
be a Cartan super-datum,

\,P:={\big(\,p_{ij}\,\big)}_{i,j\in I}\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,

a multiparameter matrix of Cartan type with associated Cartan matrix
A

— cf. Definition
2.1.3
—
and fix a realization
{\mathcal{R}}=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\big)\,
of
P
.
Fix an antisymmetric
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–bilinear map

\,\chi\!:\mathfrak{h}\times\mathfrak{h}\!\longrightarrow\!{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,

enjoying (
2.3
) — that is,

\,\chi\in\text{\sl Alt}_{\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}}^{\,S}(\mathfrak{h})\,
;
then we associate with it the matrix

\,\mathring{X}:=\!{\Big(\mathring{\chi}_{i{}j}=\chi\big(\,T_{i}^{+},T_{j}^{+}\big)\!\Big)}_{i,j\in I}\,

as above. Note that

\;+\chi\big(\,\text{--}\,,T_{j}^{+}\big)\,=\,-\chi\big(\,\text{--}\,,T_{j}^{-}\big)\;

for all
\,j\in I\,
, as direct consequence of (
2.3
).
Basing on the above, like in §
2.3
we define

P_{(\chi)}:=\,P\,+\,\mathring{X}\,=\,{\Big(\,p^{(\chi)}_{i{}j}:=\,p_{ij}+\mathring{\chi}_{i{}j}\Big)}_{\!i,j\in I}\;\,,\!\quad\Pi_{(\chi)}:=\,{\Big\{\,\alpha_{i}^{(\chi)}:=\,\alpha_{i}\pm\chi\big(\,\text{--}\,,T_{i}^{\pm}\big)\Big\}}_{i\in I}

Then, still from §
2.3
we know that

P_{(\chi)}
is a matrix of Cartan type — the same of
P

indeed — and

\,{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)}\,=\,\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi_{(\chi)}\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,

is a realization of it.

We are now ready for our result on deformations by toral
polar–
2
–cocycle.

Theorem 5.2.13
.

There exists an isomorphism of topological Hopf superalgebras

{\big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}\;\cong\;\,U_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})

which is the identity on generators. In short, every toral polar–2–cocycle deformation of a
FoMpQUESA is another FoMpQUESA, whose multiparameter
\,P_{(\chi)}
and realization
{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)}

depend on the original
P
and
{\mathcal{R}}\,
, as well as on
\chi\,
, as explained in §
2.3
.

Similar statements hold true for the Borel FoMpQUESAs and their deformations by
\sigma_{\chi}\,
, namely there exist isomorphisms
\,\;{\big(U_{\!P,\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\mathfrak{b}_{\pm})\big)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}\cong\;U_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},\,p}(\mathfrak{b}_{\pm})\;\,
.

Proof.

We begin by noting the following key

\underline{\text{Fact}}
: The generators of
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
,
when thought of as elements of the deformed algebra
{\big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}\,
,
obey the defining relations of the (same name) generators of

\,U_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\,
— with respect to the deformed product

\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}
.

Let us assume for a moment the above
\underline{\text{\sl Fact\/}}
does hold true. Then it implies that there exists a well-defined homomorphism of topological Hopf superalgebras, say

\;\ell:U_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}\;

given on generators by
\;\ell(E_{i}):=E_{i}\,
,
\;\ell(F_{i}):=F_{i}\,
,
\;\ell\big(T):=T\;
(
\,i\in I\,
,
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
) — in short, it is the identity on generators. Moreover, thanks to Proposition
5.2.11
this is in fact an
epi
morphism. As an application of this result, there exists also an epimorphism of topological Hopf superalgebras

\;\ell^{\prime}:U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\big(\,U_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{-\chi}}\;

which again is the identity on generators — just replace
\chi
with
