-\chi\,
and
P
with
P_{(\chi)}\,
.

Mimicking what we did for
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
,  we can construct, out of
\chi\,
, a normalized Hopf
2
–cocycle
{\dot{\sigma}}_{\chi}
for
{\big(\,U_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{-\chi}}\,
;  then we also have a similar
2
–cocycle
\sigma^{\prime}_{\chi}
on
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
defined as the pull-back of
{\dot{\sigma}}_{\chi}
via
\ell^{\prime}\,
,  and a unique, induced Hopf algebra homomorphism

\;\ell^{\prime}_{{\dot{\sigma}}_{\chi}}:{\big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{\chi}}\!\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Big({\Big(\,U_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\Big)}_{\sigma_{-\chi}}\Big)}_{{\dot{\sigma}}_{\chi}}\;

between deformed Hopf algebras, which is once more the identity on generators. Now, tracking the whole construction we see that
\,\sigma^{\prime}_{\chi}=\sigma_{\chi}\,
, so that

\;{\big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{\chi}}={\big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}\;
,  and

\;{\Big({\Big(\,U_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\Big)}_{\sigma_{-\chi}}\Big)}_{{\dot{\sigma}}_{\chi}}\!\!=\,U_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\;
. Then composition gives homomorphisms

\hfil\displaystyle\ell^{\prime}_{{\dot{\sigma}}_{\chi}}\!\circ\ell\,:\,U_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow U_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})

\hfil\displaystyle\ell\circ\ell^{\prime}_{{\dot{\sigma}}_{\chi}}:\,{\big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow U_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}^{{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}

which (both) are the identity on generators: hence in the end we get

\;\ell^{\prime}_{{\dot{\sigma}}_{\chi}}\!\!\circ\ell\,=\,\textsl{id}\;

and
\;\ell\circ\ell^{\prime}_{{\dot{\sigma}}_{\chi}}\!=\,\textsl{id}\;
,  thus in particular

\,\ell\,
is an isomorphism,  q.e.d.

We are now left with the task of proving the
\underline{\text{\sl Fact\/}}
above, i.e. a bunch of relations.

Indeed, most relations follow at once from the formulas in §
5.2.9
.
Namely, the identities
\;T^{\prime}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}T^{\prime\prime}=T^{\prime}\,T^{\prime\prime}\,
imply

\;T^{\prime}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}T^{\prime\prime}=T^{\prime\prime}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}T^{\prime}\,

— for all
\,T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime}\in\mathfrak{h}\,
. Also, from

\,\;T\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{j}\,=\,T\,E_{j}\,+\,2^{-1}\chi\big(T,T_{j}^{+}\big)\,E_{j}\;\,

and

\,\;E_{j}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}T\,=\,E_{j}\,T\,+\,2^{-1}\chi\big(T_{j}^{+},T\big)\,E_{j}\;\,

— for all
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
and
\,j\in I\,
— we get (since
\chi
is antisymmetric)

T\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{j}\,-\,E_{j}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}T\,\;=\;\Big(\,\alpha_{j}(T)+2^{-1}\big(\,\chi-\chi^{\,\scriptscriptstyle T}\big)\big(\,T\,,T_{j}^{+}\big)\!\Big)\,E_{j}\,\;=\;+\alpha^{(\chi)}_{j}(T)\,E_{j}

A similar, straightforward analysis also yields

\;T\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}F_{j}\,-\,F_{j}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}T\,=\,-\alpha^{(\chi)}_{j}(T)\,F_{j}\;
.

The identities
\;E_{i}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}F_{j}\,=\,E_{i}\,F_{j}\;
and
\;F_{j}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{i}\,=\,F_{j}\,E_{i}\;
in turn imply

E_{i}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}F_{j}\,-\,{(-1)}^{p(i)\,p(j)}F_{j}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{i}\;=\;E_{i}\,F_{j}\,-\,{(-1)}^{p(i)\,p(j)}F_{j}\,E_{i}\;=\;{{\;\delta_{i,j}\big(e^{+\hbar\,T_{i}^{+}}-\,e^{-\hbar\,T_{i}^{-}}\big)\;}\over{\;\;e^{+\hbar\,p_{i{}i}/2}\,-\,e^{-\hbar\,p_{i{}i}/2}\;\;}}

Eventually, since
\,p_{i{}i}=p_{i{}i}^{(\chi)}\,
by definition,
and the exponential of toral elements with respect to
\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}

is the same as with respect to the old product, we conclude that

E_{i}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}F_{j}\,-\,{(-1)}^{p(i)\,p(j)}\,F_{j}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{i}\,\;=\;\,\delta_{i,j}\,{{\;e_{\sigma_{\chi}}^{+\hbar\,T_{i}^{+}}\,-\,e_{\sigma_{\chi}}^{-\hbar\,T_{i}^{-}}\;}\over{\;e^{+\hbar\,p_{i{}i}^{(\chi)}/2}\,-\,e^{-\hbar\,p_{i{}i}^{(\chi)}/2}\;}}

where
\,e_{\sigma_{\chi}}^{\,X}\,
denotes the exponential of any
X
with respect to
\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}
--- which we already noticed that is the same as the exponential with respect to the original product.

What is less trivial is proving the other relations (for the deformed product); we do this only for the relations involving the
E_{i}
’s, those for the
F_{i}
’s being similar.

We begin with “quantum Serre relations”, i.e. those of the form

\sum\limits_{s=0}^{1-a_{ij}}\,{(-1)}^{s}{\bigg[{{1-a_{ij}}\atop s}\bigg]}_{\!q_{i}}{\big(k_{ij}^{(\chi)}\big)}^{\!s}\,E_{i}^{{(1-a_{ij}-s)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{j}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{i}^{{(s)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}}\;=\;0

for all
\,i\neq j\,
in
I
, with
\,p(i)={\bar{0}}\,
:  hereafter we set

\;q_{ij}^{(\chi)}\!:=e^{\hbar\,p_{ij}^{(\chi)}}\!=e^{\hbar\,(p_{ij}+\mathring{\chi}_{ij})}\;

and

\;k_{ij}^{(\chi)}:={\big(q_{ij}^{(\chi)}\big)}^{\!+1/2}{\big(q_{ji}^{(\chi)}\big)}^{\!-1/2}\;
(
\,i,j\in I\,
)
noting that
\,q_{ii}^{(\chi)}=q_{ii}\,
and
\,q_{i}^{(\chi)}=e^{+\hbar\,p_{i{}i}^{(\chi)}/2}=q_{i}\,
(
\,i\in I\,
), since
\,\big(P_{(\chi)}\big)_{s}=P_{s}=DA\,
; moreover, we set also
\,\kappa_{ij}:=e^{\hbar\,\mathring{\chi}_{ij}}\,
, noting then that
\,q_{ij}^{(\chi)}=q_{ij}\,\kappa_{ij}\,
(
\,i,j\in I\,
).

To prove the above identity, we analyze all factors in the summands separately.

\underline{\text{\sl Claim 1}}\,
: For
\,i\neq j\in I\,
we have

\;k_{ij}^{(\chi)}=\,k_{ij}\,\kappa_{ij}\,
,
hence
\;{\big(k_{ij}^{(\chi)}\big)}^{\!s}\!=k_{ij}^{\,s}\,\kappa_{ij}^{\,s}\;
for
\,s\in\mathbb{N}\,
.

This follows by direct computation. Next claim instead follows from §
5.2.9
:

\underline{\text{\sl Claim 2}}\,
: Fix
\,i\neq j\,
in
I
,
\,p(i)={\bar{0}}\,
, and write
\,m:=1-a_{ij}\,
. Then

E_{i}^{(m-s)_{\sigma_{\chi}}}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{j}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{i}^{(s)_{\sigma_{\chi}}}\;=\;\sigma_{\chi}\big(K_{i}^{m-s},K_{j}\big)\,\sigma_{\chi}\big(K_{i}^{m-s}K_{j}\,,K_{i}^{s}\big)\,E_{i}^{m-s}E_{j}\,E_{i}^{s}

Now we evaluate the value of the toral
2
–cocycle using the exponentials.

\underline{\text{\sl Claim 3}}\,
: For all
\,i,j\in I\,
and
\,m,s,\ell\in\mathbb{N}\,
,  we have

(a)

\;\;\sigma_{\chi}\big(K_{i}^{\ell},K_{j}\big)\;=\;\kappa_{ij}^{\,\ell/2}

(b)

\;\;\sigma_{\chi}\big(K_{i}^{m-s}K_{j}\,,K_{i}^{s}\big)\;=\;\kappa_{ij}^{\,s/2}

(c)

\;\;\sigma_{\chi}\big(K_{i}^{m-s},K_{j}\big)\,\sigma_{\chi}\big(K_{i}^{m-s}K_{j}\,,K_{i}^{s}\big)\;=\;\kappa_{ij}^{\,(m-2s)/2}

All assertions follow by computation using Lemma
5.2.6
. Indeed, for
(a)
we have

\sigma_{\chi}\big(K_{i}^{\ell},K_{j}\big)\;=\;\overline{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}\big(K_{i}^{\ell},K_{j}\big)\;=\;e^{\hbar\,2^{-1}\chi(\ell\,T_{i}^{+},T_{j}^{+})}\;=\;\kappa_{ij}^{\,\ell/2}

For item
(b)
,  Lemma
5.2.6
yields

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\sigma_{\chi}\big(K_{i}^{m-s}K_{j}\,,K_{i}^{s}\big)\;=\;\overline{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}\big(K_{i}^{m-s}K_{j}\,,K_{i}^{s}\big)\;=\;\overline{\chi}_{\scriptscriptstyle U}\big(\,e^{\hbar\,((m-s)\,T_{i}^{+}+T_{j}^{+})},e^{\hbar\,s\,T_{i}^{+}}\,\big)\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\hfill=\;e^{\hbar\,2^{-1}\chi((m-s)\,T_{i}^{+}+T_{j}^{+},\,s\,T_{i}^{+})}\;=\;\kappa_{ij}^{\,s/2}\quad

Now, putting altogether
(a)
and
(b)
we eventually get
(c)
.

Finally,
Claims 1
,
2
and
3
altogether yield, for
\,m:=1-a_{ij}\,
,

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\sum\limits_{s=0}^{m}{(-1)}^{s}{\bigg[{m\atop s}\bigg]}_{\!q_{i}}{\big(k_{ij}^{(\chi)}\big)}^{\!s}\,E_{i}^{{(m-s)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{j}\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}E_{i}^{{(s)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}}\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad=\;\sum\limits_{s=0}^{m}{(-1)}^{k}{\bigg[{m\atop s}\bigg]}_{\!q_{i}}k_{ij}^{\,s}\,\kappa_{ij}^{\,s}\,\kappa_{ij}^{\,(m-2s)/2}\,E_{i}^{m-s}E_{j}\,E_{i}^{s}\;=\hfill

\hfil\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=\;\kappa_{ij}^{\,m/2}\,\sum\limits_{s=0}^{m}{(-1)}^{s}{\bigg[{m\atop s}\bigg]}_{\!q_{i}}k_{ij}^{\,s}\,E_{i}^{m-s}E_{j}\,E_{i}^{s}\;=\;0\hfill

where the last equality comes from the quantum Serre relation w.r.t. the old product.

Finally, we have to check relations (
4.7
) through (
4.12
). To this end, like for the proof of Proposition
4.2.8
– where one also had to check relations, in a very similar manner — we observe that the relations under scrutiny involve iterated
q
–supercommutators, so the key step is to check how a single
q
–supercommutator change under polar–
2
–cocycle deformation. In this respect,
we fix notation
\,{[X\,,Y]}_{\mu}^{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}\,
to denote the
\mu
—supercommutator between
X
and
Y
with respect to the deformed product “
\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}
’’
. Then direct computation, using formulas in §
5.2.9
, gives (for all
\,i,j,k\in\,I
and for all
\,\nu,\mu\in{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,
)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]}_{\nu}\;=\;E_{i}\,E_{j}-\nu\,{(-1)}^{p(i)p(j)}E_{j}\,E_{i}\;=\hfill

\displaystyle=\;\kappa_{ji}^{+1/2}\,E_{i}{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}E_{j}-\nu\,{(-1)}^{p(i)p(j)}\kappa_{ij}^{+1/2}\,E_{j}{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}E_{i}\;=\hfill

\displaystyle=\;\kappa_{ij}^{-1/2}\,E_{i}{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}E_{j}-\nu\,{(-1)}^{p(i)p(j)}\kappa_{ij}^{+1/2}\,E_{j}{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}E_{i}\;=\hfill

\displaystyle=\;\kappa_{ij}^{-1/2}\,\big(E_{i}{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}E_{j}-\nu\,{(-1)}^{p(i)p(j)}\kappa_{ij}\,E_{j}{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}E_{i}\big)\;=\;\kappa_{ij}^{-1/2}\,{[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]}^{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}_{\nu\,\kappa_{ij}}

thus in short

{[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]}_{\nu}\;=\;\kappa_{ij}^{-1/2}\,{[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]}^{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}_{\nu\,\kappa_{ij}}

(5.2)

Similarly, iterated
q
–supercommutators in
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
can be rewritten as suitable multiples of similar, iterated
q
–supercommutators in
{\Big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\!\Big)}_{\!\!{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}}
, giving rise to “higher order analogues” of (
5.2
). Therefore, all relations from (
4.7
) to (
4.12
) for
{\Big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\!\Big)}_{\!\!{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}}
are deduced from the equivalent relations for
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
via repeated applications of (
5.2
) or of its higher order generalizations; indeed, the same applies to the quantum Serre relations as well, that we already disposed of independently.

For instance, the first of these higher order analogues comes from

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\big[[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]_{\nu}\,,E_{k}\big]}_{\mu}\;=\;\big(E_{i}\,E_{j}-\nu\,{(-1)}^{p(i)p(j)}E_{j}\,E_{i}\big)\,E_{k}\,-

(5.3)

\displaystyle-{(-1)}^{(p(i)+p(j))\,p(k)}\,\mu\,E_{k}\,\big(E_{i}\,E_{j}-\nu\,{(-1)}^{p(i)p(j)}E_{j}\,E_{i}\big)\;=

\displaystyle=\,\Big(\kappa_{ji}^{+1/2}\kappa_{kj}^{+1/2}\kappa_{ki}^{+1/2}E_{i}{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}E_{j}-\nu\,{(-1)}^{p(i)p(j)}\kappa_{ij}^{+1/2}\kappa_{kj}^{+1/2}\kappa_{ki}^{+1/2}E_{j}{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}E_{i}\Big){\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}E_{k}\,-

\displaystyle-{(-1)}^{(p(i)+p(j))\,p(k)}\mu\,E_{k}{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}\Big(\kappa_{ik}^{+1/2}\kappa_{jk}^{+1/2}\kappa_{ji}^{+1/2}E_{i}{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}E_{j}\,-

\displaystyle-\nu\,{(-1)}^{p(i)p(j)}\kappa_{jk}^{+1/2}\kappa_{ik}^{+1/2}\kappa_{ij}^{+1/2}E_{j}{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}E_{i}\Big)\;=

\displaystyle=\;\kappa_{ji}^{+1/2}\kappa_{kj}^{+1/2}\kappa_{ki}^{+1/2}\,{\big[{[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]}_{\nu\,\kappa_{ij}}^{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}},E_{k}\big]}_{\mu\,\kappa_{jk}\,\kappa_{ik}}^{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}

which eventually yields the “third order” identity

{\big[[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]_{\nu}\,,E_{k}\big]}_{\mu}\;=\;\kappa_{ji}^{+1/2}\kappa_{kj}^{+1/2}\kappa_{ki}^{+1/2}\,{\big[{[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]}_{\nu\,\kappa_{ij}}^{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}},E_{k}\big]}_{\mu\,\kappa_{jk}\,\kappa_{ik}}^{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}

(5.4)

To see how these apply, the easiest case is relation (
4.7
) for
{\Big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\!\Big)}_{\!\!{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}}
, which is deduced from (
4.7
) for
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
applying (
5.2
) as follows

0\;=\;{[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]}_{k_{ij}}\,=\;\kappa_{ij}^{-1/2}\,{[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]}^{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}_{k_{ij}\,\kappa_{ij}}\,\;\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\;\,{[E_{i}\,,E_{j}]}^{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}_{k_{ij}^{(\chi)}}\,=\;0

where we noticed that
\;k_{ij}^{(\chi)}:={\big(q_{ij}^{(\chi)}\big)}^{\!+1/2}{\big(q_{ji}^{(\chi)}\big)}^{\!-1/2}=k_{ij}\,\kappa_{ij}\;
(cf. Definition
4.2.1
).

Another, less trivial example is that of (
4.10
) for
{\Big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\!\Big)}_{\!\!{\raise-1.0pt\hbox{$\,\scriptstyle\dot{\sigma}_{\chi}$}}}
: we deduce it from the parallel relation (
4.10
) for
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
by direct application of (
5.4
) in which we fix
\,(i\,,j\,,k):=(N-2\,,N-1\,,N)\,
,
\,(\nu\,,\mu):=(\nu_{N-1}\,,\nu_{N})\,
in the first addendum, and
\,(i,j,k):=(N-2\,,N\,,N-1)\,
,
\,(\nu\,,\mu):=(\nu_{N-1}\,,\nu_{N})\,
in the second addendum.

All other cases are entirely similar, so we leave them to the reader.
∎

Remark 5.2.14
.

With notation of Theorem
5.2.13
above, we have

\;P_{(\chi)}-P\,=\,\varLambda\;

for some
antisymmetric
matrix
\,\varLambda\in\mathfrak{so}_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
.
Conversely, under mild assumptions on
P
,
this result can be “reversed” as it is shown below.

Theorem 5.2.15
.

Let
\,P,P^{\prime}\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
be two matrices of Cartan type with the same associated Cartan matrix
\,A\,
.

(a)  Let
\,{\mathcal{R}}
be a
split
realization of
\,P
and
\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
be the associated FoMpQUESA. Then there exists a
split
realization
\check{{\mathcal{R}}}^{\prime}
of
\,P^{\prime}
, a matrix
\,\mathring{X}={\big(\,\mathring{\chi}_{i{}j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\in\mathfrak{so}_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
and a toral polar–
2
–cocycle
\,\sigma_{\chi}
such that

U_{\!P^{\prime}\!,\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\,\;\cong\;{\big(\,U_{\!P,\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}

In short, if
\,P^{\prime}_{s}=P_{s}\,
then from any
split
FoMpQUESA over
P

we can obtain a split FoMpQUESA (of the same rank) over
P^{\prime}
by a toral polar–2–cocycle deformation.

(b)  Let
\,{\mathcal{R}}
be a
split minimal
realization of
P
.
Then the FoMpQUESA
\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
is isomorphic to a toral polar–2–cocycle deformation of Yamane’s standard double QUESA, that is
there exists some bilinear map
\;\chi\in\text{\sl Alt}_{\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}}^{\,S}(\mathfrak{h})\;
such that

U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,\;\cong\;{\big(\,U_{P_{A},\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}}_{st},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}

where
\,U_{P_{A},\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}}_{st},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\,
is the “quantum double” Yamane’s QUESA of Example
4.2.6
(b).

(c)  Similar, parallel statements hold true for the Borel FoMpQUESAs.

Proof.

(a)
By Proposition
2.3.5
(a)
, there exists

\;\chi\in\text{\sl Alt}^{S}_{\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}}(\mathfrak{h})\;
such that

\,P^{\prime}=P_{(\chi)}\,
and the triple — constructed as in §
2.3
—

\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}\,:=\,{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)}\,=\,\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi_{(\chi)}\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,

is a split realization of
\,P^{\prime}=P_{(\chi)}\,
. Then
\;U_{\!P^{\prime}\!,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\,\cong\,{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}\;

by Theorem
5.2.13
.

(b)
Yamane’s
U_{P_{A},\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}}_{st},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})
is, in our language, nothing but the FoMpQUESA built upon a straight split realization
\;{\mathcal{R}}_{st}\,=\,\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi_{st}\,,\Pi_{st}^{\vee}\,\big)\;
of
DA\,
,  for which we write
\,\Pi_{st}^{\vee}={\big\{\,T_{i}^{\pm}\,\big\}}_{i\in I}\,
and
\,\Pi_{st}={\big\{\,\alpha_{i}^{(st)}\,\big\}}_{i\in I}\,
. From Proposition
2.3.5
(b)
we have a suitable
\,\chi\in\text{\sl Alt}_{\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}}^{\,S}(\mathfrak{h})\;
such that the realization
\big({\mathcal{R}}_{st}\big)_{(\chi)}
obtained as deformation of
{\mathcal{R}}_{st}
via
\chi
coincides with
{\mathcal{R}}\,
;  then Theorem
5.2.13
gives
\;U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,\cong\,{\big(\,U_{P_{A},\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}}_{st},\,p}(\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}\,
.
∎

5.2.16
.

Generalizations.
The whole analysis and construction devised in this section can be also applied,
verbatim
, to the setup of QUESAs over
affine
Lie superalgebras, namely those introduced in
[
Ya2
]
and
[
Ya3
]
. Much like in the semiclassical case (cf. §
3.4.5
) one has to handle many more defining relations, keep track of their “‘mutations” under deformation, etc.: nevertheless, all this is a standard task, while the strategy stands exactly the same. On the other hand, it is worth stressing that the key ideas underlying our recipe also apply (when suitably adapted) to a much larger framework, including concrete examples: we work on this in
[
GG6
]
.

6.
Quantization and specialization: FoMpQUESAs vs. MpLSbAs

In this section we consider the the interplay of specialization
— performed onto quantum objects, namely our FoMpQUESAs —
and, conversely, of quantization — applied to semiclassical objects, namely our MpLSbA’s.

To begin with, we shall see that every FoMpQUESA yields by
specialization a suitable MpLSbA; conversely, for any MpLSbA
there exists at least one quantization, in the form of a well-defined FoMpQUESA.
Second, we shall study the interaction between the process of specialization (at
\,\hbar=0\,
)
of any FoMpQUESA and the process of deformation
— either by (toral) twist or by (toral)
2
–cocycle —
of that same FoMpQUEA or of the MpLSbA which is its semiclassical limit.
In particular, we will find that, in a natural sense,

the two processes commute with each other
.

6.1.
Deformation vs. specialization for FoMpQUESA’s and MpLSbA’s

In §
4.1
we recalled that any QUESA defines,
by specialization at
\,\hbar=0\,
, a well-defined “semiclassical limit”,
given by a specific Lie superbialgebra — or more precisely by the universal
enveloping superalgebra of the latter. In addition, by
Theorem
4.2.12
we know that our FoMpQUESA’s are
indeed QUESA’s, so they do have semiclassical limits in the previous sense.
We shall now go and see what these limits look like, i.e. what Lie superalgebras we do find in this way.

6.1.1
.

Formal MpQUESA’s vs. MpLSbA’s.

Let us fix a Cartan super-datum
\,(A\,,p)\,
, a matrix

\,P:={\big(\,p_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,

of Cartan type with associated Cartan matrix
A\,
,
and a realization
\,{\mathcal{R}}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,
of
P
.
Out of these data, we associate the FoMpQUEA
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
,
as in §
4.2
, and also the MpLSbA

\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}
introduced in
§
3.2
, where
we use the somewhat loose (yet obvious) notation such

\,\,\bar{{\mathcal{R}}}:={\mathcal{R}}\;(\,\text{mod}\;\hbar\,)\,
and

\,\bar{P}:=P\;(\,\text{mod}\;\hbar\,)\,
.

We begin by pointing out that, in a nutshell, FoMpQUESA’s and
MpLSbA’s are in bijection through the specialization/quantization process,
as one might expect:

Theorem 6.1.2
.

With assumptions as above,
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
is a
QUESA

in the sense of §
4.1.2
, whose semiclassical limit is

U\big(\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},\,p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\,\big)\,
.
In other words,
\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},\,p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}

is the specialization of
\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
, or — equivalently —

U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
is a quantization of
\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},\,p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\,
.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.2.12
we know that

U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
is a QUESA, so only have to describe its semiclassical limit.
In fact, we aim to prove that
\;U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big/\hbar\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
,
as a co-Poisson Hopf superalgebra, is isomorphic to

U\big(\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},\,p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\big)\,
.

First of all, the presentation of
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
implies that

\;U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\Big/\hbar\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
is generated by the cosets
(modulo
\hbar\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
) of the
F_{i}
’s,
T
’s and

E_{i}
’s (
\,i\in I\,
,
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
);
moreover, these cosets
\;\overline{X}:=X\!\!\mod\hbar\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\;

obey all relations induced modulo
\hbar

by the defining relations among the original generators
X
of
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
.
On the other hand, the Lie superbialgebra
\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},\,p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}

is endowed, by construction, with a presentation (as a Lie superalgebra) by generators
(the
F_{i}
’s,
T
’s and
E_{i}
’s) and relations,
and explicit formulas for the value of the Lie supercobracket
\delta

on the given generators. From this one gets an obvious presentation of

U\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}},\,p}\big)\,
,
where the generators are again the
F_{i}
’s,
T
’s and
E_{i}
’s
and the Poisson cobracket
\delta
on them is given by explicit formulas.

Comparing the presentation of
U\big(\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\big)

with that of
\;U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big/\hbar\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\;

we find that all the given relations among generators of the
latter superalgebra do correspond to identical relations among the
corresponding generators in the former: indeed, mapping
X
to
\overline{X}

(for all
\,X\in\big\{E_{i}\,,F_{i}\,\big|\,i\in I\,\big\}\cup\mathfrak{h}\,
)
turns every given relation among the
X
’s
into a same-look relation among the
\overline{X}
’s.
Eventually, this provides a well-defined epimorphism of Hopf superalgebras

\hskip-11.0pt\phi\,:\,U\big(\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},\,p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\big)\,\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\Big/\hbar\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\;\;,\quad E_{i}\,\mapsto\,\overline{E_{i}}\,,\;T\,\mapsto\,\overline{T}\,,\;F_{i}\,\mapsto\,\overline{F_{i}}

(6.1)

(
\,i\in I\,
,
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
); furthermore, comparing the formulas on both sides for the co-Poisson superbracket
we see that this is also a
co-Poisson
super Hopf epimorphism.

On the other hand, scalar restriction — via

\;{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big/\hbar\,{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\,\cong\,\Bbbk\;

— makes
U\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}},\,p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\big)

into a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–superalgebra. Then the same argument about
relations yields a well-defined
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–superalgebra epimorphism

\psi\,{}^{\prime}:U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow U\big(\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},\,p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\big)\;\;,\quad E_{i}\,\mapsto\,E_{i}\,,\;T\,\mapsto\,T\,,\;F_{i}\,\mapsto\,F_{i}\;\quad(\,i\in I\,,\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,)

whose kernel contains
\,\hbar\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
;
therefore, a
\Bbbk
–superalgebra epimorphism

\psi\,:\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\Big/\hbar\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow\,U\big(\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},\,p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\big)\;\;,\quad\overline{E_{i}}\,\mapsto\,E_{i}\,,\;\overline{T}\,\mapsto\,T\,,\;\overline{F_{i}}\,\mapsto\,F_{i}

(6.2)

(
\,i\in I\,
,
\,T\in\mathfrak{h}\,
)
is induced too. Now comparing (
6.1
)
and (
6.2
) shows that

\phi
and
\psi
are inverse to each other, so

\psi
is a super
Hopf
morphism too and we are done.
∎

6.2.
Intertwining deformation and specialization

We shall now compare the process of deformation at the quantum level
and at the semiclassical level, finding a very neat outcome:
in a nutshell,
deformation (by twist or by 2–cocycle)

commutes
with specialization
.

6.2.1
.

Intertwining twist deformation and specialization.

Fix again a Cartan super-datum
(A,p)
,
a matrix
\,P:={\big(\,p_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,

of Cartan type, a realization

\,{\mathcal{R}}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,
of it,
and the associated FoMpQUESA
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
and MpLSbA

\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\,
.
In the free
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–module
\mathfrak{h}
of finite rank
\,t\,
,
we fix a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–basis
\,{\big\{H_{g}\big\}}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\,
,
with
\,|\mathcal{G}|=\operatorname{rk}(\mathfrak{h})=t\,
.

Choose any antisymmetric matrix

\;\Psi=\big(\psi_{gk}\big)_{g,k\in\mathcal{G}}\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\;
.
Out of it, set

\hfil\displaystyle j_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}}\;:=\;{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\,\overline{\psi_{gk}}\;\overline{H_{g}}\otimes\overline{H_{k}}\,\;\in\;\,\overline{\mathfrak{h}}\otimes\overline{\mathfrak{h}}\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{as in \eqref{eq: def_twist_Lie-bialg_2nd-time}}

\hfil\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}\;:=\;\exp\Big(\hbar\;2^{-1}\,{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\psi_{gk}\,H_{g}\otimes H_{k}\Big)\,\;\in\;\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,\widehat{\otimes}\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\qquad\text{as in \eqref{eq: Resh-twist_F-uPhgd}}

so
j_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}}
is a (toral)
twist

for the
Lie superbialgebra

\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}

is a (toral)
twist
for the
Hopf superalgebra

U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
, we have the deformation

{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\big)}^{j_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}

of
\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}
by the (Lie) twist

j_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}}
and the deformation

{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}
of
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})

by the (Hopf) twist
{\mathcal{F}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}\,
.

Again out of
\Psi\,
, we define the matrix
P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}

and its realization
\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}\!:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}\big)
,
as in Proposition
2.2.2
;  then we have

U_{\!P_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi},\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi},p}(\mathfrak{g})
and

\mathfrak{g}^{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi},\,p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}\,
,
again mutually linked by a quantization/specialization relationship.

We can now state our result, which in particular claims (in a nutshell)
that
“deformation by twist commutes with specialization”
.

Theorem 6.2.2
.

\Big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\!\Big)^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}
is a QUESA,
whose semiclassical limit is isomorphic to

\,U\big({\big(\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\big)}^{j_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}}}\big)\,
.
Indeed,
\;\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\Big)^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}\cong\,U_{\!P_{\,{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}},\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}(\mathfrak{g})\;\,
and

\;{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\big)}^{j_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}}}\cong\,\mathfrak{g}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi},\,p}^{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}\;\,
.

Proof.

The claim follows, as direct application, from the isomorphisms

\Big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\Big)^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}\,\cong\;U_{\!P_{\,{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}},\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}(\mathfrak{g})\;\,,\!\quad U_{\!P_{\,{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}},\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}(\mathfrak{g})\Big/\hbar\,U_{\!P_{\,{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}},\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}(\mathfrak{g})\;\cong\;U_{\hbar}\big(\mathfrak{g}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}^{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}\big)\;\,,\!\quad\mathfrak{g}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}^{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}\;\cong\,{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\big)}^{j_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle\Psi}}}}

which come from Theorem
5.1.2
,
Theorem
6.1.2
and
Theorem
3.3.3
.
∎

Remark 6.2.3
.

The above result is proved by direct inspection;
it can also be deduced as an application of a general result about
the “semiclassical limit” of a twist of a QUESA, namely

[
GG4
, Theorem 3.1.2]
(the result there is stated for the non-super case,
but it extends straightforwardly to the super setup).

6.2.4
.

Intertwining 2–cocycle deformation and specialization.

Now we analyse what happens when one combines
deformations by 2–cocycle

— for both FoMpQUESAs and MpLSbA’s — with the specialization process.

We begin with a Cartan super-datum
(A,p)
, a matrix

\,P\!:=\!{\big(\,p_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\in M_{n}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)

of Cartan type, a realization

\,{\mathcal{R}}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,

of
P
, and a fixed
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–basis
\,{\big\{H_{g}\big\}}_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\,

of
\mathfrak{h}\,
, with
\,|\mathcal{G}|=\operatorname{rk}(\mathfrak{h})=t\,
.
Then
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
and
\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}

are defined, interlocked through quantization/specialization.

Like in §
5.2.2
, we fix a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–bilinear
map
\;\chi:\mathfrak{h}\times\mathfrak{h}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\;

which is antisymmetric and obeys (
3.12
).
Taking everything modulo
\hbar\,
, this
\chi

defines a similar antisymmetric,
\Bbbk
–bilinear map

\;\gamma:=\big(\,\chi\!\mod\hbar\,\big):\mathfrak{h}_{0}\times\mathfrak{h}_{0}\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\Bbbk\;

— with
\,\mathfrak{h}_{0}:=\mathfrak{h}\Big/\hbar\,\mathfrak{h}\,=\,\overline{\mathfrak{h}}\,
— which obeys (
3.12
)
again, up to replacing “
\,\chi\,
” with “
\,\gamma\,
”.
Following §
5.2.7
, we construct out of

\chi
a
{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}
–valued toral polar–
2
–cocycle

\sigma_{\chi}:U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\otimes U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}\,
,
and then the deformed Hopf superalgebra
\,{\big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}\,
.
Similarly, we construct as in §
3.4

(but with “
\,\gamma\,
” replacing “
\,\chi\,
”) a toral
2
–cocycle

\gamma_{\mathfrak{g}}
for the Lie bialgebra
\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\,
,
and out of it the
2
–cocycle deformed Lie superbialgebra

\,{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}},p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}}\big)}_{\gamma\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${}_{\mathfrak{g}}$}}\,
.

Still out of
\chi\,
, we define the matrix
P_{(\chi)}

and its realization
\,{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)}:=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\,\Pi_{(\chi)}\,,\,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,
,  as in Proposition
2.3.2
;
similarly, out of
\gamma
we get the matrix
P_{(\gamma)}

and its realization
\,{\mathcal{R}}_{(\gamma)}\,
: then

\,P_{(\gamma)}=\bar{P}_{(\chi)}\,
and

\,{\mathcal{R}}_{(\gamma)}=\bar{{\mathcal{R}}}_{(\chi)}\,
.
Finally, attached to the latter we have

\,U_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},p}(\mathfrak{g})\,
and

\,\mathfrak{g}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}_{(\gamma)},\,p}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{(\gamma)}}=\mathfrak{g}^{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}}_{(\chi)},\,p}_{\bar{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}_{(\chi)}}\,
,
again connected via quantization/specialization.

Next result claims that
“deformation by 2–cocycle commutes with specialization”
.

Theorem 6.2.5
.

{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}
is a QUESA, with semiclassical limit
\,U\Big(\!{(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P})}_{\gamma\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${}_{\mathfrak{g}}$}}\Big)\,
.

Proof.

The claim follows, as direct application, from the two isomorphisms

{\big(\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{\chi}}\!\cong U_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)}}(\mathfrak{g})\;\;\;,\qquad U_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)}}(\mathfrak{g})\Big/\hbar\,U_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}^{\,{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)}}(\mathfrak{g})\;\cong\;U_{\hbar}\Big(\mathfrak{g}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{(\gamma)}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}_{(\gamma)}}\Big)

of co-Poisson Hopf superalgebras (cf. Theorem
5.2.13
and Theorem
6.1.2
) and the isomorphism of Lie superbialgebras

\;\mathfrak{g}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}}_{(\gamma)},\,p}_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}_{(\gamma)}}\;\cong\,{\big(\mathfrak{g}^{\raise 1.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{R},p$}}_{\scriptscriptstyle P}\big)}_{\gamma\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${}_{\mathfrak{g}}$}}\;

— cf. Theorem
3.4.3
.
∎

Remark 6.2.6
.

The previous result is proved by direct inspection; nevertheless, we can deduce it also as an application of a general result on the “semiclassical limit” of a polar-2–cocyle of a QUESA, namely
[
GG4
, Theorem 3.3.72]
(although the result there is stated for the non-super case, it does extends easily to the super setup).

6.3.
A global synopsis

In this paper we studied multiparametric versions of formal QUESA’s,
as well as their semiclassical limits. All these are presented by generators and relations,
so their very definition highlights the relation between
the multiparameters and the action of a fixed commutative subsuperalgebra:
this is encoded in the notion of
realization

of a multiparameter matrix
P
, much like for Kac-Moody algebras.
This allows us to relate the quantum objects with their semiclassical limit,
and also multiparameter objects with standard ones:
the latter step goes through deformation(s).

In conclusion we can say, a bit loosely, that:

(a)

multiparameters are encoded in realizations;

(b)

FoMpQUESA’s are quantizations of MpLSbA’s;

(c)

multiparameter objects are given by deformation of
either the (super)algebra or the (super)coalgebra structure of (Yamane’s)
uniparameter
objects.

Finally, we display a “pictorial sketch” of the global picture, as follows:

\textstyle{U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}_{\Psi},\,p}_{\!P_{\Psi},\,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\,\cong{\Big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\Big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Psi}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\!\ }
Theorem
5.1.3
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\,\ U\Big(\mathfrak{g}^{\bar{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\Psi},\,p}_{\bar{P}_{\Psi}}\Big)\,\cong\,U\Big(\!{\Big(\mathfrak{g}^{\bar{{\mathcal{R}}},\,p}_{\bar{P}}\Big)}^{j_{\bar{\Psi}}}\Big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
Theorem
6.1.2
Theorem
3.3.4
\textstyle{U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
Theorem
5.2.15
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ U\Big(\mathfrak{g}^{\bar{{\mathcal{R}}},\,p}_{\bar{P}}\Big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
Theorem
6.1.2
Theorem
3.4.3
\textstyle{{\Big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\Big)}_{\!\sigma_{\chi}}\!\cong\,U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}_{(\chi)},\,p}_{\!P_{(\chi)},\,\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\,\ }
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\,\ U\Big(\!{\Big(\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\mathcal{R}}}},\,p}_{\bar{P}}\Big)}_{\!\bar{\chi}}\,\Big)\,\cong\,U\Big(\mathfrak{g}^{{\bar{{\mathcal{R}}}_{(\chi)}},\,p}_{\bar{P}_{(\chi)}}\,\Big)}
Theorem
6.1.2

In the above drawing, each horizontal arrow (with dotted shaft) denotes a “quantization/specialization (upwards/downwards) relationship” — which involves the “continuous parameter”
\hbar
— whereas each vertical arrow (having a waving shaft) stands for a relationship “via deformation” — that involves “discrete parameters”.

7.
Polynomial multiparameter QUESA’s and their deformations

Our FoMpQUESA’s are a close analogue, for multiparameter depending supergroups,
of the well-known (uniparameter) QUEA’s
U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
by Drinfeld,
attached with finite-dimensional semisimple Lie algebras
\mathfrak{g}\,
.
Now, a
polynomial
version of Drinfeld’s
U_{\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})

is also available, originally due to Jimbo and Lusztig
— and a multiparameter version of that is known as well, cf.
[
GG2
]
.
Similarly, we will now introduce a “polynomial version” of MpQUESA’s,
and we will shortly present its main features.

7.1.
Multiparameters of polynomial type

Given
\,n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\,
and
\,I:=\{1,\dots,n\}\,
,
let
\,\mathbb{Z}\big[\,\mathbf{x}^{\pm 1}\big]:=\mathbb{Z}\big[{\big\{x_{ij}^{\pm 1}\big\}}_{i,j\in I}\,\big]\,

be the ring of Laurent polynomials with coefficients in
\mathbb{Z}

in the indeterminates
x_{ij}
(
\,i,j\in I\,
),
and let
\,A:={\big(a_{ij}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\,
be an indecomposable,
symmetrizable Cartan matrix as in §
4.2
.
Consider the quotient ring

\;{\mathbb{Z}_{A}\big[\mathbf{q}^{\pm 1}\big]}\,:=\,\mathbb{Z}\big[\,\mathbf{x}^{\pm 1}\big]\bigg/\Big({\big\{x_{ij}\,x_{ji}-x_{ii}^{\,a_{ij}}\big\}}_{i,j\in I}\Big)\;

and denote by
q_{ij}
the coset of every
x_{ij}

(for
\,i,j\in I\,
) in
\,{\mathbb{Z}_{A}\big[\mathbf{q}^{\pm 1}\big]}\,
.
This is the ring of global sections of an affine scheme over

\mathbb{Z}\,
, call it
\mathfrak{C}_{A}\,
.

From all the identities
\;q_{ij}\,q_{ji}=q_{ii}^{\,a_{ij}}\;

in
{\mathbb{Z}_{A}\big[\mathbf{q}^{\pm 1}\big]}\,
, from the indecomposability of
A
and
from the symmetry of
\,D\,A\,
— i.e.,

\,d_{i}\,a_{ij}=d_{j}\,a_{ji}\,
for all
\,i,j\in I\,
—
one finds that there exists
\,j_{\raise-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0$}}\in I\,

such that
\,q_{ii}=q_{j_{\raise-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}\,j_{\raise-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{\,d_{i}}\,
.
From this and the relations between the
q_{ij}
’s, it is easy to
argue that
\mathfrak{C}_{A}
is a torus, of dimension

\,{{\,n\,}\choose{\,2\,}}+1\,
: in particular,
it is irreducible, hence
{\mathbb{Z}_{A}\big[\mathbf{q}^{\pm 1}\big]}
is a domain.

Finally, we extend the ring
\,{\mathbb{Z}_{A}\big[\mathbf{q}^{\pm 1}\big]}\,

using formal square roots, as follows. First, we consider the ring extension

{\dot{R}_{\,\mathbf{q}}}
of
\,{\mathbb{Z}_{A}\big[\mathbf{q}^{\pm 1}\big]}\,
generated by a square root of

\,q_{j_{\raise-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}\,j_{\raise-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\,
,  namely

\;{\dot{R}_{\,\mathbf{q}}}\,:=\,\big(\,{\mathbb{Z}_{A}\big[\mathbf{q}^{\pm 1}\big]}\,\big)[x]\bigg/\!\Big(x^{2}-\,q_{j_{\raise-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}\,j_{\raise-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\Big)\;
so that

\,q:=q_{j_{\raise-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}\,j_{\raise-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{\,1/2}:=\,\overline{x}\,\in\,{\dot{R}_{\,\mathbf{q}}}\,
.
We still denote by
q_{ij}
the images in
{\dot{R}_{\,\mathbf{q}}}

of the “old” elements with same name in
{\mathbb{Z}_{A}\big[\mathbf{q}^{\pm 1}\big]}\,
.
Note that

\;{\dot{R}_{\,\mathbf{q}}}\,\cong\,\big(\,\mathbb{Z}\big[\,{\big\{x_{ij}^{\pm 1}\big\}}_{i,j\in I}\,\big]\,\big)[x]\bigg/\Big(\,{\big\{x_{ij}\,x_{ji}-x_{ii}^{\,a_{ij}}\big\}}_{i,j\in I}\,\cup\big\{x^{2}-x_{j_{\raise-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}\,j_{\raise-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\big\}\Big)\;
.
In turn, we define also

{\dot{R}_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}\;:=\;\mathbb{Z}\big[{\big\{\xi_{ij}^{\pm 1/2}\big\}}_{i,j\in I}\,\big]\big[\xi^{\pm 1/2}\big]\bigg/\Big({\Big\{\xi_{ij}^{1/2}\,\xi_{ji}^{1/2}\!-{\big(\xi_{i{}i}^{1/2\,}\big)}^{a_{ij}}\Big\}}_{i,j\in I}\!\cup\Big\{\!{\big(\xi^{1/2\,}\big)}^{2}\!-\xi_{j_{\raise-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}\,j_{\raise-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{\,1/2}\Big\}\Big)

which is again a domain,
and we denote by
q_{ij}^{\pm 1/2}
and by
q^{\pm 1/2}
the coset of

\xi_{ij}^{\pm 1/2}
and
\xi^{\pm 1/2}
, respectively, in
{\dot{R}_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}\,
.
Then set also
\,q_{i}^{\pm 1}:=q^{\pm d_{i}}\,
for all
\,i\in I\,
;
in particular,
\;q_{j_{\raise-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}\,j_{\raise-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}^{\,1/2}:=q=q_{j_{\raise-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0$}}}\;
. Finally,
inside the field of fractions of
{\dot{R}_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}
we consider the subring

{R_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}
generated by
{\dot{R}_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}
and the elements

\,{\big(\,q_{i}^{+1}-q_{i}^{-1}\,\big)}^{-1}\,
, for all
\,i\in I\,
.

At last, for later use we introduce notation

\,k_{ij}:=q_{ij}^{1/2}\,q_{ji}^{-1/2}\;
for all
\,i\,,j\in I\,
.

7.2.
Polynomial multiparameter QUESA’s

We resume all assumptions and notation as in §
4.2

and §
7.1
above. In particular,
we fix the multiparameter matrix
\,\mathbf{q}:={\big(\,q_{ij}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\,

and the ring
{R_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}
associated with some fixed symmetrizable,
indecomposable Cartan matrix
\,A={\big(a_{ij}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\,

as in §
4.2
: we shall then say that the multiparameter

\,\mathbf{q}:={\big(\,q_{ij}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\,
is of
Cartan type
A\,
.

Definition 7.2.1
.

Let
\,\big(\,A:={\big(\,a_{i,j}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\,,\,p\,\big)\,

be a fixed Cartan super-datum, and
\,\mathbf{q}:={\big(\,q_{ij}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\,

a multiparameter matrix of Cartan type
A\,
,

(a)
We define the
polynomial multiparameter quantum
universal super enveloping algebra
— in short

polynomial MpQUESA, or simply PolMpQUESA
—
with multiparameter
\mathbf{q}
as follows. It is the unital, associative

{R_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}
–superalgebra
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
generated by the elements

K_{i}^{\pm 1}
,
L_{i}^{\pm 1}
,
\,E_{i}\,
,
F_{i}\,
(for all
\,i\in I\,
),
with
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
–grading given by setting

\;\big|K_{i}^{\pm 1}\big|:={\bar{0}}=:\big|L_{i}^{\pm 1}\big|\;
,

\;|E_{i}|:=p(i)=:|F_{i}|\;\big(\,\forall\;i\in I\,\big)\,
,
and
with relations

\hfil\displaystyle K_{i}^{\pm 1}L^{\pm 1}_{j}\,=\,L^{\pm 1}_{j}K^{\pm 1}_{i}\;\;,\qquad K_{i}^{\pm 1}K^{\mp 1}_{i}\,=\,1\,=\,L^{\pm 1}_{i}L^{\mp 1}_{i}

\hfil\displaystyle K_{i}^{\pm 1}K^{\pm 1}_{j}\,=\,K^{\pm 1}_{j}K^{\pm 1}_{i}\;\;,\qquad L_{i}^{\pm 1}L_{j}^{\pm 1}\,=\,L_{j}^{\pm 1}L_{i}^{\pm 1}

\hfil\displaystyle K_{i}\,E_{j}\,K_{i}^{-1}\,=\,q_{ij}\,E_{j}\;\;,\qquad L_{i}\,E_{j}\,L_{i}^{-1}\,=\,q_{ji}^{-1}\,E_{j}

\hfil\displaystyle K_{i}\,F_{j}\,K_{i}^{-1}\,=\,q_{ij}^{-1}\,F_{j}\;\;,\qquad L_{i}\,F_{j}\,L_{i}^{-1}\,=\,q_{ji}\,F_{j}

\hfil\displaystyle[E_{i}\,,F_{j}]\,=\,\delta_{i,j}\,\frac{\,K_{i}-L_{i}\,}{\,q_{i}^{+1}-q_{i}^{-1}\,}

as well as relations (
4.5
), (
4.6
),
(
4.7
) and all of relations (
4.8
) through
(
4.12
) both for the
E_{i}
’s and for the
F_{i}
’s alike
.

(b)
We define the
Cartan subalgebra

U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})
of a
PolMpQUESA
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
as being the unital
{R_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}
–subsuperalgebra of
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})

generated by the subset
\,\big\{K_{i}^{\pm 1},L_{i}^{\pm 1}\,\big|\,i\in I\,\big\}\,
.

(c)
We define the
positive, resp. negative, nilpotent subsuperalgebra

U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{n}_{+})
, resp.
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{n}_{-})
, of a PolMpQUESA
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})

to be the unital
{R_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}
–subsuperalgebra of
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})

generated by the subset
\,\big\{E_{i}\,\big|\,i\in I\,\big\}\,
,
resp. the subset
\,\big\{F_{i}\,\big|\,i\in I\,\big\}\,
.

(d)
We define the
positive, resp. negative, Borel subsuperalgebra

U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{b}_{+})
, resp.
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{b}_{-})
, of a PolMpQUESA
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})

to be the unital
{R_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}
–subsuperalgebra of
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})

generated by the subset
\,\big\{E_{i}\,,K_{i}^{\pm 1}\,\big|\,i\in I\,\big\}\,
,
resp. the subset
\,\big\{F_{i}\,,L_{i}^{\pm 1}\,\big|\,i\in I\,\big\}\,
.

(e)
The above are definitions for PolMpQUESA’s (or their subalgebras)
over the “generic multiparameter”
\mathbf{q}
with entries in
{R_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}\,
.
If
\,\overline{\mathbf{q}}={\big(\,\overline{q}_{ij}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\,

is any square matrix of size
n

with entries in

\Bbbk^{\times}

which are not roots of 1
, we can define the
“specialized” PolMpQUESA
U_{\overline{\mathbf{q}}\,}(\mathfrak{g})
as a
\Bbbk
–algebra
which has exactly the same presentation of
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
but for the

\overline{q}_{ij}
’s replacing the
q_{ij}
’s.
Similarly, we define “specialized” Cartan, nilpotent and
Borel subsuperalgebras
U_{\overline{\mathbf{q}}\,}(\mathfrak{h})\,
,

U_{\overline{\mathbf{q}}\,}(\mathfrak{n}_{\pm})
and
U_{\overline{\mathbf{q}}\,}(\mathfrak{b}_{\pm})\,
.

\underline{\text{Remark}}
:
The definitions in

(a)
through
(d)
are given over the ground ring
{R_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}

that is the
least
one for which the definitions make sense.
Another natural choice would be to pick as ground ring the field of fractions of
{R_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}\,
.
The further results hereafter then still hold true, up to minimal adjustments.

\diamondsuit

The key point about PolMpQUESA’s is the following:

Theorem 7.2.2
.

Every PolMpQUESA
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
is a
Hopf superalgebra over
{R_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}\,
,  with coproduct, counit and antipode given by (for all
\,i\in I\,
)

\hfil\displaystyle\Delta\big(K_{i}^{\pm 1}\big)\,=\,K_{i}^{\pm 1}\otimes K_{i}^{\pm 1}\;\;,\qquad\epsilon\big(K_{i}^{\pm 1}\big)\,=\,1\;\;,\qquad\mathcal{S}\big(K_{i}^{\pm 1}\big)\,=\,K_{i}^{\mp 1}

\hfil\displaystyle\hskip 3.0pt\Delta\big(L_{i}^{\pm 1}\big)\,=\,L_{i}^{\pm 1}\otimes L_{i}^{\pm 1}\;\;,\;\qquad\epsilon\big(L_{i}^{\pm 1}\big)\,=\,1\;\;,\hskip 12.0pt\hskip 6.0pt\quad\mathcal{S}\big(L_{i}^{\pm 1}\big)\,=\,L_{i}^{\mp 1}

\hfil\displaystyle\Delta(E_{i})\,=\,E_{i}\otimes 1+K_{i}\otimes E_{i}\;\;,\hskip 9.0pt\quad\epsilon(E_{i})\,=\,0\;\;,\hskip 13.0pt\quad\mathcal{S}(E_{i})\,=\,-K_{i}^{-1}E_{i}

\hfil\displaystyle\hskip 7.0pt\Delta(F_{i})\,=\,F_{i}\otimes L_{i}+1\otimes F_{i}\;\;,\hskip 13.0pt\quad\epsilon(F_{i})\,=\,0\;\;,\hskip 17.0pt\quad\mathcal{S}(F_{i})\,=\,-F_{i}{L_{i}}^{-1}

and Borel subsuperalgebras
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{b}_{+})
and
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{b}_{-})
are

Hopf
sub-superalgebras of
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
.

Proof.

Let
\,DA\,
the symmetrized Cartan matrix associated
with the multiparameter
\mathbf{q}
(see above).
Let
\mathbb{K}_{n}:=\Bbbk\big(\{\,x_{ij}\,|\,i,j\!\in\!I,i<j\,\}\big)\,

be the field of rational functions in
{n\choose 2}
indeterminates

\,x_{ij}\,
— with
\,i<j\,
in
\,I:=\{1,\dots,n\}\,
—
let
\,\mathbb{K}_{n}[[\hbar]]\,
the associated ring of formal power series in

\hbar\,
, and let
\,\mathbb{K}_{n}(\!(\hbar)\!)\,

be the field of formal Laurent series in
\hbar\,
. Now pick the matrix
\,P_{\underline{x}}:={\big(\,p_{ij}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\in M_{n}\big(\mathbb{K}_{n}[[\hbar]]\big)\,
given by

p_{ij}:=d_{i}\,a_{ij}+x_{ij}\;\;\;\forall\;i<j\;,\!\!\qquad p_{ii}:=d_{i}\,a_{ii}\;\;\;,\!\!\qquad p_{ij}:=d_{i}\,a_{ij}-x_{ji}\;\;\;\forall\;i>j

so that
\,P_{\underline{x}}+P_{\underline{x}}^{\,T}=2\,D\,A\,
.
Then, there exists a unique
\Bbbk
–algebra morphism, say

\,\varphi:{R_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{K}_{n}(\!(\hbar)\!)\,
,
given by
\,q_{ij}\mapsto\varphi(q_{ij})=\exp\big(\hbar\,p_{ij}\big)\,
,
which is clearly injective since the
q_{hk}
’s only obey the relations

\,q_{ij}\,q_{ji}=q_{ii}^{\,a_{ij}}\,
and the
p_{hk}
’s
the relations
\,p_{ij}+p_{ji}=2\,d_{i}\,a_{ij}\,
.

Choose any realization
\,{\mathcal{R}}=\big(\,\mathfrak{h}\,,\Pi\,,\Pi^{\vee}\,\big)\,

of
P_{\underline{x}}

over the ground ring
\mathbb{K}_{n}[[\hbar]]\,
,
which is
straight
and
split

— its existence is granted by Proposition
2.1.5
(a)
.
Out of these data, we define the FoMpQUESA
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,

still over the ground ring
\mathbb{K}_{n}[[\hbar]]\,
,
and also its scalar extension

\,\mathbb{K}_{n}(\!(\hbar)\!)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}_{n}[[\hbar]]}U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
.
Using the monomorphism
\,\varphi:{R_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}\!\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow\mathbb{K}_{n}(\!(\hbar)\!)\,

mentioned above, by scalar restriction through it we consider

\,\mathbb{K}_{n}(\!(\hbar)\!)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}_{n}[[\hbar]]}U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,

as an
{R_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}
–superalgebra too.

Inside
\,\mathbb{K}_{n}(\!(\hbar)\!)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}_{n}[[\hbar]]}U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,

we look at the
{R_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}
–subsuperalgebra
\dot{U}_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathfrak{g})

generated by
\;\Big\{\,\dot{E}_{i}\,,\,\dot{K}_{i}^{\pm 1}:=\exp\big(\pm\hbar\,T_{i}^{+}\big)\,,\,\dot{L}_{i}^{\pm 1}:=\exp\big(\mp\hbar\,T_{i}^{-}\big)\,,\,\dot{F}_{i}\;\Big|\;i\in I\,\Big\}\,
.
An easy calculation, along with the fact that the set

\,\Pi^{\vee}=\big\{\,T_{i}^{+},T_{i}^{-}\,\big|\,i\in I\,\big\}\,

is linearly independent, proves that
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
is actually contained in
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
;
moreover, the defining presentation for
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
induces for

\dot{U}_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathfrak{g})
the same presentation as for
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
.
In short,
\dot{U}_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathfrak{g})
is an isomorphic copy of
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})

embedded inside
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
,  via
\;\;\varPhi:U_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathfrak{g})\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\cong}}}\,\dot{U}_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathfrak{g})\;\;\Big(\,E_{i}\mapsto\dot{E}_{i}\,,\;K_{i}^{\pm 1}\mapsto\dot{K}_{i}^{\pm 1},\,L_{i}^{\pm 1}\mapsto\dot{L}_{i}^{\pm 1},\,F_{i}\mapsto\dot{F}_{i}\,\Big)\;
.

Yet another computation yields
\,\Delta\big(\dot{K}_{i}^{\pm 1}\big)=\dot{K}_{i}^{\pm 1}\otimes\dot{K}_{i}^{\pm 1}\,
and

\,\Delta\big(\dot{L}_{i}^{\pm 1}\big)=\dot{L}_{i}^{\pm 1}\otimes\dot{L}_{i}^{\pm 1}\,

inside
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
: together with similar formulas for the antipode
(and for the
E_{i}
’s and the
F_{i}
’s as well), this implies that
the topological Hopf structure of
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
does restrict to a (non-topological)
Hopf structure on
\dot{U}_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathfrak{g})\,
. The outcome is that

\dot{U}_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathfrak{g})
is indeed a (non-topological!) Hopf superalgebra over

{R_{\,\mathbf{q}}^{\,\scriptscriptstyle{\sqrt{\phantom{I}}}}}
, hence the same holds true for
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})

via pull-back of the structure through the superalgebra isomorphism

\varPhi
mentioned above. Finally, tracking the whole construction
we see that this Hopf structure onto
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
is indeed described by
the formulas in the main claim.

The last claim about Borel subsuperalgebras then is clear.
∎

The proof of next claim is contained in that of
Theorem
7.2.2
right above:

Corollary 7.2.3
.

Every
polynomial
MpQUESA embeds as a Hopf sub-superalgebra
inside (the suitable scalar extension of) any split straight
formal

MpQUESA defined by the same Cartan super-datum. ∎

Specializing the “generic parameters”
q_{ij}

to specific values in
\Bbbk
we get at once

Corollary 7.2.4
.

Every
specialized
PolMpQUESA
U_{\overline{\mathbf{q}}\,}(\mathfrak{g})

is a (non-topological) Hopf superalgebra over
\,\Bbbk\,
,  with
Hopf structure given as in
Theorem
7.2.2
. ∎

A special case in particular stands out:

Definition 7.2.5
.

We define the
standard
PolMpQUESA
U_{\check{\mathbf{q}}}(\mathfrak{g})

as the specialized PolMpQUESA for the “standard multiparameter”

\,\check{\mathbf{q}}:={\big(\,\check{q}_{ij}=q^{d_{i}a_{ij}}\big)}_{i,j\in I}\,
.

N.B.:
this choice corresponds to specializing the parameters

\,x_{ij}=0\,
in the proof above, whereas
the parameter
q
stands untouched.

Finally, from Theorem
4.2.13
we easily see that the
triangular decompositions for formal MpQUESA’s imply parallel decompositions for their polynomial
counterparts (just because the latter embed into the former, as shown in the proof of
Theorem
7.2.2
above).
The precise statement, whose proof is immediate, reads as follows:

Theorem 7.2.6
.

Every PolMpQUESA
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
admits
triangular decompositions

\hfil\displaystyle U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{n}_{+})\otimes U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})\otimes U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{n}_{-})\;\;\cong\;\;U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\;\cong\;U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{n}_{-})\otimes U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})\otimes U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{n}_{+})

\hfil\displaystyle U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{b}_{+})\otimes U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{b}_{-})\;\cong\;U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\;\;\cong\;\;U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{b}_{-})\otimes U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{b}_{+})

and similar decompositions also occur for Borel subsuperalgebras, namely

U_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathfrak{n}_{\pm})\otimes U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})\;\;\cong\;\;U_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathfrak{b}_{\pm})\;\cong\;U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})\otimes U_{\mathbf{q}}(\mathfrak{n}_{\pm})

A similar statement holds true for all

specialized
PolMpQUESA’s as well. ∎

7.3.
Deformations of polynomial MpQUESA’s

We discuss now how the construction of deformations by (toral)
twist or by (toral) polar–2–cocycles somehow “restrict” from formal MpQUESA’s
to
polynomial
ones — bearing in mind that the latter embed as (Hopf)
sub-superalgebras of the former.

In short, stepping from the “formal” setup to the “polynomial” one,
things get more tricky for deformations by (toral) twist, whereas they get
instead simpler for deformations by (toral) polar-2-cocycles.
We present these results a bit quickly, yet the interested reader will
certainly be able to fill in further details as needed.

7.3.1
.

Deformations by toral twist for PolMpQUESA’s.

Let
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
be some PolMpQUESA, and
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
some split,
straight FoMpQUESA such that the former embeds inside the latter,
as in Corollary
7.2.3
.
Let us consider a toral twist for
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
as in §
5.1.1
, say

\;{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}\,:=\,\exp\Big(\hskip 1.0pt\hbar\,\,2^{-1}\,{\textstyle\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}}\phi_{gk}\,H_{g}\otimes H_{k}\Big)\;
.
This belongs to
\,{U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})}^{\otimes 2}
, but
not
to

\,{U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})}^{\otimes 2}
, unless in the trivial case

\,\Phi=\big(\phi_{gk}\big)_{g,k\in\mathcal{G}}=0\,
;
hence in no way any such a toral twist for
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})

can be seen as a toral twist for
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
. Nevertheless,

for suitable choices of

\,\Phi=\big(\phi_{gk}\big)_{g,k\in\mathcal{G}}\in\mathfrak{so}_{t}\big({\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\big)\,
,
hence of
{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}\,
, the deformed coproduct
\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}
of

\,{\big(U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}}
maps
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
inside

{U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})}^{\otimes 2}
, hence it is indeed a new, “twist-deformed”
coproduct for the Hopf algebra
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
.

In order to prove the above statement clearly, we note that, directly
from the proof of Theorem
5.1.2
,
we see that the property that

\,\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big(U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)\subseteq{U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})}^{\otimes 2}\,

does hold true if and only if

\;\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\ell}:=e^{+\hbar\,2^{-1}\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}H_{k}}\in U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})\;

and similarly

\;\mathcal{K}_{\Phi,\ell}:=e^{+\hbar\,2^{-1}\sum_{g,k=1}^{t}\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})\,\phi_{kg}H_{k}}\in U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})\;

— for all
\,\ell\in I\,
— hence if and only if

\hskip-7.0pt2^{-1}{\textstyle\sum\limits_{g,k=1}^{t}}\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})\,\phi_{gk}\,H_{k}\;\in\;\textsl{Span}_{\mathbb{Z}}\big(\Pi^{\vee}\big)\;\ni\;2^{-1}{\textstyle\sum\limits_{g,k=1}^{t}}\alpha_{\ell}(H_{g})\,\phi_{kg}\,H_{k}\hskip 1.0pt\quad\big(\,\forall\;\ell\in I\,\big)

(7.1)

but since the matrix
\,\Phi=\big(\phi_{ij}\big)_{i,j\in I}\,

is antisymmetric, the double set of conditions above is equivalent to half
of them, say the left-hand side conditions in (
7.1
) above.

Now,
\Pi^{\vee}
can be completed to a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–basis of
\mathfrak{h}

— by the splitness assumption — so let us assume from scratch that the basis

\,{\{H_{k}\}}_{k=1,\dots,t;}\,
actually include
\Pi^{\vee}\,
;
even more, we assume that it coincide with it (i.e., the realization

{\mathcal{R}}
is also
minimal
); then the matrix

\,\Phi=\big(\phi_{ij}\big)_{i,j\in I}\,
can be written in blocks, say

\,\Phi=\begin{pmatrix}\;\Phi_{+}^{+}&\Phi_{+}^{-}\;\\
\;\Phi_{-}^{+}&\Phi_{-}^{-}\;\end{pmatrix}\,

where
\,\Phi_{\varepsilon}^{\eta}=\big(\,\phi_{ij}^{\varepsilon,\eta}\,\big)_{i,j\in I}\,
with

\;{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}\,:=\,\exp\Big(\hskip 1.0pt\hbar\,\,2^{-1}\,{\textstyle\sum_{\varepsilon,\eta\in\{+,-\}}}\,{\textstyle\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}}\,\phi_{ij}^{\varepsilon,\eta}\,T_{i}^{\varepsilon}\otimes T_{j}^{\eta}\Big)\;
.
Then the necessary and sufficient conditions for having

\,\Delta^{\scriptscriptstyle\Phi}\big(U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)\subseteq{U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})}^{\otimes 2}\,
are

{\textstyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}}\,2^{-1}\,\phi_{ij}^{\varepsilon,\eta}\,\alpha_{\ell}\big(T_{i}^{\varepsilon}\big)\;\in\;\mathbb{Z}\hskip 45.0pt\forall\;\;\ell\,,j\in I\,,\;\varepsilon,\eta\in\{+,-\}

(7.2)

Finally, recalling that
\,\alpha_{\ell}\big(T_{i}^{+}\big)=p_{i\ell}\,

and
\,\alpha_{\ell}\big(T_{i}^{-}\big)=p_{\ell{}i}\,
, conditions
(
7.2
) eventually read, in matrix form,
as “integrality conditions” of the following form:

P^{T}\!\cdot\Phi_{+}^{\eta}\,\in\,2\,M_{n}(\mathbb{Z})\;\;,\quad P\cdot\Phi_{-}^{\eta}\,\in\,2\,M_{n}(\mathbb{Z})

[formula]

When these conditions are fulfilled (and only then),
the deformation of
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
by the “compatible” toral twist

{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}
does “restrict” to a deformation of the polynomial MpQUESA

U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
as well, that we denote by
{U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})}^{({\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi})}\,
.
The new coproduct, counit and antipode in
{U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})}^{({\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi})}

are described by formulas which are
(a)
unchanged for the

K_{i}^{\pm 1}
’s and
L_{i}^{\pm 1}
’s, and
(b)

the very same formulas as in the first part of the proof of
Theorem
5.1.2
for the
E_{i}
’s and

F_{i}
’s — as those formulas now make sense as formulas inside

{U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})}^{\otimes 2}
or
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
. Then the
“polynomial counterpart” of Theorem
5.1.2

holds true, stating in particular that every “twist deformed” object

{U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})}^{({\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi})}
issued by this procedure — out of a given
polynomial MpQUESA
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
, through a toral twist
{\mathcal{F}}_{\Phi}

which is “compatible” in the sense explained above —
is yet another
polynomial
MpQUESA. And similar
statements hold true for Borel subalgebras too.

7.3.2
.

Deformations by toral 2–cocycle for PolMpQUESA’s.

Let again
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
and
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
be a PolMpQUESA and a split,
straight and minimal FoMpQUESA, respectively, the former embedding into
the latter as in Corollary
7.2.3
.

Let
\;\sigma_{\chi}:U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\times U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow{\Bbbk(\hskip-1.7pt(\hbar)\hskip-1.7pt)}\;

be a toral polar–2–cocycle of
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
— cf. §
5.2.7
—
which by construction is defined starting from a
{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}
–bilinear map

\;\chi:\mathfrak{h}\times\mathfrak{h}\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Bbbk[[\hbar]]}\;

that vanishes (both on the left and the right) on the
S_{i}
’s (
\,i\in I\,
).
Let us restrict this
\,\sigma_{\chi}\,
to

\,U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\times U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
:  by construction,

\,\sigma_{\chi}{\Big|}_{U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\times U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})}\,
is uniquely determined by

\,\chi{\Big|}_{U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})\times U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})}\,
. Now,
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})

is nothing but the group algebra over
R_{\mathbf{q}}
of the free Abelian group

\,\varGamma\cong\mathbb{Z}^{2n}\,
(generated by the
K_{i}
’s and the
L_{i}
’s);
therefore
\,\chi{\Big|}_{U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})\times U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})}\,
is uniquely
determined by its restriction
\,\eta(\chi):=\chi{\Big|}_{\varGamma\times\varGamma}\,
.
A straightforward check shows that
\,\eta(\chi)\,

is just a standard group 2–cocycle with values in
R_{\mathbf{q}}\,
, and likewise

\,\chi{\Big|}_{U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})\times U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})}\,
is a
standard

Hopf 2–cocycle for the Hopf algebra
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})\,
,
with values in ground ring
R_{\mathbf{q}}\,
, and the same holds for

\,\sigma_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}_{\chi}}:=\sigma_{\chi}{\Big|}_{U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\times U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})}\,

— in particular, no more “polar” is involved, nor there is any need to
resort to any larger ring than the fixed ground one. The outcome is that
restricting to the PolMpQUESA
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
the deformation process onto the larger FoMpQUESA

U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
via the (toral) polar–2–cocycle
\sigma_{\chi}
yields
the same outcome as deforming
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
by its Hopf 2–cocycle

\sigma_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}_{\chi}}
via the standard procedure in Hopf algebra theory.

By the way, note that (by construction) every such
\,\sigma_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}_{\chi}}\,

vanishes on the subgroup
\varGamma_{0}
of
\varGamma

generated by the products
K_{i}^{+1}L_{i}^{-1}
(
\,i\in I\,
).

Conversely, starting from any standard group 2–cocycle

\,\hat{\sigma}_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\,
on
\varGamma
with values in

R_{\mathbf{q}}

which vanishes on the subgroup
\varGamma_{0}\,
,
we can uniquely extend it to a Hopf 2–cocycle of
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{h})
and then
even to a Hopf 2–cocycle
\sigma_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}
of
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})

— adapting the recipe in §
5.2.7
.
These Hopf 2–cocycles (in a standard sense: no “polar” is involved!)
of
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
will in fact coincide with all those coming from
restriction of toral polar–2–cocycles of
U^{\,{\mathcal{R}}\,,\,p}_{\!P,\hskip 0.7pt\hbar}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
,
hence we will call these
\sigma_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}
“toral” as well.

To finish with, whenever we perform a deformation of
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})

by any toral 2–cocycle
\sigma_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}
we get a genuine
deformation of the polynomial MpQUESA
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\,
, that we denote by

{\big(U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}}\,
: its coalgebra structure
is unchanged, whilst the new product is described by a straightforward (and easy), partial adaptation of formulas given in §
5.2.9
. Then the “polynomial counterpart” of Theorem
5.2.13
holds true, namely there exists an isomorphism

\;{\big(U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})\big)}_{\sigma_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}}\cong U_{\mathbf{q}*\hat{\mathbf{q}}}(\mathfrak{g})\,

which is the identity on generators. In short, every toral 2–cocycle
deformation of a PolMpQUESA is yet another PolMpQUESA, whose new multiparameter

\,\mathbf{q}*\hat{\mathbf{q}}\,
is a suitable combination of the original one

\mathbf{q}
and of the further parameters encoded within
\sigma_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}

— details can be easily deduced again from §
5.2.9
. In particular, one can prove that any PolMpQUESA
U_{\mathbf{q}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{g})
can be obtained from the standard PolMpQUESA
U_{\check{\mathbf{q}}}(\mathfrak{g})
by a
2
–cocycle deformation, by choosing the parameter
\hat{\mathbf{q}}
such that
\,\check{\mathbf{q}}*\hat{\mathbf{q}}=\mathbf{q}\,
.
Similar statements also hold true for the Borel PolMpQUESAs and their
deformations by
\sigma_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\,
.
